### 2026-10-16 · 2.0 Store spine: `nip ingest`, a store-backed Timeline, and run/flag persistence

- **Why:** Phase 1's `nip run` read files and wrote JSON; Phase 2's SQL rules, pgvector index and `GET /runs/{id}/diff` need a store. Ruling R1 ([B-120](decisions.md#b-120--phase-2-plan-rulings-r1-to-r15-adopted)) added this deliverable to W0, ahead of the W1 agents. Commits `f6c3506` (chore(deps): instructor; rapidfuzz was already added by 2.5's `7c20c54` and boto3 was already a dependency; the resolve moved rich 15.0.0 → 14.3.4, instructor's pin), `5989485` (R10 `nip eval compare`, entry below), `870c54b` (store spine, 19 files).
- **What was built:**
  - `src/nip/store/objects.py`: a boto3 S3 client from `NIP_S3_ENDPOINT` with content-addressed keys `{show}/{ep}/source/{sha}.{ext}`, `{show}/show-level/{sha}.{ext}`, `{show}/{ep}/keyframes/{shot}_{n}.jpg`, `runs/{run_id}/...`.
  - `src/nip/store/ingest.py` (`nip ingest`): classify each `[TAG=]PATH` ([B-130](decisions.md#b-130--nip-ingest-takes-tagpath-positionals)), validate every file before any write, hash every file (a sha256 the episode, or for show-level files the show, already has is skipped), then upload and write rows in one transaction. `FORMATS`, `InputRejected` and `ingest()` moved here from `pipeline.py`; the duplicate-scene check moved inside `ingest()`, so file mode and store mode share it. Writes show, episode, source_file, scene + script_line, subtitle track + cues, shot + keyframes (from a cached `picture.json` checked against the probe, or from `analyze_picture`), bible entities + aliases ([B-134](decisions.md#b-134--bible-mapping-to-entity-and-entity_alias)), glossary terms + approved renderings, clearance items.
  - `src/nip/store/load.py`: `load_timeline(conn, show, ep)` returns the same spans as `build_timeline`, then script lines, ASR segments, active facts and clearance items ([B-136](decisions.md#b-136--line-asr-fact-and-clearance-refs-are-built-in-nipstoreload), [B-137](decisions.md#b-137--fact-spans-carry-their-source-quotes-clearance-item-spans-carry-the-item-name)).
  - `src/nip/store/runs.py`: `ext_id` / `db_id` (`run_912` ⇄ 912, the 1.x M14 minor), the R13 `fingerprint`, `code_version` (git SHA), and the plan, run, work_unit and flag + evidence writers.
  - `src/nip/pipeline.py`: `run_store` and `nip run --show S --episodes E… [--agents auto|a,b] [--force] [--out-dir D]`, synchronous and in-process (R8). One `STAGES` list (timeline → checks → verification → flags → report); `EpisodeRun` carries per-episode state; `build_checks` reads each agent package's `CHECKS` and calls `Check.active` ([B-138](decisions.md#b-138--the-checks-construction-convention)); coverage comes from the work-unit rows. The Phase 1 file-mode CLI is renamed `nip run-files` ([B-143](decisions.md#b-143--the-file-mode-cli-is-renamed-nip-run-files-nip-run-is-the-store-command)).
  - Smaller: `localization.CHECKS = [Readability]`; an empty `nip.knowledge` package for W1; `serving._bind` binds `nip.runtime.clients.<slot>.Actor` when that module defines one, else `Placeholder`, and raises if the module exists but fails to import ([B-146](decisions.md#b-146--a-serve-client-module-is-named-after-its-slot-clientstext_embedderpy)); `clients/llm.py` reads `NIP_SERVE_URL` (default `http://127.0.0.1:8000`); `compose.dev.yaml` (R9) publishes Postgres 55432→5432, SeaweedFS 8333 and ray-head Serve 8001→8000 on loopback, `compose*.yaml` is LF in `.gitattributes`; `.env.example` gains `NIP_SERVE_URL`, `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, `KEYCLOAK_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, `NIP_OIDC_ISSUER_BASE` (the 1.5 M4 gaps) and commented host-side values.
  - Tests: `tests/integration/conftest.py` `pg` fixture (a throwaway database `nip_test_<pid>` with `create_all` and the vector/btree_gist extensions, truncated after each test, skipped without `NIP_TEST_DATABASE_URL`, which `NIP_CI` turns into a failure) and a `FakeS3`; `test_store.py` 10 integration tests; `tests/unit/store/test_store_logic.py` 4; +2 pipeline, +1 CLI, +1 serving hook, +1 compare.
- **Commands:** `docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.dev.yaml up -d postgres objectstore objectstore-init`; `uv run nip ingest --show sintel --episode S01E01 <first-minute PDF> en=<en srt> es=<es srt> <Sintel mkv> --picture data/runs/1.10/sintel/picture.json`; `uv run nip run --show sintel --episodes S01E01 --out-dir data/runs/2.0/sintel-run1` (and `run2`). Dev DB `nip` on pgvector 0.8.2-pg18, SeaweedFS 4.47.
- **Measured** (this laptop, `time`; table in [C-21](experiments.md#c-21--store-backed-nip-run-vs-the-110-file-run-on-sintel)): first ingest of 4 files with the 1,172,428,172-byte mkv uploaded **48.4 s** wall; re-ingest of the same 4 files (hash only, `stored: []`, all 4 `unchanged`) **11.7 s**; `nip run` whole CLI process (incl. NVML probe and catalog load) **8.7 s**; stages timeline (load) 0.075 s, checks 0.009 s, verification 0.0009 s, flags 0.083 s, report 0.036 s. Rows after two runs: plan 2, show 1, episode 1, run 2, source_file 4, scene 2, script_line 16, shot 162, subtitle_track 2, subtitle_cue 52, work_unit 4, flag 12, evidence 22. S3: 4 sources (1,554 B, 1,514 B, 1,172,428,172 B, 20,541 B); per run `runs/run_N/S01E01/report.json` (2,556 B) and `runs/run_N/plan.json` (432 B).
- **Done criteria:** `load_timeline == build_timeline` on Sintel (PDF, en+es, mkv probe, cached picture) over **614 spans** (scene 2, shot 162, keyframe 398, subtitle_cue 52 equal the 1.10 file run, plus 16 script lines); the 6 `loc.readability` flags equal the 1.10 file run (commit `77257b6`) by title, evidence ref minus the episode prefix, frames, role, severity and tier (cues 4, 5, 7, 14, 23 gaps; cue 11 at 17.1 CPS), all `evidence: pass` and `rule_recheck: pass`; re-ingest adds no rows; two runs give identical sorted fingerprint lists (6 = 6; the reviewer confirmed this in the dev DB, run 2 has `previous_run_id=1`).
- **Broke and fixed:** (1) `classify` returned nothing for a tagged csv (`glossary=g.csv` fell through to the "give csv as…" rejection); the unit test caught it. (2) The equality test first compared `Timeline` objects with `==`: Pydantic compares private attributes, and `_by_ref` on the `model_copy` still held the original's index, so equal span tuples compared unequal; the test compares `model_dump()` (the frozen contract's list-vs-tuple minor, rr m7). (3) A "replace es subtitles" test reused the en file's bytes: same sha256, so ingest correctly skipped it; the test now uses different bytes. (4) Process: early edits used a bare `python` heredoc, which was the system Python 3.14 (text rewrites only); every test ran in the 3.12 venv.
- **Deferred minors closed here:** 1.10 M3 (`nip run --show/--episodes`); 1.4 M4 (non-timeline evidence: `fact/1` and `clr/1` resolve); 1.4 M3/M13 duplicate scene numbers at ingest (the DB `UNIQUE` belongs to 2.8's migration 0002); M14 contract ids vs bigint; 1.11's compare minors (entry below); 1.5 M4 `.env.example` gaps; 1.8A M3 partly (ingest refuses a cached `picture.json` whose fps or duration differ from the probe, or whose shots end past the probed duration; the decoder count is still not recorded by `analyze_picture`). `nip ingest`, `nip run` and `nip run-files` all print `INPUT_REJECTED: <file>: <reason>` and exit 1. Not fixed (not 2.0's files, proposals in the notes): rr m3 `refs.parse` lang shape, rr m4 module-level importorskip under `NIP_CI`, rr m2 `Check.version` not enforced, rr m7 Timeline list vs tuple.
- **Tests:** integration (`test_store.py`, `test_migrations_pg.py`, 2.5's `test_asr_persist.py`, which already uses `pg`) **12 passed in 12.9 s**; `NIP_PROFILE=cpu pytest -m "not gpu and not integration"` **409 passed, 5 failed, 1 skipped (ray) in 111.8 s**, all 5 failures in other agents' uncommitted work (4 in 2.5's `test_align.py`, `test_inject.py::test_committed_labels_validate` from 2.9A's label changes); ruff and mypy clean on 2.0's files; `lint-imports` 7 kept, 0 broken.
- **Left open in the report:** the script speaker name is lost (`script_line` has no speaker column, [B-145](decisions.md#b-145--script_linespeaker-is-added-by-28-in-migration-0002)); the embedder module name vs slot ([B-146](decisions.md#b-146--a-serve-client-module-is-named-after-its-slot-clientstext_embedderpy)); store tests not in CI (assigned to W-int, [B-150](decisions.md#b-150--the-store-integration-tests-go-into-ci-in-w-int)); the dev Postgres now holds the Sintel ingest and 2 runs, SeaweedFS the 1.17 GB mkv.
- **Source:** `notes/2.0.md`, `reports/2.0-report.md`; commits `f6c3506`, `870c54b`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.0 `nip eval compare` prints every logged metric (R10) and closes 1.11's compare minors

- **What:** ruling R10 ([B-120](decisions.md#b-120--phase-2-plan-rulings-r1-to-r15-adopted)) made 2.0 extend `eval/report.py` `compare_table`, which printed only flag P/R/F1 and fixed rows, so experiment numbers can come from `nip eval compare` alone. It now prints a row for every other logged metric key ([B-149](decisions.md#b-149--nip-eval-compare-prints-every-logged-metric-and-refuses-mixed-groups-r10)).
- **1.11 minors closed** (deferred in [review-findings.md](review-findings.md#111-eval-metrics-and-mlflow-logging)): `--exp` is validated against `[\w.-]+` because it is interpolated into an MLflow filter; `--subset` must be `shown` or `all`; a variant column whose runs repeat a seed or carry different `plan_fingerprint` values is refused instead of silently averaged; `nip eval run` ids are `{set}-s{seed}-{ts}-{variant}-{4 hex}`, so two runs in the same second no longer collide. `--md` is still a no-op flag (harmless).
- **Tests:** +1 compare test in `tests/unit/eval/test_report.py`.
- **Source:** `notes/2.0.md` (What was built, Deferred minors), `reports/2.0-report.md`; commit `5989485`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.0 Review and two fix rounds: the stage contract, artifacts after commit, stable refs, a savepoint per unit

- **Review** (`2.0-review.md`): spec pass, quality "changes needed", **0 Critical, 3 Important, 9 Minor** ([review-findings.md](review-findings.md#20-store-spine)). The reviewer accepted the per-episode report key, refusing replacements until 2.9B, the `run-files` rename and the refs built in `load.py`. The findings were about the conventions the five W1 deliverables were about to build on.
- **I1, the stage contract.** `coverage()` called `owner(stage)` on every unit, so a content-stage unit (`knowledge.extract`, `timeline.asr`) raised `KeyError` and failed the run; `stage_checks` bulk-inserted all `ep.units`, so a stage that wrote its own units would insert them twice; `EpisodeRun.plan` was a `ReportPlan`, so a stage could not see the `Plan` decisions it must route calls by. **Fix** (commit `0a086a1`): `EpisodeRun.plan` is the real `Plan` with its `plan_N` id; `with ep.unit(stage, key, model_variant=...)` records one unit `done` or `failed`; a failure is logged, swallowed, counts as a gap and the stage carries on; units are inserted once per episode after its last stage; coverage counts only units whose stage is a check id ([B-140](decisions.md#b-140--run-lifecycle-and-the-stage-contract-epunit-gaps-coverage-from-check-units)). **Found while testing:** a latent `executemany` bug, where the columns come from the first row, so a `done` row (no `error` key) followed by a `failed` row lost the error text; every row now carries the same keys.
- **I2, artifacts before commit.** Reports were uploaded inside the per-episode loop, before the single commit; a later crash rolled back every flag but left S3 and `--out-dir` holding "completed" reports listing `flg_N` ids that would never exist. **Fix:** `publish()` uploads `runs/{id}/plan.json` and `runs/{id}/{ep}/report.json` and writes `--out-dir` only after `conn.commit()` ([B-141](decisions.md#b-141--artifacts-are-published-only-after-the-commit-the-whole-run-is-one-transaction)); test `test_failed_run_publishes_no_artifacts` (S3 unchanged, `--out-dir` absent, 0 flag rows, run `failed`).
- **I3, fingerprint collapse.** R13 drops `fact/` and `asr/` refs, so a flag citing only facts hashed to `sha256(check + "\n" + lang)` and every such flag of one check, in every episode, shared one fingerprint; 2.3's SQL rules would naturally emit fact-only evidence. **Controller ruling:** every flag must carry at least one stable ref; flags without one are dropped and counted ([B-147](decisions.md#b-147--every-flag-must-carry-at-least-one-stable-evidence-ref)). `runs.fingerprint` raises `ValueError` and `require_stable_refs` drops such flags at verification, in store and file mode.
- **Minors fixed in the same commit:** m1 `add_stage(name, fn, before="timeline")` (the report's own wiring advice, repeated `STAGES.insert(0, ...)`, would have reversed the order); m2 `code_version` gets `-dirty` when tracked files differ (the dev DB runs had recorded the deps commit `1507da1` while running uncommitted 2.0 code); m3 `episode_ids` stored and compared sorted; m4 show slug must match `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*`; m6 a `--picture` for an already-ingested video logs a warning; m7 `_bind` raises `ServingError` when one app's slots resolve to different actor classes. m5 (`analyze_picture` inside the open ingest transaction) deferred with a `ponytail:` note; m8 not changed (`POSTGRE_PASSWORD` is Label Studio's own variable name, not a typo); m9 (2.6's `clients/ocr.py` / `ner.py` hard-code `SERVE_URL`) passed to the controller.
- **Tests after fix round 1:** integration **15 passed in 7.3 s**; CPU suite **470 passed, 5 failed (others' uncommitted work), 1 skipped in 73.9 s**.
- **Controller ruling during the round:** the whole run stays one transaction for Phase 2, and 2.9B must write stage-cache entries in their own transactions so a later-episode crash does not discard paid-for GPU units ([B-141](decisions.md#b-141--artifacts-are-published-only-after-the-commit-the-whole-run-is-one-transaction)); 2.9B-1 did this ([B-122](decisions.md#b-122--cache-objects-are-gzip-json-not-zstd) and the 2.9B-1 [journal](journal.md#2026-11-06--29b-1-stage-cache-incremental-re-runs-and-get-runsiddiff)).
- **Re-review 1** (`2.0-rereview.md`): I1, I2, I3, m1 ADDRESSED; **new Important N1**: `ep.unit()` had no savepoint, so (a) a unit that wrote 3 rows and then raised left them committed with a `completed_with_gaps` run, and (b) a DB error inside a unit put the Postgres transaction into the aborted state, every later unit failed with `InFailedSqlTransaction` (each swallowed as a gap) and the run finally died at `save_flags` with a misleading error. Plus minor m-a: a malformed evidence ref now crashed the run where it used to drop the flag.
- **Fix round 2** (commit `9d0727d`): `ep.unit()` runs its body inside `conn.begin_nested()` (a SAVEPOINT), rolls back to it on an exception and records the gap, releases it on success; cost one SAVEPOINT/RELEASE round trip per unit ([B-148](decisions.md#b-148--each-work-unit-runs-in-a-savepoint-a-malformed-evidence-ref-is-a-counted-drop)). `require_stable_refs` catches the `ValueError` from `refs.parse` and drops the flag with reason `malformed evidence ref: …`. Test `test_failed_unit_rolls_back_its_rows_and_does_not_poison_the_next`: unit 1 updates scene 1's `story_seq` then raises (rolled back, stays NULL), unit 2 runs `SELECT 1/0` (recorded failed with `DivisionByZero`), unit 3 updates scene 2 and commits; run `completed_with_gaps` with units `{failed: 2, done: 2}`, the 2 readability flags kept. **37 passed** (store integration + pipeline/CLI/store unit).
- **Re-review 2** (`2.0-rereview2.md`): N1 and the malformed-ref minor ADDRESSED; `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` still abort the run through `run_store`'s `except BaseException` rollback; residual, not blocking: a raise from RELEASE itself would fail the run instead of counting a gap. The reviewer ran `tests/unit/test_pipeline.py`: 10 passed.
- **Source:** `notes/2.0.md` (Fix round 1, Fix round 2), `reports/2.0-report.md`, `2.0-review.md`, `2.0-rereview.md`, `2.0-rereview2.md`, `progress.md` (2.0 entries); commits `0a086a1`, `9d0727d`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.9A Injectors for localization, clearance text and plot Layer 1, a synthetic show, and `nip eval inject`

- **What:** everything under `eval/`, nothing in `src/`, no new dependency. Commit `2e3cb6e`.
  - `eval/injectors/common.py`: `picture_fps` (ffprobe of the picture next to the subtitle file, never a constant), `Srt` (a text-level SRT editor that keeps cue numbers, line endings and untouched cues and refuses what `nip.ingest.parse_srt` refuses), `SIDECAR`.
  - `loc_offset.py` fixed (1.9 M1/M2 and the cue-numbering minor): id bumped to `loc.offset.v2`, fps probed, label carries `title`, `episode`, `lang`, kind `subtitle_track` and every cue ref (R12); negative times are never produced.
  - New injectors: `loc.term.v1`, `loc.shot_change.v1`, `loc.drift.v1` (ratio relative to the probed rate: 25/24 and 1000/1001 on the 24 fps films), `clr.text.v1`, `plot.knowledge_order.v1`, `plot.dead_acts.v1`, `plot.two_places.v1`, `plot.prop_provenance.v1`; `loc.readability.v1` gained `title=` and `avoid=` (cues earlier injectors already edited). Each seeds `random.Random(f"{injector id}/{seed}")` ([B-278](decisions.md#b-278--each-injector-seeds-its-own-stream-from-injector-id-seed)).
  - `eval/synthetic/{model,generate,rules}.py`: deterministic template generator (R2, [B-269](decisions.md#b-269--the-synthetic-show-is-a-template-generator-with-truth-by-construction-a-script-line-is-a-fountain-element)): 6 episodes x 25 scenes = 150 scenes, 471 lines, 365 facts, 483 mention records for seed 1 (counted from `build(1)`; 704 after the fix round); `rules.py` is a Python reference of the four Layer 1 rules over the truth facts (clean show 0 violations for seeds 1 to 20; injected show exactly the 12 planted).
  - `eval/inject.py`: `nip eval inject --set S --seed N [--out --only loc,clr,plot --corpus --shots]` (wiring line for the integration step, [B-277](decisions.md#b-277--nip-eval-inject-is-wired-as-a-command-not-a-sub-app); wired by W-int in `3d66622`).
  - Sidecars `eval/corpus/sidecar/{sintel,tears-of-steel}/{bible.yaml,glossary.csv,clearance_log.csv}`, all six passing `nip.ingest.parse` with no warnings; `synthetic-show` added to `eval/corpus/manifest.yaml` and the `mvp` set ([B-276](decisions.md#b-276--synthetic-show-in-the-manifest-with-license-cc-by-40-and-no-source)).
- **Measured** (laptop, Windows, ffmpeg 8.1, real corpus, `uv run`, seed 1):
  - `nip eval inject --set mvp --seed 1 --only loc,plot`: **1.7 s** wall. Labels: Sintel 14 loc (term 4, shot_change 2, readability 6, drift 1, offset 1), Tears of Steel 18 loc (term 8, shot_change 2, readability 6, drift 1, offset 1), synthetic show 12 plot (3 per check).
  - `--only clr`: **2 min 36 s** for both films (Sintel 1080p 21,313 frames, ToS 720p 17,620 frames), including the low-motion scan. Outputs: Sintel 784,483,847 bytes (source 1,172,428,172), ToS 285,935,670 bytes (source 372,178,639). Labels: Sintel 4 clr, ToS 6 clr. Items rendered (from the `.clr.json` files): Sintel 12 (8 positives, 4 negatives), ToS 11 (8 positives, 3 negatives; capped by its 11 still stretches). Cleared positives and negatives are not labelled ([B-271](decisions.md#b-271--cleared-clearance-items-are-rendered-but-not-labelled)).
  - **Determinism:** `--only clr` run twice into two directories: sha256 of both re-encoded films identical, labels identical (second run 3 min 02 s, machine busy with tests).
  - **Low-motion scan** ([C-41](experiments.md#c-41--low-motion-threshold-for-clearance-overlay-windows)): Sintel decode at 48x27 gray 21 to 23 s; at threshold 1.0 gray level, 21 still stretches of at least 84 frames (3,343 frames) after dropping the stretch that starts at frame 0; ToS 11 stretches (1,290 frames), 5.8 s.
  - **Native findings:** the readability check flags 6 native cues in Sintel es and 33 in ToS es on the originals; the flags on the injected copies are the originals' plus the planted ones (verified for Sintel es and ToS es/fr). They stay unlabelled except through the native-label route.
- **Broke or surprised:** (1) the Fountain reader merges consecutive action paragraphs into one element (`'One.\n\nTwo.'`), so a stored script "line" is an element; the model gained `merge_actions` and the generator and plot injectors merge adjacent actions so that `{ep}/line/{scene}.{seq}` is exact (a test compares every parsed element with the truth line, [B-269](decisions.md#b-269--the-synthetic-show-is-a-template-generator-with-truth-by-construction-a-script-line-is-a-fountain-element)); (2) `Srt` first accepted a file with no timing lines (0 == 0 cues) because `parse_srt` reports "no cues" as an error with `data` still set; the check now needs `parsed.ok`; (3) EXP-002's `picture.json` stores `cuts`, `nip timeline picture` stores `shots`; `inject.py` reads both; (4) a bash heredoc/escape problem ate backslashes in two test files (raw control characters); rewritten with literal content, no control characters left in `eval/`; (5) the first generator draft hit `IndexError` on `rng.choice([])` placing a secret's 4th event for some seeds; fixed by sampling the later events together, and the clean-show invariant is now checked for seeds 1 to 20; (6) a still-stretch assertion was too strict (a dark plate on a dark background); the test compares copy and source frames (mean abs diff > 5 in the box).
- **Tried → switched:** text drawing with ffmpeg `drawtext` (the build has it) → Pillow-rendered PNGs plus `overlay=enable='between(n,a,b)'` (no font-file dependency, same result on Linux CI; frame accuracy tested: diff in the box above 5 grey levels on the 2nd and second-to-last labelled frame, below 5 three frames outside); low-motion via cached shot ranges plus a motion measure (needs cached shots for every title) → "frame-to-frame difference under 1.0 for at least 84 frames", which a cut cannot straddle; one output copy per injector → stacking in one copy of the track with an `avoid` set (term → shot_change → readability) plus dedicated tracks for drift and offset ([B-274](decisions.md#b-274--track-plan-per-title-stacked-edits-in-one-copy-separate-tracks-for-drift-and-offset)).
- **Tests:** 57 passed in the six 2.9A files (`test_inject.py` 8, `test_inject_loc.py` 20, `test_inject_plot.py` 8, `test_inject_clr.py` 7, `test_inject_cli.py` 5, `test_synthetic.py` 9 counting parametrized cases); whole `tests/unit` before commit 369 passed, 1 deselected, then stopped at the first failure (2.5's `test_align.py`); ruff check, ruff format --check and mypy clean on 2.9A's files; `lint-imports` 7 kept, 0 broken.
- **Concerns in the report:** `test_committed_labels_validate` fails in the shared checkout because 2.5's untracked `eval/labels/exp004/*.jsonl` are not Label format ([B-280](decisions.md#b-280--evallabels-is-reserved-for-label-format-files-exp-004-ground-truth-moves-out)); `loc.shot_change` found 2 usable cues for Sintel and 1 per language for ToS (k = 3 asked), because most ToS cues are back to back and the injector refuses edits that could create a gap/min-duration finding; `loc.readability` still saves through pysubs2 (renumbers 1..n, loses line endings; harmless for the 1..n LF corpus files, and it runs last in the stack); the decoder yields 21,312 frames for Sintel where the probe says 21,313, and the clearance windows use decoder frame numbers.
- **Later consequence (W-int):** 2.4's review found that `loc.shot_change` could plant the now-compliant cut-2 out point; the injector was fixed in W-int (`be494b4`), not in 2.9A.
- **Source:** `notes/2.9A.md`, `reports/2.9A-report.md`; commit `2e3cb6e`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.9A Review and fix round: film episodes, detectable phone positives, complete mention truth

- **Review** (`2.9A-review.md`): spec PASS with deviations (D-A to D-J recorded), quality approved, **0 Critical, 3 Important, 7 Minor**. The reviewer ran a 40-seed synthetic/plot consistency check (clean show 0 violations, injected show exactly the planted ones, each label's first ref on a violation line), a mention-coverage count on `build(1)`, a `phonenumbers` validity check and a Presidio `PhoneRecognizer` leniency check; the implementer's tests were not re-run.
- **I1, film labels could never match.** Film labels used `episode = <title slug>` (inherited from 1.9/1.10), but the 2.0 store accepts only `SxxEyy` episodes and `metrics.overlap` returns 0 whenever `flag.episode != label.episode`, so no film flag would ever match a film label (cue refs read `sintel/es/12` instead of `S01E01/es/12`). **Controller ruling:** a film is `show=<slug>`, `episode="S01E01"` ([B-279](decisions.md#b-279--a-film-is-showslug-episodes01e01)). **Fix** (commit `2894fdd`): `FILM_EPISODE = "S01E01"` is every injector's default; label ids start with the title (`sintel-es-term-s1-7`); inherited 1.10 code fixed too (`loc_readability.py` defaults, `eval/labels/walking-skeleton/sintel.native.jsonl` rewritten to `S01E01`, `test_inject_readability.py`). New test `test_film_labels_use_the_store_episode_and_match_a_flag_from_that_naming`: labels fullmatch the store's `EPISODE_RE`, the injected es track goes through `build_timeline(label.episode, ...)` and the real Readability check, and `eval.metrics.match` pairs every planted readability label with a flag.
- **I1b, phone positives the detector cannot see** ([C-42](experiments.md#c-42--phone-positives-nanp-expansion-area-codes-vs-555-numbers-outside-the-fiction-range)). The doc's recipe (NANP "reserved for expansion" area codes) gave (290) 482-7716 and (390) 731-2208: `is_possible_number` True, `is_valid_number` False, no match at `Leniency.VALID`; Presidio's `PhoneRecognizer` defaults to leniency 1 (VALID) and 2.7's `FictionAwarePhone` does not override it, so each film had a structural false negative. **Controller ruling:** phone positives must be `phonenumbers`-valid and outside the fiction ranges ([B-273](decisions.md#b-273--phone-positives-must-be-valid-numbers-outside-the-fiction-range)). **Fix:** N9X rows dropped; positives `(212) 555-0234` and `(415) 555-2671` (Sintel), `(310) 555-0287` and `(212) 555-0963` (ToS), all `is_valid_number` True and outside 555-0100..0199; test `test_phone_positives_are_valid_numbers_outside_the_fiction_range` also asserts every negative under `NEG_AREAS` is valid and inside the fiction range.
- **I2, mention truth covered characters and one brand only.** On `build(1)`, 189 lines naming a location, 24 a prop and 4 an org had no mention record, nor did 4 "the informant" surfaces inside secret descriptions; EXP-020 scores resolution against these mentions, and a registry-enum extractor would emit them legitimately. **Fix:** `model.find_mentions` scans each line's final text for every entity surface (whole words, longest first), used by the generator and the plot injectors ([B-281](decisions.md#b-281--mention-truth-records-every-entity-surface-in-a-line)). Mention records 483 → **704** (463 character names, 9 descriptions, 1 nickname, 189 locations, 24 props, 14 brand, 4 org); generated scripts unchanged (same random stream) ([C-43](experiments.md#c-43--synthetic-show-mention-truth-template-bookkeeping-vs-scanning-every-surface)). Test `test_every_entity_surface_in_a_line_has_a_mention_record` scans independently on a clean and an injected show.
- **Controller ruling on the red test:** the reviewer judged `test_committed_labels_validate` correctly scoped (`eval/labels/` is Label format by spec); 2.5's EXP-004 alignment ground truth moves out of `eval/labels/` rather than the test being loosened ([B-280](decisions.md#b-280--evallabels-is-reserved-for-label-format-files-exp-004-ground-truth-moves-out)).
- **Verification:** `uv run pytest tests/unit/eval` **111 passed, 1 deselected** (`test_committed_labels_validate`, red only because of 2.5's `eval/labels/exp004`; the walking-skeleton labels now pass it); the reviewer re-ran the same command with the same result. The implementer ran `ruff format eval` once over the whole `eval/` tree, which also reformatted 2.5's untracked `eval/exp/*` files (disclosed).

### 2026-10-16 · 2.2 Story order and story days: rules, one LLM unit per ambiguous transition, bible overrides

- **What:** `src/nip/knowledge/story.py` and the transition prompt `config/prompts/knowledge.story.v1.md`, commit `6ad9a53`. `derive(scenes, overrides=(), llm=None, *, unit=, memo=) -> Story` is pure over `SceneIn(ref, heading, elements)` (built from a parsed `Script` via `scenes_from_script`, or from `script_line` rows via `load_scenes`). `llm=None` is EXP-007's rules-only arm; any object with `complete(prompt, schema)` (`StubLLM`, `LLMClient`, a recorded stub) is the rules + LLM arm. `Story` also returns `ambiguous` (transitions the rules could not settle) and `llm_calls`, and every `Position` carries `source` = rule | llm | override, so one return value scores both arms ([B-185](decisions.md#b-185--a-pure-derive-with-the-marker-lexicon-as-module-constants-the-llm-only-for-ambiguous-transitions)).
- **Rules** (module docstring): a day counter (day 1 = first scene of the show, continuing across episodes); NEXT MORNING / NEXT DAY / "N DAYS|WEEKS LATER" advance it; CONTINUOUS / LATER / MOMENTS LATER / THAT NIGHT keep it; a time of day that does not go backwards keeps it. Markers: FLASHBACK and "N YEARS EARLIER" (ended by PRESENT DAY / BACK TO PRESENT / END FLASHBACK), INTERCUT / SAME TIME / MEANWHILE, DREAM / VISION. Cues are read from the heading, the previous scene's transitions and closing all-caps slug, and a scene's opening all-caps super.
- **Order:** `story_seq` = rank of units (a scene plus the scenes sharing its seq) by (story_day, screen order), unknown-day flashbacks first ([B-186](decisions.md#b-186--flashbacks-with-an-unknown-day-rank-first-in-the-show-and-are-inferred)), dense from 1 across the show, episodes by `episode_seq`. Flashback spans: [B-187](decisions.md#b-187--flashback-spans-a-heading-cue-marks-one-scene-a-lead-in-cue-marks-until-the-present-returns).
- **Ambiguous transition** = a time of day that goes back between present scenes with no cue (NIGHT then DAY). Default: next day, `story_inferred=true`. With an LLM: one `Verdict` (relation `next_day | same_day | later_days | earlier | simultaneous`, `days`, `reason`) per transition; prompt = previous present scene + this scene + next heading; temperature 0 via `LLMClient.complete`. A failed call leaves the default, and `ep.unit` records the unit `failed` (a coverage gap).
- **Stage `knowledge.story`** (`stage_story`): loads the whole show's scenes and the latest ingested bible's `story_overrides` from S3 (`s3.get_object`), derives, and overwrites `markers / story_day / story_seq / story_inferred` with one Core `executemany`. The LLM is used only when the Plan has a `text_llm` decision ([B-044](decisions.md#b-044--the-laptop-cannot-plan-an-llm-at-613-gb-free-exp-001-runs-before-the-walking-skeleton): the planner may drop it). Units: `ep.unit("knowledge.story", <scene ref>, model_variant=<variant>)`. Every episode's stage re-derives the whole show ([B-189](decisions.md#b-189--the-stage-re-derives-the-whole-show-for-every-episode-verdicts-memoised-per-variant)).
- **Helpers:** `story_confidence(conf, inferred)` applies `NIP_INFERRED_STORY_CONFIDENCE_FACTOR` (0.85) for 2.3 and later checks; `kendall_tau(truth, got)` (scipy tau-b, ties = shared seq) for EXP-007.
- **Measured** (synthetic show from 2.9A: 6 episodes, 150 scenes, 2 flashbacks, same-time scenes; script → `parse_fountain` → `derive`; truth from `truth.json`; details in [C-27](experiments.md#c-27--story-order-on-the-base-synthetic-show-rules-only-vs-rules--bible-and-stress-variants)): rules + bible overrides tau **1.0000** on seeds 1, 2, 3 (150/150 days on seed 1, 0 inferred); rules only **0.9998** (148/150 days on seed 1, 2 inferred: the two flashbacks). Rules only misses one pair of 11,175: without the bible's day the two flashbacks keep screen order, and the generator puts the later one earlier in story time. Through Postgres (6 episodes and the bible ingested, `nip run` of one episode with the stage): all 150 `story_seq` and `story_day` rows equal `truth.json`, tau 1.0.
- **Stress variants of seed 1** (ad hoc, not committed): replacing "NEXT MORNING" with "MORNING" gives 14 ambiguous transitions and the next-day default still gets 150/150; removing every time of day keeps the order right (tau 1.0) but only 8/150 days. So the synthetic show has no ambiguous transition the default gets wrong, and an LLM cannot raise accuracy on it. This became the controller ruling for 2.2B ([B-191](decisions.md#b-191--an-ambiguous-transitions-synthetic-variant-so-exp-007-can-discriminate)).
- **What broke on the way:**
  1. First synthetic run: **141 of 150 days wrong** from scene 8 on. The generator writes `NEXT MORNING` on every scene of a morning, and the first rule advanced a day on each. Fix: two NEXT-cue scenes in a row with the same time of day are one morning (`after_next`); 150/150 afterwards.
  2. Supers and slugs were never seen: the Fountain parser merges consecutive action paragraphs (`"THREE YEARS EARLIER\n\nMaya laughs.\n\nINTERCUT WITH:"` is one element). Fix: `_paras` splits action elements on blank lines; a test with that exact shape covers it.
  3. A dream or intercut scene shares the seq and day of the last non-sharing scene even when that scene is a flashback; the first test expected the present. By design; the test was wrong.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/knowledge/test_story.py` 30 passed (cpu profile); `tests/integration/test_story_pg.py` 4 passed (whole-show columns from a one-episode run, work units, a failed LLM call as a gap, idempotent re-runs). ruff clean; `mypy` clean on `story.py` (the two `mypy src` errors were in 2.5's then-uncommitted `timeline/align.py`); `lint-imports` 7 kept. The integration tests carry their own `ReadableS3`, because 2.0's `FakeS3` in `tests/integration/conftest.py` has no `get_object` and the conftest was not 2.2's file.
- **Not done in W1:** no `config/pipeline.yaml` edits (lexicon as module constants; keys proposed, [B-185](decisions.md#b-185--a-pure-derive-with-the-marker-lexicon-as-module-constants-the-llm-only-for-ambiguous-transitions)); EXP-007 left to W2.
- **Source:** `.superpowers/sdd/phased-plan/notes/2.2.md`, `reports/2.2-report.md`; commit `6ad9a53`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.2 Review and fix round: markers in any heading segment, overrides always win

- **Review** (`2.2-review.md`): spec **partial** (3 gaps in "overrides always win" and marker coverage), quality good with defects; 0 Critical, 3 Important, 7 Minor. The reviewer probed `derive` on small inputs (no files written) rather than re-running the tests ([review-findings](review-findings.md#22-story-order-and-story-days)).
  - **I1:** cues were read only after the heading's last " - ": `INT. HOUSE - FLASHBACK - NIGHT` and `FLASHBACK - INT. B - DAY` became present scenes, and the next scene then looked like a NIGHT → DAY regression (a spurious ambiguous next day).
  - **I2:** an LLM verdict could overwrite an override: with an override of `markers=[continuous]` on a NIGHT → DAY scene and the LLM answering `earlier`, the result was markers `(continuous, flashback)`, day -2, `source=llm`.
  - **I3:** an override `story_day` on a dream / vision / intercut scene was ignored for ordering: day 9 with seq 1 next to a day-1 scene with seq 2.
- **Fixed in `ad1b1b9`** ([B-188](decisions.md#b-188--bible-overrides-always-win-including-over-the-llm), [B-190](decisions.md#b-190--markers-stored-in-the-data-model-set-cues-read-from-every-heading-segment-except-the-location)): `_tail` reads every " - " segment except the location (the segment starting INT./EXT., else the first) plus every parenthetical, so `DREAM HOUSE` and `FLASHBACK DINER` stay out; a markers-only override lets the LLM fill only the unset day (`next_day`, `same_day`, `later_days`, source stays `override`) and ignores `earlier` / `simultaneous`; an override day on a sharing scene makes it its own rank unit (marker kept, day counter not moved). Minors done in the same commit: **M1** an override flashback with no day is `inferred=true`; **M2** `Verdict.reason` lost its `max_length=300` (with `max_tokens=300` a longer reason failed validation); **M3** the verdict memo is keyed per model variant (`_VERDICTS[variant][prompt]`).
- **Counts:** 43 passed (39 unit, 4 integration); synthetic tau unchanged (1.0000 with overrides, 0.9998 rules only); ruff and mypy clean on `story.py`.
- **Re-review** (`2.2-rereview.md`, probes re-run, `test_story.py` 39 passed): I1, I2, I3 and M1 ADDRESSED; no new Critical or Important. New minor: an unoverridden dream/intercut scene right after an override-day sharing scene now shares that scene's day and seq (day 9), not the previous present scene's; rare, not worth a round.
- **Source:** `reports/2.2-review.md`, `reports/2.2-report.md` (Fix round 1), `reports/2.2-rereview.md`; `progress.md` lines 158-162.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.3 Script Critic Layer 1: four SQL rules and an oracle-facts ceiling

- **What:** commit `35a33ca`. `config/rules/plot.{knowledge_order,dead_acts,two_places,prop_provenance}.sql`, one parameterized file per rule (`:show` slug, `:episode` ref), story order, active facts only; each row is one violating script line with fixed columns (claim fact/line/quote, other fact/scene, subject/object names, `inferred`, confidence). `src/nip/agents/script_critic/{__init__,layer1}.py`: `CHECKS = [KnowledgeOrder, DeadActs, TwoPlaces, PropProvenance]`, all `SqlRule(Check)` with `requires={SCRIPT}`, constructed with `conn` and `show` (file mode: `units()` is empty) ([B-199](decisions.md#b-199--one-parameterized-sql-file-per-rule-claim-on-the-script-line-one-flag-per-claim-line)).
- **Evidence:** claim = the script line `{ep}/line/{scene}.{seq}` with the event quote (kept only when it is literally in the line text); the fact that makes it a violation is `fact/{id}` evidence (role setup or contradiction) with its quote. Every flag therefore has a stable ref (R13). The re-check runs the same SQL again and passes only while the claimed line is still a row ([B-204](decisions.md#b-204--the-re-check-re-runs-the-rules-sql-and-compares-only-the-claim-line)).
- **Oracle benchmark:** `eval/exp/layer1_oracle.py` loads an injected synthetic show into a throwaway Postgres database, writes `truth.json` facts as consolidated `fact` rows, runs the checks with `require_stable_refs` + `verify`, and writes flags JSONL ([B-205](decisions.md#b-205--the-oracle-facts-loader-lives-in-evalexplayer1_oraclepy-tagged-plot-l1)).
- **Measured** (2.9A plot-L1 injected show, `nip eval inject --set mvp --seed N --only plot`, 12 labels per seed = 3 per check; `nip eval run --set plot-l1 --exp PLOT-L1 --variant oracle-facts --seed N`; table from `nip eval compare --exp PLOT-L1 --md --subset all`): **P = R = F1 = 1.00** for every check and overall on 3 seeds, tp 12 / fp 0 / fn 0 per seed; the clean show raises 0 flags on seeds 1-3; rule wall time 0.03-0.11 s per check over all 6 episodes of a seed ([C-29](experiments.md#c-29--layer-1-rules-on-oracle-facts-prf1-100-a-rules-only-ceiling)). These are the oracle facts of a template generator, so the number measures the rules, not extraction: it is the ceiling for EXP-007 and EXP-020. The MVP targets (0.80 recall / 0.85 precision) are about extracted facts and were not measured here.
- **What broke / changed on the way:**
  1. **A prop use could not be its own fact.** The first design stored a use as a separate `holds` fact next to the obtain fact. The schema's EXCLUDE constraint (same subject, predicate, object, overlapping story) rejects that, and 2.1's consolidation merges all of a character's `holds` events of one prop into one interval starting at the first event, so a use before the pickup would be absorbed. The rule now reads the source events (`fact_event`) of active facts and works on merged facts ([B-201](decisions.md#b-201--the-fact-contract-the-rules-assume-and-what-21-must-emit)). Test: `test_prop_use_merged_into_the_holders_fact_is_still_found`.
  2. **The confidence factor would have been applied twice.** 2.1's `consolidate.py` already multiplies fact confidence by `NIP_INFERRED_STORY_CONFIDENCE_FACTOR` for inferred scenes, and the 2.3 brief says to apply it here too. The rules therefore read raw `fact_event.confidence` (min over the cited events) and the loader applies the factor once when any cited scene is `story_inferred` ([B-200](decisions.md#b-200--severities-4--3--3--2-the-inferred-story-factor-applied-once-on-raw-event-confidence)).
- **Tests** (compose Postgres, `NIP_TEST_DATABASE_URL`): `tests/integration/test_layer1.py` 36 (per rule positive and negative fixtures: reference before learning / never learned / same story moment / earlier / story vs screen order; dead acts via `present_at` and via a spoken line, exempt flashback, dream and vision scenes; two places at one `story_seq`, cross-episode, intercut, marked scenes; prop used before obtained, never obtained, after destroyed, obtained at the same moment, picked up again; confidence x factor; retracting each fact a flag stands on makes the re-check drop it, 7 cases; `verify` passes; a `run_store` end-to-end run persists the flag with its stable line ref and equal fingerprints across two runs); `tests/integration/test_layer1_oracle.py` 2 seeds (clean show 0 flags, injected show exactly the 12 planted flags); `tests/unit/agents/test_layer1.py` 7. **45 passed** with the database, 8 passed + 35 skipped without it. ruff and mypy clean on its files; `lint-imports` 7 kept, 0 broken.
- **Concerns at hand-off** (report, DONE_WITH_CONCERNS): the fact contract with 2.1 (2.1's `consolidate.py` was uncommitted when 2.3 looked; the rules were not run against its output); `speaker_entity_id` NULL until 2.1/W-int fills it (the reviewer later found 2.1's `extract.py` already fills it).
- **Source:** `notes/2.3.md`, `reports/2.3-report.md`; commit `35a33ca`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.3 Review and fix round: "lost" never fired on 2.1's facts, and the earliest loss masked the latest

- **Review** (`2.3-review.md`): spec pass with one integration gap, quality approved with fixes; 0 Critical, 2 Important, 7 Minor. The reviewer read 2.1's `consolidate.py` / `extract.py` / prompt to check the fact contract and did not re-run tests (no DB URL documented for review) ([review-findings](review-findings.md#23-script-critic-layer-1)).
  - **I-1:** "used after lost" could never fire on real 2.1 output. In 2.1, `lost` was in `END` (not `TERMINAL`), so a `state: lost` event started no fact, and `holds` events with value `lose`/`drop` started none either; they closed the holder's interval but sat in no `source_event_ids`, so the rule could not see them. Only `destroyed` survived. The oracle benchmark hid this because it loads facts with 2.3's own consolidation stand-in.
  - **I-2:** `after_gone` took the *earliest* loss before the use, so obtain 1 → lost 2 → found 3 → lost 4 → use 5 was not flagged (the pickup at 3 masked the loss at 4). The generator plants one `destroyed` per prop, so the benchmark never exercised it.
- **Controller ruling** on I-1 ([B-206](decisions.md#b-206--lost-is-handled-like-destroyed-21-builds-state-lost-facts)): 2.1 builds `state: lost` facts from lose/lost/drop events; 2.3 handles `lost` exactly like `destroyed`.
- **Fixed in `3aa1594`:** `ORDER BY d.story_seq DESC, d.fact_id` (the latest loss before the use), then no pickup between that loss and the use; `lost` documented in the SQL header; oracle loader `--gone-as lost` (variant `oracle-facts-lost`, seed 2 of the oracle test writes every destroyed prop as `lost`); **M-4** the no-op `retract(use + 0)` test replaced by an asserted loss-without-pickup case; **M-5** the old MLflow runs deleted and all 3 seeds re-scored for both variants with the committed SQL; **M-6** `consolidate()` in `layer1_oracle.py` documented as a stand-in for 2.1's.
- **Numbers after the fix:** P = R = F1 = 1.00 for all four checks and overall, for both `oracle-facts` and `oracle-facts-lost`, 3 seeds. Still a rules-only ceiling on 2.3's own consolidation; the 2.1 loss mapping was not verified end to end here.
- **Tests:** 49 passed against the compose Postgres (`NIP_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://nip:change_me@127.0.0.1:55432/nip`); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Re-review** (`2.3-rereview.md`, re-ran the 49 tests): I-2 and both halves of I-1 ADDRESSED; 2.1's working-tree `consolidate.py:142-143` now emits `state: lost` for lose/lost/drop (not run end to end). The reviewer checked that `DESC` cannot lose an earlier loss's flag: a pickup between the latest loss and the use also covers any earlier loss. No new Critical or Important; one comment line over 100 columns.
- **Source:** `reports/2.3-review.md`, `reports/2.3-report.md` (Fix round 1), `reports/2.3-rereview.md`, `notes/2.3.md` (Fix round 1); `progress.md` lines 167-172.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.8 Scene vector index, hybrid retrieval and migration 0002

- **What:** the text embedder, the scene index, hybrid retrieval and schema migration 0002. Commits `300b09b` (chore(deps): sentence-transformers) and `7a664c1` (feature). No experiment was assigned (brief: "No EXP"); the numbers below are build-time measurements ([C-39](experiments.md#c-39--scene-retrieval-vector-only-vs-hybrid-vector--entity-overlap), [C-40](experiments.md#c-40--scene-embedding-throughput-cpu-vs-gpu-qwen3-embedding-06b)).
- **Dependency:** `sentence-transformers` 6.1.0, which pulled scikit-learn 1.9.1, joblib, narwhals and threadpoolctl; torch stayed at 2.13.0+cu130 (no resolver change). The Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B download took 30 s and is 1,207,489,041 bytes (12 files in `models/hf`).
- **Embedder** (`src/nip/runtime/clients/text_embedder.py`, named after the Slot by controller ruling, [B-261](decisions.md#b-261--the-embedder-client-module-is-clientstext_embedderpy)): `SentenceTransformerEmbedder` for `qwen3-embedding-0.6b` and `-8b` (`truncate_dim=1024`, normalised, left padding, `max_seq_length` 1024); `HashEmbedder` stub (a signed bag of md5-hashed words, 1,024-d, unit length, deterministic, texts that share words are close); `make_embedder(variant, catalog, device="cpu")`; the Serve `Actor` (cuda), which 2.0's generic `_bind` finds by module name.
- **Index** (`src/nip/knowledge/index.py`): `index_scenes(conn, embedder, episode_ids)` embeds a scene when its stored chunk text differs from its current text (once per text and variant, an upsert on the new unique key, [B-263](decisions.md#b-263--unique-scene_id-kind-embedder_variant-on-scene_chunk-the-writer-is-an-upsert)); `stage(ep)` is one `knowledge.index` work unit per episode and a no-op when the Plan has no `text_embedder` decision ([B-268](decisions.md#b-268--the-index-stage-is-a-no-op-without-a-text_embedder-decision-never-a-silent-stub)).
- **Retrieval** (`src/nip/knowledge/retrieval.py`): `retrieve(conn, scene_id, k, variant=, filters=, weight=)`, `score = cosine + weight * overlap`, overlap = the IDF-weighted share of the query scene's entities that the candidate shares; an entity is in a scene when an active fact sourced from that scene's fact events has it as subject or object; IDF = `ln(scenes / scenes containing the entity)` (a character in every scene weighs 0, a prop a lot). Candidate pool = HNSW top max(10k, 50) union every scene sharing an entity, then re-ranked ([B-265](decisions.md#b-265--hybrid-score--cosine--weight-x-idf-weighted-entity-overlap-weight-10)).
- **Migration 0002** (`0002_scene_index.py` + `schema.py`): `script_line.speaker text` (controller ruling, [B-262](decisions.md#b-262--script_linespeaker-text-in-migration-0002-holds-the-raw-character-cue)); `UNIQUE (scene_id, kind, embedder_variant)` on `scene_chunk`; one HNSW partial expression index per catalog embedder variant (`(embedding::vector(1024)) vector_cosine_ops WHERE embedder_variant = '<v>'`, 0.6b and 8b, [B-264](decisions.md#b-264--one-hnsw-partial-expression-index-per-catalog-embedder-variant-with-the-variant-inlined)). 2.0's ingest writer `_write_script` now fills `speaker` (the one allowed edit). `schema.py` also carried another agent's formatting-only `ruff format` change left in the working tree since 1.x; 2.0's report said 2.8 owns the file, so it went into this commit.
- **Drift test:** `test_migrations_pg` stayed green with no change to the catalog-diff logic ([B-005](decisions.md#b-005--migration-0001-is-frozen-raw-ddl-schemapy-is-core-drift-is-a-catalog-diff)): it compares columns, constraints and indexes, so the new column, constraint and indexes are covered. Two assertions were added (the migrated DB has `script_line.speaker` and exactly 2 partial HNSW indexes). `alembic downgrade base` runs 0002's downgrade first.
- **Retrieval quality** (synthetic show seed 1: 6 episodes, 150 scenes, 3 planted setup/payoff pairs; `NIP_INDEX_REAL=cpu pytest -s tests/integration/test_index_pg.py -k "real or lift"`): with the real qwen3-0.6b, the setup scene was in the top 5 for **1/3** payoff queries vector-only and **3/3** hybrid at weight 1.0 (ranks 6/2/22 → 2/1/3). Full sweep in [C-39](experiments.md#c-39--scene-retrieval-vector-only-vs-hybrid-vector--entity-overlap). The default weight was chosen from the same 3 pairs (small sample, agent's own caveat).
- **Throughput** (150 synthetic scenes = 7,759 tokens; best of 3 runs after warm-up; model load 14.9 to 21.3 s): CPU fp32 5.8 to 6.0 scenes/s (298 to 311 tok/s, measured while other agents used the CPU); RTX 4060 fp16 under `data/gpu.lock` 242.5 to 280.8 scenes/s (12,544 to 14,524 tok/s); peak GPU memory 1.42 GiB (catalog says 1.6). End to end `index_scenes` for the 150 scenes on CPU: 25.1 s. CPU kept as the in-process default ([B-266](decisions.md#b-266--cpu-is-the-in-process-embedding-default-the-serve-actor-uses-cuda), [C-40](experiments.md#c-40--scene-embedding-throughput-cpu-vs-gpu-qwen3-embedding-06b)).
- **Things that broke:**
  1. sentence-transformers 6.1 removed `tokenizer_kwargs` (now `processor_kwargs`) and transformers 5 renamed `torch_dtype` to `dtype`; mypy caught the first, both fixed.
  2. `hnsw.ef_search` is capped at 1000; a k=150 query asked for 1,500, so the value is clamped.
  3. The partial HNSW index is only used for a query whose predicate the planner can prove, so the variant is inlined into the SQL (validated `[\w.-]+`) instead of bound. At 150 rows the planner seq-scans anyway; `test_hnsw_partial_index_serves_the_variants_query` proves the index serves `embedding::vector(1024) <=> q` with `enable_seqscan` off.
  4. Every vector query sets `hnsw.iterative_scan = strict_order` (pgvector 0.8), so the show filter applied after the index scan cannot starve the result.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/knowledge/test_index.py` 3 passed; `tests/integration/test_index_pg.py` 7 passed, 1 skipped (real model, needs `NIP_INDEX_REAL=cpu|cuda`); `test_migrations_pg` + store tests 10 passed; CPU suite 668 passed, 3 failed, 1 skipped (the 3 in `tests/unit/test_pipeline.py`, attributed by the implementer to 2.4's uncommitted `localization` changes; the reviewer did not re-run them). ruff and mypy clean on the files; `lint-imports` 7 kept.
- **Review:** spec PASS, quality GOOD, 0 Critical, 0 Important, 9 Minor, no fix round ([review-findings](review-findings.md#28-scene-vector-index-and-hybrid-retrieval)). The reviewer verified on a scratch DB (6,000 random 1,024-d chunks, ANALYZE, the exact `retrieve` query) that the planner picks `Index Scan using ix_scene_chunk_hnsw_qwen3_embedding_0_6b`, and that without the inlined variant it would not.
- **Source:** `.superpowers/sdd/phased-plan/notes/2.8.md`, `reports/2.8-report.md`, `reports/2.8-review.md`, `progress.md` (2.8 entries); commits `300b09b`, `7a664c1`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.5 faster-whisper on the Windows host: a PyAV break and a system cuBLAS

- `uv add faster-whisper rapidfuzz` gave faster-whisper 1.2.1, ctranslate2 4.8.3, av 19.0.1, rapidfuzz 3.14.6 (commit `7c20c54`, pyproject + uv.lock only).
- **CUDA libraries:** the ctranslate2 wheel ships `cudnn64_9.dll`; `cublas64_12.dll` was found on PATH from the system CUDA Toolkit 12.4, not from the torch cu130 wheels (those carry `cublas64_13.dll`). So the host depends on that system install. `add_cuda_dll_dirs()` adds `site-packages/nvidia/*/bin` if the `nvidia-cublas-cu12` / `nvidia-cudnn-cu9` wheels are ever installed (not added here, about 0.5 GB). ASR was not run in the nip image by 2.5.
- **Broke:** `WhisperModel.transcribe("x.wav")` raised `TypeError: open() got an unexpected keyword argument 'metadata_errors'`: faster-whisper 1.2.1's `decode_audio` passes an argument PyAV 19 no longer accepts. Options: pin `av<17` (touches the shared lock) or bypass the decoder. **Chose to bypass** ([B-219](decisions.md#b-219--faster-whispers-own-audio-decoder-is-bypassed-nip-reads-a-16-khz-wav)): ffmpeg already writes a 16 kHz mono PCM16 wav, and `read_wav()` (stdlib `wave` + numpy) hands faster-whisper a float32 array.
- **Weights** (into `models/hf`): `Systran/faster-whisper-large-v3` model.bin 3,087,284,237 bytes (2.9 GiB dir); `mobiuslabsgmbh/faster-whisper-large-v3-turbo` model.bin 1,617,884,929 bytes (1.6 GiB dir), the repo faster-whisper's own `turbo` alias resolves to ([B-037](decisions.md#b-037--catalog-numbers-that-the-docs-do-not-give)). Both are fp16 CT2 repos; int8 is applied on load.
- **Built:** `src/nip/runtime/clients/asr.py` (`FasterWhisperASR`, `StubASR`, `make_asr`, Serve `Actor`), `src/nip/timeline/asr.py` (ffmpeg audio extract, frame placement at the probed fps, `asr` CLI), `src/nip/timeline/align.py` (two matchers, scene placement, persistence, `align` CLI), `config/pipeline.yaml` `timeline.asr` / `timeline.align` (the controller had ruled that `config/pipeline.yaml` stays 2.5's file until 2.5 completes). Commit `bd74e53`.
- **Source:** `notes/2.5.md` (journal "faster-whisper on the Windows host"); commits `7c20c54`, `bd74e53`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.5 EXP-003 round 1: VAD mis-times lines, no-VAD hallucinates over music

- **VAD on** (`vad_filter=True`, the usual advice for film audio): Sintel WER vs the official SRT 0.45 for both models, 48 of 110 reference words deleted. Worse than the deletions: "I have failed." (SRT 7:25.85, audio energy rise at 445.6 s) came back at **211.75 s**; segment times were wrong by up to 234 s, which is fatal for frame placement. Short exclamations ("Scales!" x6, "Yeah! Come on!") and the first two cues were dropped.
- **VAD off:** timings right ("I have failed." at 445.0 s), but Whisper hallucinates over music: Sintel 199 inserted words (turbo) and large-v3 WER 1.95 on Sintel, although SRT-covered words are mostly right.
- **VAD threshold 0.3** (`vad03`): WER improves (turbo 0.356) but the timing problem stays (EXP-004 token alignment 40 % within +-12 frames vs 68 % without VAD).
- **`hallucination_silence_threshold=2.0`** (`novad-hst`): insertions 248 → 218 (turbo), alignment unchanged.
- **Round-1 replacement:** VAD off + drop segments whose mean word probability is under 0.5 (`min_prob`), `temperature=0.0`, `condition_on_previous_text=False`. Insertions 248 → 200 (turbo) and 336 → 293 (large-v3). Round-1 headline: turbo WER **0.574 vs 0.815**, sub+del 0.124 vs 0.155, RTF 0.0139 vs 0.0339 (2.44x), peak VRAM 1.29 vs 2.32 GiB. The agent wrote that hallucinated segments in general sit at p 0.05-0.56 and that large-v3 "hallucinates more over music". **Both claims were wrong** (next entries).
- **Source:** `notes/2.5.md` (journal "EXP-003 first attempt"), `reports/2.5-report.md`; full tables in [EXP-003](experiments.md#exp-003--speech-to-text-whisper-large-v3-vs-large-v3-turbo).

### 2026-10-16 · 2.5 Alignment: Whisper's first word starts early, and two bugs the unit tests found

- **Early first word:** per-line start offsets (Whisper first-matched-word `f_in` minus official cue start, Sintel, turbo no-VAD) were -9 to -50 frames, median -9. The first word of a segment after silence or music is stretched ("This" 106.17-107.17 s, "I" 445.08-445.79 s) while later words last 0.1-0.5 s; its end is the steady edge.
- **Is the SRT a fair reference?** An agent spot-check (not a human label) of audio energy (16 kHz RMS in 100 ms steps) on 4 Sintel lines: onsets about 106.9 s ("This blade", SRT 107.25, Whisper 106.2), 137.6 s (SRT 138.0, Whisper 137.7), 445.6 s (SRT 445.85, Whisper 445.1), about 125.1 s (SRT 125.25, Whisper 124.5). The official cue starts sit 0.15-0.35 s after the audible onset, Whisper's first word 0.1-0.7 s before it.
- **Fix** (`first_word_cap_s: 0.3`): a line's start is `max(first_word.f_in, first_word.f_out - 0.3 s)`. EXP-004 token variant 46 % → 68 % within tolerance ([B-223](decisions.md#b-223--token-level-alignment-is-the-default-with-a-03-s-first-word-cap)).
- **Bug 1, rapidfuzz window on a stray word:** after a noisy line ("tom" for "thom") the best `partial_ratio_alignment` window began on a leftover word, so the next line started 82 frames early. Fix: trim leading/trailing window words that are not tokens of the line. Line variant 43.8 % → 51.4 % within +-12 frames.
- **Bug 2, false `not_in_cut`:** scenes whose few dialogue lines the ASR missed were `not_in_cut` (Sintel 2 of 10 scenes, "Yeah! Come on!" and "Scales!"), which removes them from screen-order checks. Now `not_in_cut` needs at least 8 dialogue words and no anchored line ([B-224](decisions.md#b-224--not_in_cut-needs-at-least-8-unplaced-dialogue-words)). Sintel 10/10 and ToS 12/12 scenes in cut afterwards.
- **Source:** `notes/2.5.md` (journal "Whisper's first word starts early", "Alignment bugs found by the unit tests").

### 2026-10-16 · 2.5 Derived scripts, EXP-004 and the real runs of the aligner

- **Derived scripts (R5):** `eval.exp.exp004_scripts derive` writes one Fountain dialogue element per official-en-SRT cue (speaker `UNKNOWN`) and a synthetic `INT. SEQUENCE n` scene per silence of at least 10 s: Sintel 26 lines / 10 scenes, ToS 76 lines / 12 scenes (`eval/corpus/derived/<title>/script.fountain`, parsed back through `parse_fountain` with each element asserted equal to its cue). This also gives ToS an ingestable script. Labels: start = cue start frame (24/1 from the probe), `labeller: agent`, `basis: srt-cue-start`. Scene breaks are synthetic, so scene snapping is exercised but not scored.
- **Sintel's real first-minute script** (PDF) has 9 dialogue lines worded differently from the film; `exp004_scripts real` pairs each with its best cue (`token_set_ratio` >= 60): 3 lines labelled (cues 7, 8, 10), 6 without a counterpart. "You're lucky to be alive..." (cue 4's beat) scored 56.6 against the wrong cue 7 and was not hand-paired. Labels live in `eval/exp/exp004_labels/` ([B-225](decisions.md#b-225--exp-004-ground-truth-derived-as-broadcast-scripts-labels-outside-evallabels), controller ruling). Found while parsing: `parse_pdf` returns U+FFFD for the PDF's apostrophes ("You�re", "SHAMAN�S HUT"); not 2.5's file.
- **EXP-004 result:** token-level 0.68 vs line-level 0.51 within +-12 frames on 105 labels with the cap; bar 95 % **not met** ([EXP-004](experiments.md#exp-004--script-to-transcript-alignment-token-level-monotonic-vs-rapidfuzz-line-level)).
- **Real runs** (`align_command`): Sintel (picture.json from 1.10, 24/1) 24/26 lines placed, 10/10 scenes in cut; ToS (shots from EXP-002's TransNetV2 cuts, 140 shots over 17,620 frames) 62/76 lines, 12/12 scenes. `timeline.source == "picture"`; scene spans carry SMPTE (Sintel sc/1 `00:01:41:05` to `00:03:26:00`). Known failure kept: Sintel's last two lines ("Scales?" / "Scales...") land on the earlier "Scales!" near frame 13405 because the ASR heard none of the final calls; a one-token match of a repeated word is the weak spot of both variants.
- **GPU test on real audio** (`tests/integration/test_asr_gpu.py`, 23 s of Sintel, turbo): the "You're a fool..." segment starts within 1 s of the official cue; passes under the lock in 6 s (commit `abadd4c`). The first version cut the clip with `-c copy` and the audio start drifted 2.6 s (keyframe-aligned cut); now audio only, re-encoded.
- **Tests and gates (initial):** 24 passed in the three 2.5 unit files; `test_asr_persist.py` passed on 2.0's Postgres; a mid-session full `tests/unit` run had 668 passed, 4 failed, 8 setup errors, all in files 2.5 did not own.
- **Source:** `notes/2.5.md`, `reports/2.5-report.md`; commits `bd74e53`, `abadd4c`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.5 Review and fix round: the large-v3 gap was repetition loops at temperature 0

- **Review** (`2.5-review.md`): spec ✅ with gaps, quality approved with fixes; 0 Critical, 3 Important, 9 Minor. The reviewer recomputed pooled WER from `run1.novad-p50.json` (0.8153 / 0.5743), re-scored EXP-004 on CPU (0.676 / 0.514) and located every insertion: Sintel's ~1.6 WER for both models comes from the credits song after the last cue (131 words large-v3, 138 turbo); the ToS gap (insertions 117 vs 38) is large-v3 repeating lines at high confidence ("We're out of time, we're out of time!" x5 at 415-426 s, p 0.91-0.99; "Oh, my God." x6+ on Sintel). `temperature=0.0` turns off faster-whisper's temperature fallback, the retry that exists for these loops, and `min_prob 0.5` keeps them.
- **I-1 fix, three new arms** (no VAD, 3 fresh-process runs per model under `data/gpu.lock`, after waiting about 5 h behind 2.1's vLLM pass): `fb` (faster-whisper's fallback tuple 0..1.0), `fb-p50` (`fb` + `min_prob 0.5`), `fb-cond` (all faster-whisper defaults). WER now logged with S/D/I per film; earlier arms re-logged from their saved run JSONs (no new measurements for those). Result: large-v3 ToS insertions **117 → 33**; at `fb-p50` turbo 0.5428 vs large-v3 0.5548, RTF 0.0219 vs 0.0705, VRAM 1,605 vs 3,448 MiB; under plain `fb` turbo is 4.3 points worse; `fb-cond` unstable (WER SD 0.23-0.26) and rejected. The fallback makes transcripts non-deterministic (WER SD 0.003-0.018) and 1.6-2.1x slower (the re-reviewer measured 2.16x for large-v3 `fb`). The agent shipped turbo + `fb-p50` ([B-221](decisions.md#b-221--decoding-no-vad-temperature-fallback-min_prob-05-superseded-by-b-222-for-the-fallback-and-the-filter), later superseded) and retracted the round-1 claims in the notes.
- **I-2 fix, store stages:** `nip.timeline.asr.stage(ep)` (`timeline.asr`) and `nip.timeline.align.stage(ep)` (`timeline.align`) use `ep.conn` and `ep.unit(stage, episode_ref, model_variant=...)`; `persist_transcript` split from `persist_alignment(conn, ep_id, result)`; `tests/integration/test_asr_persist.py` runs both through `pipeline.run_store` (happy path, no ASR decision, failing backend → `completed_with_gaps`). Commit `0440e1d`.
- **I-3 fix:** `with_picture_timing` labels a frameless in-cut scene "(unplaced)" and "(not in cut)" only when `in_cut` is false; unit test added (`0440e1d`).
- **Controller note, layering:** `nip.timeline.{asr,align}` imported `nip.pipeline` (timeline → pipeline → agents), which broke `lint-imports`; `ep` is now typed by a `StageEpisode` Protocol ([B-226](decisions.md#b-226--timeline-stages-type-the-episode-with-a-protocol-instead-of-importing-nippipeline)). `lint-imports` 7 kept, 0 broken; `mypy src` clean (77 files). Commit `051534b`. Decision and config: `fa47838`.
- **Minors fixed:** M1 (EXP-004 scorer takes fps from the probe), M4, M5, M6, M7, M8 (the line variant's 400-char lookahead commented as load-bearing: widening it drops line 0.514 → 0.286). M2, M3, M9 recorded as doc corrections / revisit trigger.
- **Counts:** `tests/unit/timeline` 68 passed; `test_asr_persist.py` 2 passed (Postgres at 127.0.0.1:55432).
- **Re-review** (`2.5-rereview.md`): all three Important and the layering note ADDRESSED. The reviewer queried MLflow: `exp003-asr` holds **48 active runs** (8 configs x 2 models x 3 runs), the JSONs dated 15:42-16:09, all re-logged at 16:12, every reported mean matched. Key finding (N-1): for turbo the fallback gains nothing measurable: the ToS turbo run-1 transcripts of `fb-p50` and `novad-p50` are **byte-identical**, sub+del is identical (0.1239), EXP-004 identical; the 3.1-point WER gap is Sintel insertions in the credits song and the "Scales!" region; the -1.2-point margin over large-v3 is inside turbo's run spread (0.529-0.563), so "parity", not "turbo ahead". `novad-hst` is also an equal-footing arm that meets the bar (0.6059 vs 0.6036). N-2: idle device VRAM was 1,128-1,577 MiB in the fallback runs vs about 505-520 MiB in the round-1 runs, so the RTF cost compares sessions, not an interleaved re-run.
- **Source:** `notes/2.5.md` (Round 2), `reports/2.5-report.md` (Fix round 1), `2.5-review.md`, `2.5-rereview.md`; commits `0440e1d`, `051534b`, `fa47838`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.5 Controller ruling: the deterministic novad-hst decoding ships

- **Ruling** ([B-222](decisions.md#b-222--asr-decoding-default-is-the-deterministic-novad-hst-arm)): the ASR default is turbo-int8 with the `novad-hst` arm (temperature 0 only, `hallucination_silence_threshold` 2 s, `min_prob` 0), not `fb-p50`. Reasons in the ledger: it meets the bar, has better sub+del, and is deterministic (cacheable, reproducible re-runs); the fallback gained nothing measurable (ToS byte-identical). Cost if wrong: a rare repetition loop is not retried.
- **Applied in W-int** (commit `3d66622`, `config/pipeline.yaml timeline.asr`): turbo WER 0.606 vs large-v3 0.604 (+0.2 points, bar +1.0), sub+del 0.115 (fb-p50 0.124), ToS insertions 38 (fb-p50 37), RTF 0.0161 vs 0.0372 (2.3x faster), WER SD 0. The W-int agent first wrote two wrong claims into the yaml comment ("best sub+del of every config": `fb` has 0.113; "within the bar under every config": `fb` has turbo 4.3 points worse) and removed them after re-reading the table. `notes/2.5.md:26` was corrected. `eval/exp/exp003_asr.py` still says `SHIPPED = "fb-p50"`: left on purpose, because it only labels MLflow variant tags and changing it would relabel existing runs.
- **EXP-004 under the ruling:** the token variant is unchanged (0.676 with the cap); see [EXP-004](experiments.md#exp-004--script-to-transcript-alignment-token-level-monotonic-vs-rapidfuzz-line-level) for the line variant.
- **Also found by W-int:** without a pin the planner (quality objective) picked `faster-whisper-large-v3-int8` on the laptop, against EXP-003; `nip run` now pins asr to `timeline.asr.variant` unless `NIP_PIN_ASR` is set.
- **Source:** `progress.md` (2.5 ruling), `notes/2.Wint.md` (2b); commit `3d66622`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.4 Cue alignment: rank pairing failed on splits and merges, onset coincidence replaced it

- **What:** `nip.timeline.cue_align` links each target cue to its source cue(s). It fits one line per track, `target start = (1 + slope) x source start + intercept`, then links by overlap (at least 30 % of the shorter cue) with the source moved onto that line, so a drifted or shifted track still aligns and `loc.term` keeps working on it. `groups()` returns 1:1, 1:n, n:1 and n:m groups; `stage` persists `subtitle_cue.aligned_source_cue_ids` (`[]` = aligned to nothing, NULL = never aligned). Commit `fa1f18c`. Decisions [B-207](decisions.md#b-207--cue-alignment-fits-one-line-per-track-by-onset-coincidence-then-measures-overlap-on-that-line), [B-208](decisions.md#b-208--links-are-persisted-by-the-stage-the-checks-recompute-them).
- **Tried X, then Y** (full numbers in [C-30](experiments.md#c-30--cue-alignment-rank-pairing-vs-shifted-rank-pairing-vs-rank-free-onset-coincidence)):
  1. **Cue ranks as the first pairing, nearest-start rounds, Theil-Sen.** A synthetic track with every 10th pair merged and every 7th cue split locked onto a line one cue period off (offset 3.2 s, slope 0): nearest-start pairing under a one-cue shift is self-consistent and its residuals (+-0.5 s) pass a 1.5 s inlier test. 15 of the first 18 unit tests failed.
  2. **Rank pairing shifted by -5..+5 cues, keep the line with the most starts within 0.4 s.** Unit tests passed, but a 900-cue stress track with 12-25 % merges/splits and 4 % drift gave **0 of 916** exact links: ranks wander by about sqrt(n x p) = 10+ cues, more than the shift range.
  3. **Adopted, rank-free onset coincidence.** Both tracks' cue starts binned to 1 s; scaled source onsets cross-correlated with target onsets by FFT over a slope grid (step half a bin over the track length, +-30 %) and offsets +-60 s; the best coincidence seeds nearest-start + Theil-Sen rounds (tolerance 3, 2, 1.5 s). Stress: 916/916, 912/912, 905/905, 492/492, 887/887 exact links over five drift/offset settings; 1.3-1.7 s per 900-cue track (single thread), 0.04-0.15 s per Sintel/ToS track (26 / 76 cues).
  4. **Found by the store integration test (3b):** on a 5-cue track the coarse search over about 8,000 lines found a coincidence anywhere and reported a +29-frame offset on an identical track. A hysteresis against the identity line (best > 1.2 x identity + 2) did not stop it. Fix: refine two starts, "same timing" and the coarse one, and keep the one with more target starts within 0.4 s of their source on the final line ("same timing" wins a tie).
- **Test-data trap:** the first synthetic source had gaps and lengths from `i % 5`, `i % 4`, so it repeated every 20 cues and the onset coincidence was ambiguous at +-60 s (a 16 s offset reported). Real tracks are not periodic; tests use seeded random gaps. One stress run (-1.0 s offset) scored 76/890 only because the agent's test truth numbering was not renumbered after dropping cues that start before 0; the fit itself was right (slope 0.0, offset -1.0).
- **Source:** `notes/2.4.md` (journal "Cue alignment"); stress driver `scratchpad/a24/stress24.py` (not committed); commit `fa1f18c`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.4 loc.term, loc.shot_change, loc.drift and loc.offset: what each check does

- **loc.term** ([B-211](decisions.md#b-211--locterm-matching-rules)): glossary renderings (from the 2.9A sidecars) and bible names are matched word by word in the union of the aligned target cues of a group. Casefolded words; words of 3 letters or fewer ignored (articles) but positions kept.
  - **Tried X, then Y, set match:** content words compared as a set missed `Écrasement mémoire` for `Écrasement de mémoire` (ToS fr, seed 1: 7 of 8 planted terms, recall 0.88). Now the content words must form the phrase: same order, same number of words between them.
  - **Tried X, then Y, inflection:** accepting any 1-3 letter ending passed the planted variants `Thomas` (for `Thom`) and `Sintell` (for `Sintel`). Inflection is now allowed only for common nouns (no capitalised content word in the *source* surface), both stems at least 5 letters once accents are dropped, ending of 1-3 letters (`dragón`/`dragones`). An accent alone (`Capitan` for `Capitán`) is a misspelling. The first attempt took "common" from the capitalisation of the *rendering*, which broke German (`Drache` vs `Drachen`: every German noun is capitalised; this was the one clean-track term flag found while building, a matcher bug); it now comes from the source surface.
  - A missing word that resembles a target word (rapidfuzz `ratio` >= 75, >= 85 for words under 5 letters) is a misspelling at confidence 0.92; missing outright 0.80; a group of more than 2 source or target cues caps confidence at 0.65; observed-standard cases below 0.70 are suppressed for Phase 3 adjudication.
  - Registry: confirmed `entity` + `entity_alias` rows of the show, entities with the same name merged (a bible character and a glossary term), renderings by language tag with fallback (glossary `es-419` serves a track tagged `es`).
- **loc.shot_change:** an in or out point at distance 1..`ceil(fps x 1/2) - 1` frames from the first frame of a shot (11 at 24/1 and 24000/1001, 12 at 25), either side of the cut; the first shot's start is not a cut; the out point is the exclusive end frame. One flag per cue. (Changed in the fix round: an out point at cut - 2 and cut - 1 passes, [B-216](decisions.md#b-216--shot_change-passes-an-out-point-at-cut---2--cut-and-flags-points-on-either-side-of-a-cut).)
- **loc.drift:** the fitted slope compared with `rate / picture fps - 1` and `picture fps / rate - 1` for every `timing.sync_rates` entry (25, 24, 30, 24000/1001, 30000/1001; the picture's own rate skipped) within 20 %: 25/24 gives +4.167 %, 25 on 24000/1001 +4.27 %, 24 on 24000/1001 +0.1 %. An unknown slope above `drift_min_slope` 0.05 % and `drift_min_total_s` 0.5 s is still flagged at confidence 0.90 (0.99 when classified).
- **loc.offset:** the intercept when drift is significant, else the median difference; flagged at |offset| >= 0.25 s (6 frames at 24), reported in frames of the probed rate. Drift and offset flags claim the first and last cue of the target track (R12), `lang` = target language; recheck re-fits the track.
- **Inputs at first:** shot_change = picture + target subs; drift and offset = picture + both tracks ([B-209](decisions.md#b-209--drift-and-offset-require-the-picture-superseded-by-b-210), later superseded); term = both tracks + bible or glossary. `localization.CHECKS = [Readability, Term, ShotChange, Drift, Offset]`; thresholds in `config/languages/_base.yaml` `timing:` and `term:`.
- **Tests (initial):** `test_cue_align.py` 18, `test_term.py` 39, `test_shot_change.py` 29, drift and offset 45 together, `tests/integration/test_cue_align_pg.py` 1; every check flags exactly the planted cases at 24/1 and 24000/1001 (drift at 25/24, 1000/1001, 25025/24000, 1001/1000 and the inverse; offsets -24..+36 frames; with and without merged cues). ruff, mypy (77 files) and `lint-imports` (7 kept) clean. 2.0's `tests/unit/test_pipeline.py` was edited (three assertions: the `skipped` lists name the new checks), outside 2.4's file list.
- **Source:** `notes/2.4.md`, `reports/2.4-report.md`; commit `fa1f18c`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.4 Benchmark round 0 on the 2.9A copies of Sintel and Tears of Steel

- **Method:** `nip eval inject --set mvp --seed N --only loc` (run as `python -c "from eval.inject import app; app()"` because the CLI line was not wired yet), each injected copy ingested as a show into a throwaway Postgres database (`nip_a24`) with the sidecar bible and glossary and a `picture.json` made from the EXP-002 cuts, `run_store` with the two new stages, flags scored by `nip eval run` (MLflow experiment `loc-2.4`, tag `exp=2.4`), table from `nip eval compare --exp 2.4 --md`. Term and shot_change scored on the tracks the injector edited (es; ToS also fr), drift and offset on every track. "Native" labels = what the same check flags on the clean copy.
- **Numbers** (3 seeds): term 1.00 / 1.00 on both films; shot_change with natives 0.95 / 1.00 (Sintel) and 0.99 / 1.00 (ToS); raw 0.12 / 1.00 and 0.03 / 1.00; drift and offset 1.00 / 1.00. Full table in [C-31](experiments.md#c-31--localization-checks-on-the-29a-injected-sintel-and-tears-of-steel-rounds-0-1-and-the-w-int-re-run). Clean copies: Sintel es/fr/de 13 / 13 / 13 shot_change flags (39), ToS 40 / 40 / 41 (121), 0 term, drift or offset flags.
- **What the numbers did not show:** the official Sintel/ToS tracks are timed identically in every language, so alignment is trivially 1:1 there (1:n / n:1 only on synthetic tracks); the seeds only drew drift 25/24 (+4.169 % Sintel, +4.167 % ToS) and offsets of +-12 frames; no 24000/1001 film exists in the corpus. The 4 shot_change false positives left with natives (Sintel cues 17 and 19, ToS 12 and 11) are cues the *readability* injector edited, which moves a point 4-9 frames from a cut. Raw shot_change precision is low because the official subtitles are not cut-aware: 13 of 26 Sintel cues and 40 of 76 ToS cues per language have an edge 1-11 frames from a cut.
- **Problems that the review then raised:** the driver lived in the git-ignored workspace (`notes/2.4-bench.py`) and the 12 runs carried the commit tag of `bd74e53` (2.5's commit), because the 2.4 code was uncommitted when they ran. ToS `it` (the 2.9A offset injection) has no rule pack, so the driver ran Drift/Offset on it in-process ([B-215](decisions.md#b-215--the-tos-it-offset-injection-runs-in-process)).
- **Stage wall times** (Sintel, 3 target tracks): `timeline.cue_align` 0.12-0.15 s, `loc.observed` 0.01 s, all checks 0.07-0.2 s. "4 s of alignment for a 45-minute episode" is an extrapolation from the stress runs, not a measurement.
- **Source:** `notes/2.4.md` (Benchmark section, superseded by fix round 1), `reports/2.4-report.md`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.4 Review and fix round: the 2-frame gap, drift without a picture, a show-wide standard and a committed driver

- **Review** (`2.4-review.md`): spec ❌ (three spec gaps), quality ✅ with fixes; 0 Critical, 4 Important, 6 Minor ([review-findings.md](review-findings.md#24-cue-alignment-locterm-locshot_change-locdrift-locoffset)). The reviewer measured signed out-point distances of the official es tracks against the EXP-002 cuts (Sintel out -1 x2, ToS out -2 x2) and found the edges spread over -12..+12 frames with no peak at +-1, so most natives are real violations of the rule, not an off-by-one in the cut positions.
- **I-1, shot_change vs the 2-frame gap.** architecture.md says a cue ending within 1/2 s before a cut extends to it "keeping the 2-frame gap"; the readability gap rule asks for exactly that; shot_change flagged it, so a compliant track could not be flag-free. `ShotChange` now reads `min_gap_frames` from the readability pack and passes an out point at cut - 2, cut - 1 and the cut ([B-216](decisions.md#b-216--shot_change-passes-an-out-point-at-cut---2--cut-and-flags-points-on-either-side-of-a-cut)). Clean copies: Sintel 39 → **33** shot_change flags, ToS 121 → **115**.
- **I-2, drift and offset without a picture.** `Input.PICTURE` dropped; the slope is matched against the ratio of every ordered pair of `timing.sync_rates` (confidence 0.95; 0.99 with a probed rate) and the offset is stated in seconds only ([B-210](decisions.md#b-210--drift-and-offset-run-without-a-picture-and-say-less), superseding [B-209](decisions.md#b-209--drift-and-offset-require-the-picture-superseded-by-b-210)). 2.0's `test_pipeline.py` and `test_store.py` were edited again (3 extra done units, coverage 2/3).
- **I-3, observed standard over the show.** Other episodes are read from the store through `aligned_source_cue_ids`; a one-word name's rendering can be a translated word; an episode that switches is flagged at 0.80; `term.stage` recomputes and deletes stale rows ([B-213](decisions.md#b-213--observed-standard-over-the-whole-show-recomputed-every-run), superseding [B-212](decisions.md#b-212--observed-standard-per-episode-names-kept-verbatim-only-superseded-by-b-213)). The doc's own example (Wren kept in E1, E2, E4 at 5 mentions each, "Reyezuelo" in E3: 15 of 20) now gives 5 flags at 0.80; the integration test runs two episodes twice and stores `("Wren", es, "Wren", 6)` both times.
- **I-4, benchmark reproducible.** The driver is `eval/exp/loc_bench.py` with `tests/unit/eval/test_loc_bench.py`; 12 MLflow runs re-run with tag `exp=2.4r1`, all carrying the commit tag of `313ccd0` (the tag holds the pre-rewrite SHA). Natives are now called SELF-LABELLED and the raw column is the precision ([B-214](decisions.md#b-214--native-labels-for-shot_change-come-from-the-clean-run-and-are-a-regression-guard-not-truth), [B-217](decisions.md#b-217--the-benchmark-driver-lives-in-evalexploc_benchpy-and-runs-at-a-committed-sha)).
- **Round-1 numbers** ([C-31](experiments.md#c-31--localization-checks-on-the-29a-injected-sintel-and-tears-of-steel-rounds-0-1-and-the-w-int-re-run)): term 1.00 / 1.00 (12 and 24 planted); drift and offset 1.00 / 1.00 (3 per title and check); shot_change raw P 0.12 +- 0.04 / R 0.83 +- 0.29 (Sintel), 0.03 +- 0.01 / 1.00 (ToS). Sintel recall dropped from 1.00 to **0.83** because the 2.9A injector had planted an out point 2 frames before a cut (seed 3, cue 16), which I-1 made the compliant position.
