- **Minors fixed:** M-2 (`_base.yaml` read once per config dir), M-3 (loose source-side match for common nouns only: "Wren" no longer mentioned by "wrench", "Thom" not by "Thomas"), M-5 (the alias upsert only updates observed rows). M-1, M-4, M-6 recorded as decisions.
- **Counts:** `NIP_PROFILE=cpu pytest -m "not gpu" tests/unit tests/integration/test_store.py tests/integration/test_cue_align_pg.py`: 728 passed, 7 errors in `test_keyframes.py` / `test_shots.py` (media fixture errors only in the combined run; both pass alone; not 2.4 files). The re-reviewer ran the 154 unit tests of drift, offset, shot_change, term, test_pipeline and test_loc_bench: all pass.
- **Re-review** (`2.4-rereview.md`): I-1..I-4 ADDRESSED; checked the MLflow DB: 12 runs `exp=2.4r1`, all after the commit time. New minors M-a..M-g.
- **Found while running the suite, not 2.4's:** `lint-imports` broken by 2.5's `nip.timeline.align` / `nip.timeline.asr` importing `nip.pipeline`, and `mypy src` reporting `timeline_config` not exported by `nip.timeline.asr`; both fixed by 2.5 in `051534b`.
- **Source:** `notes/2.4.md` (Fix round 1), `reports/2.4-report.md`, `2.4-review.md`, `2.4-rereview.md`; commit `313ccd0`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.4 Follow-up in W-int: the injector stops planting a compliant out point

- **Controller assignment** ([B-218](decisions.md#b-218--24-follow-ups-assigned-to-w-int)): the 2.9A injector fix and the stale MLflow runs went to W-int.
- **Tried first:** draw the sign, then the distance from `MIN_GAP+1..11` for out points before a cut. The RNG stream changed for every seed and `test_shot_change_leaves_readability_clean` (seed 2) failed: a different cue moved and its reading speed went over the limit. Root cause: the injector's duration guard (0.8-1.25x) does not guarantee CPS, so the existing seeds pass by luck of the draw; recorded, not fixed.
- **Shipped** (commit `be494b4`): keep the old draw order and skip when an out point would land 1..2 frames before the cut. Regenerating seeds 1-3 changed exactly one label (Sintel seed 3, cue 16: "out-point 2 frames before the cut at 6112" became "in-point 5 frames before the cut at 6021"). New test: 40 seeds, no planted out point 1 or 2 frames before a cut. `loc_bench --checks loc.shot_change` scores only the named checks.
- **Re-run** (tag `exp=wint-sc`, 3 seeds): Sintel shot_change recall **0.83 → 1.00 +- 0.00**, meeting the 0.98 MVP target; ToS unchanged ([C-31](experiments.md#c-31--localization-checks-on-the-29a-injected-sintel-and-tears-of-steel-rounds-0-1-and-the-w-int-re-run)). The first bench run crashed (scoring subprocess exit 3221225477, access violation, as an image build ended; memory pressure suspected, not proven); its 7 partial runs were soft-deleted and the re-run was clean (1 m 32.5 s).
- **MLflow cleanup:** the 12 stale `exp=2.4` runs (11 with the commit tag of `bd74e53`, 1 with that of `7a664c1`) were soft-deleted (restorable).
- **Source:** `notes/2.Wint.md` (4c, MLflow cleanup, Experiment results); commit `be494b4`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.6 PaddleOCR beside torch: three conflicts, then RapidOCR

- **Brief:** try `paddlepaddle-gpu` beside torch cu130 first; on conflict fall back to RapidOCR. Laptop (RTX 4060 8 GB, driver 610.62), Sintel's 398 cached keyframes (1920x818 jpg), `data/gpu.lock` held for every timing.
- **Paddle installs and runs alone.** PyPI only has 2.6.2 (and would downgrade protobuf to 3.20.2); Paddle's own cu130 index has 3.4.0. In a scratch venv outside the repo: torch 2.13.0+cu130, then `uv pip install --index-url <paddle cu130> --extra-index-url pypi --index-strategy unsafe-best-match paddlepaddle-gpu==3.4.0` (uv's default index strategy refuses a package that exists on the first index at another version), then `paddleocr==3.7.0`. PP-OCRv6_medium det+rec 133 MB; paddle package dir 962 MB.
- **Conflict A, DLL search:** `paddle.utils.run_check()` failed (`cublas64_13.dll ... error code is 126`): Paddle looks only on PATH for the CUDA 13 libs its own wheels installed. Prepending those dirs fixed paddle alone.
- **Conflict B, both in one process:** paddle then torch → `WinError 127 ... torch\lib\cudnn_cnn64_9.dll` (paddle pins cuDNN 9.15.1.9, torch bundles 9.20.0: two cuDNN 9 builds in one process); torch then paddle → the same error on paddle's copy. After uninstalling paddle's `nvidia-*` wheels: torch first then paddle works; paddle first then torch fails (`shm.dll`, duplicate OpenMP runtime, paddle ships `libiomp5md.dll`). Workable only with a fixed import order: fragile.
- **Conflict C, the lock (decisive):** a copy of the project lock with paddle added: `uv lock` → "torch==2.13.0 depends on nvidia-nccl-cu13{sys_platform == 'linux'}==2.29.7 and paddlepaddle-gpu>=3.4.0 depends on nvidia-nccl-cu13{sys_platform == 'linux'}==2.30.7 ... incompatible". The nip image is Linux and torch is pinned to the vLLM 0.30.0 image's version, so paddle cannot share the one environment. Also: `paddleocr` pulls `opencv-contrib-python`, which collides with `opencv-python` (shared `cv2`), and paddle needs a second, non-PyPI index.
- **Paddle's numbers, for the record:** 5.24 keyframes/s (398 in 75.9 s), VRAM peak delta 1,300 MiB, 1,640 text lines: about 4x faster than what shipped.
- **Switched to RapidOCR 3.10.0** with the same model family as ONNX (`multi_PP-OCRv6_{det,rec}_medium`, weights from ModelScope into `models/rapidocr/`, SHA256 matching the package yaml): same first test line as Paddle ("THIS FILM WAS SUPPORTED BY", confidence 0.9999); text on 183/398 frames ([B-229](decisions.md#b-229--the-ocr-slot-uses-rapidocr-onnx-runtime-with-pp-ocrv6-medium-the-catalog-id-pp-ocrv6-medium-is-kept)).
- **Source:** `notes/2.6.md` (journal 1-6), `reports/2.6-report.md`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.6 RapidOCR on the GPU: a shadowed onnxruntime and a recognizer slower on CUDA than on CPU

- **ONNX Runtime GPU was silently CPU.** The first "cuda" bench gave 0.47 kf/s, slower than CPU (0.80). The log held `No registered plugin EP device found for 'CUDAExecutionProvider'`. Cause: `rapidocr` requires the CPU `onnxruntime`; uv installed it beside `onnxruntime-gpu`; both packages own the `onnxruntime/` directory and the CPU build overwrote the GPU one. Fix: `[tool.uv] override-dependencies = ["onnxruntime; sys_platform == 'never'"]` and `onnxruntime-gpu==1.31.0` (commit `eafce85`, [B-231](decisions.md#b-231--the-cpu-onnxruntime-is-excluded-from-the-lock)). With torch imported first, ORT reuses its CUDA 13 / cuDNN 9 DLLs.
- **The CUDA recognizer is slower than CPU.** On 30 keyframes (100-129): det 0.7 s on GPU vs 6.0 s on CPU, rec **5.3 s on GPU vs 0.5 s on CPU** (variable line widths → new input shapes). Tried `cudnn_conv_algo_search` HEURISTIC (kept) / DEFAULT (0.7 kf/s) / EXHAUSTIVE (1.04 kf/s), and padding rec batches to multiples of 160 px (no gain: rec 5.9-6.5 s). Shipped: detection on CUDA, recognition on CPU, 5.89 kf/s on those 30 frames ([B-230](decisions.md#b-230--ocr-detection-on-the-gpu-recognition-on-the-cpu), [C-33](experiments.md#c-33--onnx-runtime-cuda-detector-and-recognizer-where-each-runs)).
- **All 398 keyframes** ([C-32](experiments.md#c-32--ocr-backends-on-sintels-398-keyframes-paddleocr-gpu-vs-rapidocr-variants)): shipped 1.29 kf/s (309.1 s), VRAM peak delta 1,569 MiB, 1,607 lines; CPU only 0.80 kf/s; the shadowed build 0.60 kf/s; Paddle 5.24 kf/s. Mean OCR confidence 0.972; 93 lines below 0.80 (the future vlm re-read set). The 1,569 MiB includes torch's CUDA context and ORT's arena; the catalog's `pp-ocrv6-medium` estimate was 1.0 GB. About 400 keyframes per episode means about 5 min at 1.29 kf/s (arithmetic, not a measurement).
- **Source:** `notes/2.6.md` (journal 7-9); commits `eafce85`, `9c3bf5a`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.6 GLiNER and Presidio: recognizers for on-screen text

- **Deps** (commits `9b6787a`, `1507da1`, `eafce85`): rapidocr 3.10.0, onnxruntime-gpu 1.31.0, presidio-analyzer 2.2.364, gliner 0.2.29, urlextract 1.10.0, phonenumbers 9.0.41, spaCy 3.8.16, `en_core_web_sm` / `de_core_news_sm` 3.8.0 (MIT).
- **GLiNER** (`knowledgator/gliner-x-base`): installing gliner lowered `huggingface-hub` 2.2.0 → 1.33.0 and `transformers` 5.20.0 → 5.16.1 in the shared lock ([B-237](decisions.md#b-237--gliners-cap-transformers5170-is-accepted-for-now)). `from_pretrained` downloads fp16, bf16 and onnx copies (3.9 GB extra); the client uses `snapshot_download(ignore_patterns=[...])`, leaving model.safetensors 1,975,639,728 B. The repo config asks for the `stanza` word splitter (needs stanza + langdetect and downloads a tokenizer per language at run time); replaced by gliner's own whitespace/punctuation splitter. GPU: load 21.7 s, **24 ms/call**, VRAM peak delta **1,347 MiB** (catalog estimate 2.3).
- **Presidio:** `SlimSpacyNlpEngine` with MIT en/de and `spacy.blank` es/fr; the class fills `_blank_languages` only on a path that would load the GPL `es_core_news_sm`, so the private attribute is set directly; a test asserts neither GPL package is importable ([B-236](decisions.md#b-236--presidio-without-gpl-spacy-models-gliner-carries-names-gliner-with-its-own-word-splitter-and-no-extra-weights)). Engine + analyzer build 5.4 s. Recognizers for en/de/es/fr: `FictionAwarePhone` (regions US GB DE FR IL IN CA BR + ES), the stock `EmailRecognizer` (offline tldextract), `UrlExtractRecognizer` (bundled TLD list, never `update()`), plate regexes, a real-brand deny-list, `NerRecognizer` (GLiNER address / organization / product). No spaCy NER at all.
- **Fiction-reserved numbers** on the national significant number with `fullmatch` ([B-232](decisions.md#b-232--fiction-reserved-phone-numbers-are-matched-on-the-national-significant-number)); (212) 555-0123 is not a candidate per D-026 ([B-233](decisions.md#b-233--555-01000199-is-fiction-reserved-under-every-area-code), controller ruling). Known gap: a bare 7-digit number ("555-0142") is not detected by libphonenumber for US.
- **OCR URL cleanup, tried X then Y:** a general ". followed by 2-letter word" rule turned "Hello. It works" into the host `Hello.It` (Spanish "Dolor. Es muy" into `Dolor.Es`); restricted to known TLDs after a spaced dot ([B-241](decisions.md#b-241--ocr-url-cleanup-joins-a-spaced-dot-only-before-a-known-tld-cctlds-only-in-lower-case)).
- **Real-GLiNER findings that shaped rules** ([B-242](decisions.md#b-242--gliner-spans-that-overlap-a-structured-finding-are-not-second-findings)): it tags a bare phone line "+1 415 555 0100" as `address` (an address candidate now needs a word of at least 3 letters) and the host of a URL ("acme-shop.com") as an organization (a span overlapping a phone/URL/email span, fiction-safe ones included, is not a second finding).
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/agents/test_ocr_text.py` (the notes say 44 candidate/rows tests, the report 48 incl. 36 parametrized cases) + client tests: 112 passed with `tests/unit/runtime` (31 s); `test_ocr_detections_pg.py` passed on the compose Postgres. The GPU integration test did not run at first: 2.1's vLLM pass held `data/gpu.lock` for over an hour; it passed on CPU with real OCR + real GLiNER.
- **Source:** `notes/2.6.md` (journal GLiNER, Presidio), `reports/2.6-report.md`; commit `9c3bf5a`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.6 Review and two fix rounds: plates, Ofcom ranges and the transformers cap

- **Review** (`2.6-review.md`): spec ✅ with caveats, quality approved with fixes; 0 Critical, 3 Important, 13 Minor. Controller ruling applied by the reviewer: D-026 governs, so (212) 555-0123 is not a candidate.
- **I-1, plates over-match captions.** Presidio's `PatternRecognizer` defaults to `re.DOTALL | re.MULTILINE | re.IGNORECASE`, so the yaml's "upper case" never held, and the US alternatives matched "MAY 1985", "DEC 2019", "100 MPH", "300 BCE", "BOX 123"; DE matched "UP TO 50". Fix round 1 (commit `fa95853`): `global_regex_flags=re.DOTALL | re.MULTILINE`, the space-separated US alternatives dropped, DE needs the hyphen; negatives added.
- **I-2, GB drama ranges incomplete.** Ofcom's page answered HTTP 403 to WebFetch; the agent used britishtelephones.com's reproduction of Ofcom's table plus a web-search summary and added the 01xx `496` blocks, 029 2018 0xxx and 028 9649 6xxx; three blocks recalled by the reviewer (Coventry, Portsmouth, Southampton) were added as `# unverified`.
- **I-3, gliner caps `transformers<5.17.0`** for the whole environment including the vLLM image. Recorded as a decision (accept for now) ([B-237](decisions.md#b-237--gliners-cap-transformers5170-is-accepted-for-now)); controller ruling: W-int re-runs the vLLM image smoke.
- **M-9 fixed with I-1:** ccTLDs after a spaced dot only in lower case ("Hola . Es verdad" is no host).
- **GPU test:** a killed lock waiter of the agent had left `data/gpu.lock` created and owned with no process behind it; the agent took it over, ran `tests/integration/test_ocr_text.py` with CUDA detection and GLiNER on CUDA, **1 passed in 62.8 s**, and released it. After the plate/Ofcom changes the lock was held by 2.5, so the re-run (56 unit cases) was CPU-only with the real models: PASS.
- **Re-review 1** (`2.6-rereview.md`): I-1, I-2, I-3 ADDRESSED; **new Important N-1**: the narrowed patterns now missed "ABC 1234", "ABC 123", "123 ABC" and German "B AB 1234" (the hyphen is not printed on a German plate), and a missed plate is the costly side in clearance; hyphenated captions ("MAY-1985", "FBI-1234") still matched. M-b: remove the three unverified GB blocks.
- **Fix round 2** (commit `3c1a5de`): US/DE patterns allow one space again with named stop-lists in a negative lookahead (months JAN..DEC and BOX/DAY/VOL/ACT/... before "ABC 1234"; units MPH/KPH/BCE/... after "123 ABC"; function words UP/GO/IN/... before German "B AB 1234", which also needs 3-4 digits when spaced); the three unverified GB blocks removed. `test_ocr_text.py` 64 passed; real-model check on CPU: PASS ([B-238](decisions.md#b-238--plate-patterns-are-case-sensitive-allow-one-space-and-exclude-caption-words-by-name), [B-239](decisions.md#b-239--gb-drama-ranges-from-a-secondary-source-of-ofcoms-table-unverifiable-blocks-removed)).
- **Re-review 2** (`2.6-rereview2.md`): N-1 ADDRESSED. Probe of the yaml regexes: 16 of 16 plate shapes match; 26 of 50 caption-shaped strings still match (for example "NBA 2023", "ISO 9001", "TOP 100"). Judged acceptable for Phase 2: the residual error is extra `clr.plate` candidates, the safe side, and EXP-019 owns plate precision ([C-34](experiments.md#c-34--plate-regexes-over-three-rounds-captions-vs-real-plates)).
- **Follow-ups done elsewhere:** the vLLM image smoke on transformers 5.16.1 passed in W-int (load 197.7 s, 119.0 tok/s, slower than the earlier 85.4 s / 188.6 tok/s, n = 1, not attributed); the catalog `pp-ocrv6-medium` (`family: rapidocr`, `vram_gb: 1.6`) and `gliner-x-base` (`vram_gb: 1.4`) were corrected by 2.close (`d2a8fa3`).
- **Source:** `notes/2.6.md` (Fix rounds 1 and 2), `reports/2.6-review.md`, `2.6-rereview.md`, `2.6-rereview2.md`, `progress.md`; `notes/2.Wint.md` (smoke); commits `fa95853`, `3c1a5de`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.1 Knowledge layer: extraction, grounding, resolution and consolidation on recorded stubs

- **What:** one LLM unit per scene (`knowledge.extract`, unit key `{ep}/sc/{no}`). The prompt (`config/prompts/knowledge.extract.v1.md`, `string.Template`) gives the scene's lines numbered by seq (`SPEAKER: text` for dialogue), the registry and the bible entries of the characters present. The reply schema is built per scene: registry keys as an enum plus `UNKNOWN`, or free-text names (`enum=False`, the EXP-020 arm). A line number outside the scene is the only structural error and gets instructor's one repair; a bad quote never triggers a repair (D-025). `value_json` maps the value text to 2.3's shapes (`{"state": ...}`, `{"event": ...}`, `{"kind": ...}`, `{"importance": n}`). `save_events` runs grounding → resolution → `fact_event`; `save_speakers` fills `script_line.speaker_entity_id`. Commit `38fa323` (157 files, 142 of them recordings).
- **`LLMClient` now goes through instructor** (1.7 review M6, [B-079](decisions.md#b-079--only-the-llm-client-in-phase-1-stubs-are-minimal-valid-schema-instances-instructor-deferred)): JSON-schema mode, `max_retries=1`, `context=` as the validation context, temperature 0, `extra_body.chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking=false`. Two things broke on the way: instructor 1.17 takes `context=`, not `validation_context=` (a TypeError in the first probe); and the instance instructor returns subclasses the schema and carries `_raw_response` in `__dict__`, so `==` against a plain instance failed (1.7's wire test caught it). The client now parses the final reply into a plain instance ([B-152](decisions.md#b-152--llmclient-goes-through-instructor-and-returns-a-plain-instance-parsed-from-the-final-reply)).
- **Recorded stub (D-020):** `record_key = sha256(variant, system, prompt, JSON schema)`, stored as `tests/fixtures/llm/{key}.json`; `StubLLM` replays the recording of `qwen3.5-4b-awq` for the exact call, else falls back to `stub_instance`. With `NIP_LLM_RECORD_DIR` set every real reply is recorded. 1.7 review M5 done for numeric bounds and `maxLength`; `pattern` / `uniqueItems` stay documented gaps. 142 recordings, 560 KB ([B-153](decisions.md#b-153--recorded-llm-stub-keyed-by-sha256variant-system-prompt-schema)).
- **Grounding** (`grounding.py`): NFKC, typographic quote marks and dashes to ASCII, whitespace collapsed, then rapidfuzz `partial_ratio` against the cited line. A quote longer than its line is scored with `ratio`, because otherwise a fabricated quote that contains the whole line scores 100.
- **Resolution** (`entities.py`, first version): registry key, else WRatio ≥ 90 on lower-cased names including aliases, secret descriptions and earlier candidates, with a unique best entity; else a candidate entity (`status=candidate, origin=extracted`) with the name as alias and a kind guessed from the event role. Replaced in the fix round ([C-22](experiments.md#c-22--entity-name-scorer-wratio-vs-whole-word-subset-vs-exact-first)).
- **Consolidation** (`consolidate.py`): deterministic interval rules, overlap merge, confidence = min of the source events × `NIP_INFERRED_STORY_CONFIDENCE_FACTOR` when a source scene's story position is inferred; 2.2's `story_seq` when every scene of the show has one, else script order; `review_queue` returns facts below `NIP_FACT_REVIEW_THRESHOLD` and the candidate entities.
- **Broke along the way:** `_merge` sorted tuples containing `None` object ids (fixed with `object_id or 0`); `state: lost` was swallowed because the END verbs (holds "lose/lost") were applied to every predicate, fixed with the controller ruling in [B-160](decisions.md#b-160--fact-shapes-shared-with-23-and-state-lost-from-losedrop-events); the integration test re-patched `pipeline.STAGES` on its second run, so stages ran twice (146 superseded facts instead of 73), a test bug; ingest failed until the dev DB was migrated with `alembic upgrade head` (0001 → 0002), because 2.8's committed migration adds `script_line.speaker` and 2.8's ingest writer already fills it.
- **Tests:** 27 unit tests in `tests/unit/knowledge/test_{extract,extract_exp,grounding,consolidate}.py` (every consolidation rule, including the overlap merge, flashback order, the inferred factor and lost/destroyed) and 2 integration tests. `NIP_PROFILE=cpu uv run pytest -m "not gpu and not integration"`: **730 passed, 1 skipped** (ray); `pytest -m integration tests/integration/test_knowledge.py test_store.py`: **16 passed**. The cpu-profile run of synthetic S01E01 writes 75 `fact_event` rows = the 75 recorded S01E01 events, 26 units done. ruff, ruff format and mypy clean on 2.1's files; lint-imports 7 kept, 0 broken.
- **Source:** `notes/2.1.md`, `reports/2.1-report.md`; commit `38fa323`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.1 First real-model extraction pass: 150 scenes per arm, a whitespace runaway and a lock held for five hours

- **Commands** (dev override stack, under `data/gpu.lock`): `mkdir data/gpu.lock` at 10:19 EST; `up -d --wait ray-head` 13.1 s; `exp020_resolution up` (deploy inside ray-head) 5 m 21 s; `extract --variant enum --record` and `extract --variant free-text` from the host against `NIP_SERVE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8001`; `down`; `docker compose stop ray-head; rm -r data/gpu.lock` at 15:30 EST; then `exp020_resolution score`, `exp006_grounding sheet` and `sweep`, and `nip eval compare --exp EXP-006|EXP-020 --md --tracking-uri sqlite:///data/mlflow/mlflow.db`.
- **Measured** (real runs on the laptop, from `notes/2.1.md`):

| What | Number | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Plan for text_llm (probe at 590 MiB idle) | 16,384 context, gmu 0.79, KV 2,565,865,472 B, `--enforce-eager` | `up` output |
| qwen3.5-4b-awq load, fresh container | 314.2 s | `up` (`deploy`) |
| Device VRAM used while serving | 7,397 MiB | nvidia-smi |
| Enum arm, 150 scenes | 2,684.9 s wall, 17.9 s/scene, 8 scenes failed | `data/exp020/enum/meta.json` |
| Free-text arm, 150 scenes | 2,643.9 s wall, 17.63 s/scene, 7 scenes failed | `data/exp020/free-text/meta.json` |
| Raw events (enum / free text) | 564 / 653 | `events.jsonl` |
| Enum predicates | present_at 257, holds 107, mentions 67, learns 38, state 29, references 22, relationship 21, decision 18, wearing 3, setup 2 | `events.jsonl` |
| Grounding scores, enum (all 564) | 544 at 100, 20 below 85, none in [85, 100) | `events.jsonl` |
| Replay through 2.2 story + 2.1 + 2.3 rules, S01E01–E03 | 249 active facts; 8 `plot.knowledge_order` flags on the clean show, all false positives | scratch pytest, not logged |

- **The scene failures are a whitespace runaway inside guided decoding.** 8/150 (enum) and 7/150 (free text) scenes failed with `IncompleteOutputException` at `max_tokens` 2048. A raw call of S01E01/sc/20 with the same prompt and schema and no instructor shows the cause: the model stops mid-object (`"value":`) and then emits spaces until `max_tokens`; the JSON grammar allows any whitespace there, and the repair call hits the same wall. Not fixed in 2.1 (serving flags were not 2.1's files); proposal 6 asked for vLLM's no-whitespace option. W-int then added `disable_any_whitespace` to `serving.vllm_args`, W-serve measured that 2 of 25 scenes still ran to `max_tokens` with it on, and found the model loops inside valid JSON (sc/7: 42 events, 7 distinct, 40 `wearing` at confidence 0.0); the controller handed the runaway to 2.1B.
- **Throughput 17.9 s per scene** is slow next to EXP-001's about 150 tok/s; the notes attribute it to pretty-printed JSON replies (indentation tokens) and grammar-constrained decoding. Token counts were not logged, so this is not split further (an explanation, not a measurement).
- **Teardown:** `down` raised `ServingError: VRAM used 8095 MiB after teardown, idle was 590 MiB` after the 120 s wait; `docker compose stop ray-head` brought the device to 675 MiB. Possible cause, not investigated: the in-flight runaway raw request kept the engine busy.
- **The lock was held from 10:19 to 15:30 EST.** The two passes took about 1 h 35 m of it (load, then 44.9 + 44.2 min); the rest was idle time while the agent's session was paused by the API rate limit (ledger: rate limit #3 at about 14:40) with vLLM still up. 2.5 and 2.6 waited. Lesson recorded by the agent: tear down and release in the same command as the last pass.
- **Formatting outside its files:** `ruff format src/nip/knowledge` was run once over the whole directory, which also held 2.2's `story.py` and 2.8's `index.py` / `retrieval.py` (uncommitted then). It reported "2 files reformatted"; the agent believes those were its own two files but cannot prove it. Formatting only; every later run named files explicitly (review M-3).
- **Results:** [EXP-006](experiments.md#exp-006--grounding-validator-threshold) (no threshold meets the bar; hallucinated kept 0.93 at 85, 90 and 95) and [EXP-020](experiments.md#exp-020--entity-resolution-enum-constrained-ids-vs-fuzzy-matching-afterwards) first wave (resolution accuracy 0.95 enum vs 0.97 free text, inconclusive).
- **Source:** `notes/2.1.md` (Journal, Measured, Broke), `reports/2.1-report.md` (Concerns); ledger `progress.md` (2.1 entries).

### 2026-10-16 · 2.1 Review and two fix rounds: "tomato" is not Tom, and fact ids that survive re-consolidation

- **Review** (`2.1-review.md`): spec pass, quality approved with fixes; **0 Critical, 2 Important, 10 Minor**. The reviewer ran the 27 unit tests, confirmed instructor's `Mode.JSON_SCHEMA` sends a native `json_schema` `response_format`, probed rapidfuzz, checked that no free-text candidate is credited as a registry entity (0 of 271 grounded free-text candidate arguments map to a registry truth id), and checked consolidation against the `fact_consolidated_no_overlap` EXCLUDE constraint (satisfiable by construction).
- **I-1, name matching.** WRatio scores any containment at exactly 90 when the length ratio is ≥ 1.5 (`partial_ratio × 0.9`), so `WRatio("tomato", "Tom") = 90`, `WRatio("Dover Street", "Dov") = 90`, `WRatio("Ruins", "Rui") = 90`: an unknown prop or place containing a character's first name resolved silently to that character, with no candidate for the review queue. **Fixed** (`6b2ddf4`): both sides lower-cased and stripped of punctuation; a name scores 100 when its words are a subset of a known name's words *and* at least half of them, else rapidfuzz `ratio`. The reviewer's suggested `token_set_ratio` was rejected because it accepts the reverse containment ("Dov Street" → Dov, "Captain Ada Kovac" → Ada). The agent's first version (subset without the half rule) made every dialogue speaker NULL in the integration test: "TOMAS" is a subset of both "Tomas Vale" and the secret description "Tomas Vale takes money from Greywater Holdings", and the tie left it unresolved; the half rule fixed it ([B-161](decisions.md#b-161--names-match-on-whole-words-subset-with-at-least-half-the-words-an-exact-name-scores-101), [C-22](experiments.md#c-22--entity-name-scorer-wratio-vs-whole-word-subset-vs-exact-first)).
- **I-2, fact ids.** `write_facts` superseded every active fact of the show and reinserted all of them, and `load_timeline` resolves `fact/{id}` only for active facts, so episode 1's flag evidence stopped resolving after episode 2's consolidation and every re-run grew the table. **Fixed** (`6b2ddf4`): an active fact with the same identity (predicate, subject, object, value, story range, screen range) keeps its row and id; facts no longer produced are superseded before the inserts; new facts get `supersedes` = the superseded fact of the same (predicate, subject, object); counts are logged. Integration test: a second S01E01 run leaves the active ids and the row count unchanged; after S01E02, at least 80 % of S01E01's facts keep their ids and resolve, and every fact S01E02 closes is superseded with a successor ([B-158](decisions.md#b-158--consolidation-re-runs-show-wide-and-keeps-the-ids-of-unchanged-facts)).
- **Minors fixed in the same commit:** M-4 a quote needs two words unless it is the whole line (the reviewer showed `score("a", <any line containing 'a'>) == 100`); M-8 holds/owns/wearing events whose object did not resolve are not stored; M-9 `StubLLM` logs a recording miss; M-10 the integration test asserts the dropped events directly; M-6 caveats added to the EXP write-ups. **Not changed:** M-1 (raw-events upload inside the stage; `publish` was W-int's file), M-2 (accepted), M-3, M-5 (module constants kept under the controller's ruling, [B-151](decisions.md#b-151--knowledge-thresholds-stay-module-constants-until-configpipelineyaml-is-free)), M-7 (proposal only).
- **EXP-020 replay without the model:** `extract --replay` re-applies the shipped resolution and grounding to the saved raw events. Enum arm unchanged. Free-text arm: 41 arguments changed (phrases such as "we were meeting at Pier Nine" no longer resolve by containment); grounded events 631 → 629, recall 0.863 → 0.858, name-mention recall 0.81 → 0.77, accuracy stays 0.966. EXP-006 unchanged (same 300 ids, numbers identical).
- **Counts:** cpu **743 passed, 1 skipped**; integration (`test_knowledge`, `test_store`) **16 passed**.
- **Re-review** (`2.1-rereview.md`): I-1 and I-2 ADDRESSED; one **new Important, N-1**: an exact name scored the same 100 as a subset match, and `match` returns None on a tie, so with Tomas Vale and Mira Vale registered four `resolve(UNKNOWN, "Vale")` calls minted candidates 100, 101, 102, 103, and three "Ada Kovac" calls (with "Ada Maria Kovac" present) minted 104, 105, 106. **Fixed** (`608c5a1`): an exact normalized name scores 101. Found while testing: rapidfuzz's `default_process` keeps double spaces, so "ada  kovac" did not equal "ada kovac"; whitespace is now collapsed on both sides. EXP-020 re-replay: 8 free-text arguments map to a different truth id, metrics identical to 4 decimals (0.9664 / 0.5985 / 0.8575 / 629), so the fix-round-1 MLflow runs stand. Knowledge unit tests 75 passed; integration 16 passed.
- **Re-review 2** (`2.1-rereview2.md`): N-1 ADDRESSED, no new Critical or Important. 2.1 complete (`38fa323`, `6b2ddf4`, `608c5a1`).
- **Source:** `notes/2.1.md` (Fix rounds 1 and 2), `reports/2.1-review.md`, `2.1-rereview.md`, `2.1-rereview2.md`, `reports/2.1-report.md`.

### 2026-10-16 · W-int Wiring every W0/W1 stage and agent into `nip run`

- **What:** W-int ran alone after W0 and W1 were complete. It wired every content stage and agent package into the store pipeline that 2.0 built, applied the small cross-deliverable fixes the W0/W1 reviews had deferred to it, re-ran the vLLM image smoke on the new lock, and made the first end-to-end store runs on three titles. Commits on `phase-2`: `be494b4` (injector fix, `loc_bench --checks`, loc_bench mypy), `3d66622` (wiring, config moves, fixes 2b/3/4a/4b/4d/4e, the ASR script-only fix), `ff7b038` (serving placeholder integration test). All numbers below are from real runs on the laptop (RTX 4060 8 GB, desktop holding about 1.07 to 1.33 GiB of VRAM), 2026-10-16, about 16:15 to 17:10 EDT; GPU work ran under `data/gpu.lock`.
- **Baseline before any change** (HEAD `fa47838`): `NIP_PROFILE=cpu uv run pytest -m "not gpu and not integration"` 745 passed, 1 skipped (ray not on the host), 83 deselected, 71.0 s; the ten Postgres store test files 75 passed, 1 skipped (`test_real_embedder_ranks`, needs `NIP_INDEX_REAL`), 48.1 s; `mypy` had 1 pre-existing error (`eval/exp/loc_bench.py:157`, `parse_srt(...).data` may be None, from 2.4's `313ccd0`; fixed in `be494b4` with an assert); `nip --help` 2.54 s (one `time uv run nip --help`, uv overhead included).
- **Tried first (on paper, then checked against the tests): `add_stage` at import time of `nip.pipeline`.** Rejected: nine integration tests (`test_store`, `test_knowledge`, `test_story_pg`, `test_cue_align_pg`, `test_asr_persist`, `test_index_pg`, `test_layer1`, `test_store_logic`) copy `pipeline.STAGES` and add their own stage, so module-level wiring would run their stages twice and make every one of them also run ASR, extraction and the four SQL rules; it would also load the knowledge modules on every `nip --help`. **Shipped:** an idempotent `nip.pipeline.wire()` that `nip run` calls, with the imports inside it; a direct `run_store` call keeps only the core stages ([B-282](decisions.md#b-282--wiring-by-an-idempotent-pipelinewire-called-from-nip-run-not-module-level-add_stage)).
- **Stage order (differs from the brief's list):** `timeline.asr → timeline.align → knowledge.story → knowledge.extract → knowledge.consolidate → knowledge.index → timeline → timeline.cue_align → loc.observed → checks → verification → flags → report`. cue_align and loc.observed must come after `timeline` because `cue_align.stage` reads `ep.timeline`; story, extract, consolidate and index must come before `timeline` because `load_timeline` has to see the new `fact/{id}` rows and the aligned frames. The brief put cue_align before story; nothing in story or extract reads cue links, so the shipped order is data-equivalent and the only one that works ([B-283](decisions.md#b-283--stage-order-knowledge-stages-before-timeline-cue-alignment-after-it)).
- **`nip run --dry-run`** prints the stage order and the check ids without touching the database. `AGENTS` now holds localization, script_critic and clearance, giving 9 checks: `loc.readability`, `loc.term`, `loc.shot_change`, `loc.drift`, `loc.offset` and the four `plot.*` rules; `clearance.CHECKS` is still empty (2.7).
- **CLI:** `nip timeline asr|align` (2.5's sub-apps added under `picture.app`) and `nip eval inject` (`eval_app.command("inject")`); 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 2.4, 2.6 and 2.8 ship no sub-app. Cost: importing the old CLI module set takes 1.25 to 1.30 s and the new imports add 0.05 to 0.06 s (3 runs); `nip --help` now 2.25 s (one run).
- **Clearance OCR stage not wired:** 2.6 exposes `ocr_detections` and `insert_detections` but no `fn(ep)` stage, and the brief said to wire it only "if it exposes one"; `wire()` is in `pipeline.py`, which 2.7 does not own ([B-289](decisions.md#b-289--the-clearance-ocr-stage-is-not-wired-by-w-int-27-may-add-the-one-line)).
- **Found by the tests:** `test_cli_ingest_and_run` invokes `nip run`, which now wires globally, and the wiring leaked into the next test (`test_content_stage_records_units_once...` saw `timeline.cue_align` units and real extract units). Fix: the test monkeypatches copies of `STAGES` and `AGENTS`; it also asserts that the wired pipeline ran (`timeline.asr` in the stage list) and still gets 2 kept flags.
- **Model slots** ([B-284](decisions.md#b-284--nip-run-asks-for-asr-text_llm-and-text_embedder-asr-pinned-to-timelineasrvariant)): `nip run` passes `{asr, text_llm, text_embedder: len(episodes)}` to `current_plan`, and asr is pinned to `timeline.asr.variant` from `pipeline.yaml` (turbo) unless `NIP_PIN_ASR` is set. Without the pin the planner (quality objective) picked `faster-whisper-large-v3-int8` (gpu_fraction 0.38) on this laptop, against EXP-003's decision; with it: asr = turbo (0.19), text_embedder = qwen3-embedding-0.6b (0.20), mode resident. **text_llm is skipped ("no hardware for text_llm")**: the probe read 6.69 to 6.73 GB free and the planner needs the 6.25 GiB startup peak + 0.5 margin ([B-044](decisions.md#b-044--the-laptop-cannot-plan-an-llm-at-613-gb-free-exp-001-runs-before-the-walking-skeleton) behaviour, not a wiring bug). Without a text_llm decision `stage_extract` fell back to `StubLLM` and the story stage made no LLM calls. Under `NIP_PROFILE=cpu` every slot resolves to `stub`.
- **ASR decoding default (controller ruling, 2.5 re-review N-1)** ([B-222](decisions.md#b-222--asr-decoding-default-is-the-deterministic-novad-hst-arm)): `config/pipeline.yaml timeline.asr` = `min_prob: 0.0`, `decode: {temperature: 0.0, hallucination_silence_threshold: 2.0}`, the EXP-003 arm `novad-hst`. EXP-003 numbers quoted from notes/2.5.md (3 runs per arm): turbo WER 0.606 vs large-v3 0.604 (bar: within +1.0 point, met); turbo sub+del 0.115 (fb-p50: 0.124); turbo RTF 0.0161 (fb-p50: 0.0219); Tears of Steel insertions 38 (fb-p50: 37); WER SD 0, so the transcript is identical every run. **Caught before commit:** the yaml comment was first written with two wrong claims ("best sub+del of every config": `fb` has 0.113; "within the bar under every config": `fb` has turbo 4.3 points worse); both were removed after re-reading the EXP-003 table. `notes/2.5.md:26` and its EXP-003 decision line were corrected (the round-2 text called `fb-p50` "the only config where the stated bar is checked on equal footing and met", but `novad-hst` also meets it at +0.2 points). `eval/exp/exp003_asr.py SHIPPED = "fb-p50"` was left as is: it only labels the harness's MLflow variant tags, and changing it would relabel existing runs.
- **Knowledge constants into config** ([B-285](decisions.md#b-285--knowledge-and-story-constants-move-to-configpipelineyaml-with-identical-values)): a `knowledge:` section in `config/pipeline.yaml` (`extract.prompt_version` knowledge.extract.v1, `extract.max_tokens` 2048, `grounding.threshold` 90, `resolve.threshold` 90, `story.llm_max_tokens` 300, `story.excerpt_chars` 600, `story.days_per_unit` {WEEK 7, MONTH 30, YEAR 365}), read through a new `nip.config.pipeline_config()`; module names unchanged, values pinned by `tests/unit/knowledge/test_knowledge_config.py`. The 2.1 replay test still replays all 75 S01E01 events, so the prompt is byte-identical. `NIP_INFERRED_STORY_CONFIDENCE_FACTOR` (an env var read in story, consolidate and layer1) was not moved.
- **Source:** `.superpowers/sdd/phased-plan/notes/2.Wint.md`, `reports/2.Wint-report.md`, `briefs/2.Wint-brief.md`; commits `be494b4`, `3d66622`, `ff7b038`.

### 2026-10-16 · W-int Cross-deliverable fixes: raw events after commit, superseded facts, a vLLM whitespace flag, CI, the shot_change injector

- **4a (2.1 review M-1):** `EpisodeRun.artifacts` (name → bytes). `stage_extract` puts `knowledge.events.raw.jsonl` there and `publish()` uploads `runs/{run_id}/{ep}/{name}` after the commit; `test_failed_run_publishes_no_artifacts` covers the failure path.
- **4b (2.1 note):** `load_timeline` loads facts with status `active` or `superseded` (not `retracted`), so an earlier episode's `fact/{id}` evidence keeps resolving after a later consolidation closes it; `test_knowledge` asserts every S01E01 fact id resolves after S01E02's consolidation and that at least one was superseded ([B-286](decisions.md#b-286--load_timeline-resolves-superseded-facts-as-well-as-active-ones)).
- **4d (2.1 proposal 6, the ~5 % of scenes that ran to max_tokens):** `serving.vllm_args` always appends `--structured-outputs-config {"backend": "xgrammar", "disable_any_whitespace": true}`. Checked in the image: vLLM 0.30.0's `StructuredOutputsConfig` has `disable_any_whitespace` and rejects it unless the backend is `xgrammar` or `guidance`; the 2.1 note's `--guided-decoding-backend xgrammar:disable-any-whitespace` is the old syntax. Unit test parses the flag. Whether it removes the runaway was **not verified** here; W-serve later showed it does not ([journal](journal.md#2026-10-16--w-serve-the-extraction-runaway-a-loop-inside-valid-json-not-whitespace)) ([B-287](decisions.md#b-287--vllm-structured-outputs-run-with-xgrammar-and-disable_any_whitespace)).
- **4e (controller ruling at W0: adding the store tests to CI is W-int's):** CI's integration step runs the ten Postgres-only store files with `NIP_PROFILE=cpu` and `--deselect tests/integration/test_index_pg.py::test_real_embedder_ranks` (it downloads 1.2 GB under `NIP_INDEX_REAL`, and under `NIP_CI=1` a skip fails). Locally with the exact CI command and `NIP_CI=1`: 76 passed, 0 skipped, 50.0 s ([B-288](decisions.md#b-288--cis-integration-step-runs-the-postgres-only-store-tests)).
- **4c (2.4 review I-1): the 2.9A `loc.shot_change` injector planted an out point at cut−2, which 2.4 had made the compliant position.**
  - **Tried first:** draw the sign first and then the distance from `MIN_GAP+1..11` for out points before a cut. The RNG stream changed for every seed and `tests/unit/eval/test_inject_loc.py::test_shot_change_leaves_readability_clean` (seed 2) failed: a different cue was moved and its reading speed went over the limit. Root cause: the injector's duration guard (0.8 to 1.25x) does not guarantee CPS, so the existing seeds pass only by luck of the draw. Recorded as a latent 2.9A weakness, not fixed.
  - **Shipped (`be494b4`):** keep the old draw order `(randint(1, 11), choice(±1))` and `continue` when an out point would land 1..`MIN_GAP` (2) frames before the cut; every other plant stays byte-identical. Regenerating `--only loc` for seeds 1 to 3 (2.3 s per seed) changed exactly one label: Sintel seed 3, cue 16, "out-point 2 frames before the cut at 6112" became "in-point 5 frames before the cut at 6021". New test: 40 seeds with cuts reachable only by out points; no planted out point is 1 or 2 frames before a cut.
  - **`eval/exp/loc_bench.py --checks loc.shot_change`** scores only the named checks (labels, flags and natives filtered).
  - **The first bench run crashed:** the scoring subprocess `nip eval run` (Tears of Steel, seed 1, regression variant) exited 3221225477 (0xC0000005, access violation) after its MLflow run was logged, as the image build ended (20:40:23 UTC); memory pressure suspected, not proven. Its 7 partial `wint-sc` runs were soft-deleted and the bench re-run cleanly in 1 m 32.5 s.
  - **Result** ([C-44](experiments.md#c-44--locshot_change-recall-after-the-injector-stops-planting-a-compliant-out-point)): Sintel shot_change recall **1.00 ± 0.00**, up from 0.83 ± 0.29 in 2.4's round 1; Tears of Steel unchanged; clean-copy natives unchanged (Sintel 33, ToS 115).
- **MLflow cleanup:** in experiment `loc-2.4`, `MlflowClient.delete_run` soft-deleted (lifecycle `deleted`, restorable) the 12 runs tagged `exp=2.4`: 11 carry the commit tag of 2.4's first commit (`bd74e53` today) and 1 (`469685959a07…`, variant `2.4-det-sintel`, seed 1) the tag of `7a664c1`, from the same stale round-0 set (superseded by `2.4r1`), so it went too. The reviewer confirmed it was beyond the brief, disclosed and restorable.

- **Why (controller ruling):** 2.6's `gliner==0.2.29` caps transformers below 5.17, which moved the lock from transformers 5.20.0 to 5.16.1 and huggingface-hub 2.2.0 to 1.33.0; the controller ruled that W-int must re-run the vLLM image smoke ([B-290](decisions.md#b-290--the-vllm-image-smoke-is-re-run-on-the-new-lock-with-a-forced-exact-kv-setting)).

- **`pip check`:** the same 5 lmcache lines and the nccl line as the Phase 1 image smoke, plus one new: `faster-whisper 1.2.1 requires onnxruntime, which is not installed`. onnxruntime-gpu 1.31.0 is installed (`eafce85` dropped the CPU wheel); faster-whisper needs onnxruntime only for Silero VAD, which is off (`vad: false`).
- **GPU lock 16:44:02 → 16:50:06 EDT**, VRAM 1,100 MiB before. `docker compose -f compose.yaml -f eval/exp/exp001.compose.yaml up -d --wait ray-head` 15.0 s; RAM 1.12 GiB.
- **`tests/integration/test_serving.py` in ray-head (9.7 s): 1 failed, 1 skipped, first misread as "1 passed".** The agent's `grep -v warn` filter also hid pytest's "1 failed, 1 skipped, N warnings" summary line; a re-run at 17:09 showed the failure. `test_placeholder_app_answers_and_is_deleted` deploys `App("it-placeholder", (Slot.ASR,))`; since 2.0's `_bind` hook a slot whose `clients/<slot>.py` defines `Actor` gets that actor, and 2.5's `clients/asr.py` does, so the replica built `FasterWhisperASR("it-placeholder")` and raised `KeyError: 'it-placeholder'` in `FALLBACK_REPOS`. Latent since 2.0 + 2.5 (2.0 moved the unit test to `Slot.DETECTOR` but not this integration test, which only runs inside ray-head); unrelated to the lock. **Fix `ff7b038`:** `Slot.DETECTOR`. Re-run under the lock (17:09 to 17:10:12): **1 passed, 1 skipped, 6.73 s**. `test_laptop_staged_plan...` skipped both times: "not enough free VRAM for the LLM right now: no hardware for text_llm, no hardware for vlm".
- **One qwen3.5-4b-awq start** (scratch driver `smoke_vllm.py` in ray-head; EXP-001's `kv-planner` setting forced, `--kv-cache-memory-bytes 1006632960`, gmu gate 0.5, 8k context, `--enforce-eager`, args built by the shipped `serving.vllm_args`, so the new flag was included; the planner's own text_llm path refused at this VRAM): the engine config printed `structured_outputs_config=StructuredOutputsConfig(backend='xgrammar', disable_any_whitespace=True, ...)`; **load 197.7 s** (the whole `deploy()`; "Model loading took 3.91 GiB memory and 43.56 s"; the KV allocation was logged about 2 min after the API server start); KV 21,597 tokens, 2.64x at 8k (EXP-001 exact KV: 2.64); **119.0 tok/s** (EXP-001's `_bench`, concurrent); guided JSON 2.07 s, 24 tokens, `{"color": "red", "items": ["strawberries", "apples", "tomatoes"]}`, valid, with exactly one space after `:` and `,` (xgrammar uses fixed separators when any-whitespace is off); unload 4.0 s; device peak 7,692 MiB (6,359 over the idle 1,333), back to 1,299 MiB.
- **Against the Phase 1 smoke** (`notes/image-smoke.md`: 85.4 s load, 188.6 tok/s): load 2.3x slower and throughput 0.63x, n = 1, on the first container of a new image (the Phase 1 smoke also saw a slow first container: test suite 263.9 s cold vs 137.8 s warm), with the desktop using the GPU at 8 to 9 % throughout. **Not attributed.** Verdict **PASS**: the transformers / hf-hub / openai downgrades did not break vLLM serving, chat completions or xgrammar guided JSON ([C-45](experiments.md#c-45--vllm-on-the-rebuilt-image-transformers-5161-lock-vs-the-phase-1-image-smoke)).

### 2026-10-16 · W-int First end-to-end store runs: three titles, no gaps, and no real LLM

Films: 2.5's derived Fountain scripts, en source + es/fr/de targets, the picture, bible, glossary and clearance-log sidecars (Sintel `picture.json` from 1.10: 162 shots, 398 keyframes; Tears of Steel from 2.5: 140 shots, no keyframes). Synthetic: seed-1 show (`write_show(build(1))`), bible + 6 episodes.

The second native crash at process exit that day (the first was the 0xC0000005 bench subprocess); not reproducible, root cause unknown; `PYTHONFAULTHANDLER=1` added to the run env.
- **Pass A, `NIP_PROFILE=cpu`:** Sintel 17.5 s, completed, 45 units done, 53 flags (readability de 6 / es 6 / fr 8, shot_change 11 each); asr 13.35 s is the stub ASR's S3 download + audio extract for an empty transcript. Tears of Steel 8.5 s, 65 units, 211 flags (readability 34/33/29, shot_change 39/38/38). Synthetic 7.2 s, 6 × 31 units, 19 `plot.knowledge_order` flags (E1 2, E2 4, E3 2, E4 2, E5 4, E6 5). Knowledge rows on the synthetic show: 541 fact events, 500 active facts, 23 superseded, 319 speaker lines resolved, 150 `story_seq` set. The films get 0 events (the stub's minimal instance). No gaps, no crashes in `nip run`.
- **Pass B, laptop Plan (asr turbo on the GPU, text_embedder on CPU, text_llm skipped) under the lock.**

Its walls (Sintel asr 99.8 s, index 65.4 s) include the downloads and were discarded. The agent did not delete the directories (the user's home); the controller later recorded "Do NOT delete C:\Users\pc872\.cache\huggingface — it also holds the user's own models (faster-whisper-base, GLM-5.2); ask user", and W-serve made `HF_HOME` default to the repo ([B-298](decisions.md#b-298--importing-nip-defaults-hf_home-to-the-repos-model-cache)).

Both downloads ran at 184 MiB/s from SeaweedFS on loopback. The index wall is almost all model load: the first (discarded) attempt had already embedded the scenes.
  - **Synthetic on the laptop Plan** (no GPU, no lock): 46.4 s, 6 × 31 units, the same 19 flags; index 37.6 s over 6 episodes (first real-embedder pass over 150 scenes on CPU, model load included); asr records no unit (after the fix below).

Fix: a `_video_key()` check before the unit; new integration test `test_a_script_only_episode_with_an_asr_decision_records_no_unit`.

Input for 2.1B (argument type checks, EXP-006 proposal 8).

The controller answered with a new W-serve step ([B-292](decisions.md#b-292--a-w-serve-step-before-w2-nip-run-inside-ray-head-with-staged-serving-and-an-hf_home-default)).

### 2026-10-16 · W-serve Design: stage segments, only vLLM slots deployed, skipped slots recorded, `HF_HOME` default

- **What:** a way to run `nip run --show S --episodes E…` with real models on the laptop: `nip run … --in-cluster` inside the ray-head container, with each Serve group deployed around the stages that call it, torn down, and VRAM checked back to idle before the next. Plus the W-int review's I-1 and the `HF_HOME` default. Commit `c02a542`; fix round `dff0f7f`. Real runs on the laptop (RTX 4060 Laptop 8 GB, driver 610.62), 2026-10-16, about 17:20 to 19:09 EDT, under `data/gpu.lock`; raw outputs in `data/wserve/`.
- **What the stages actually call:** only `text_llm` goes over HTTP to a Serve app (`LLMClient` → `/models/<variant>`, used by `knowledge.story` and `knowledge.extract`). `timeline.asr` builds faster-whisper in-process (cached, CUDA) and `knowledge.index` builds sentence-transformers in-process on CPU. So `serving.apps()` over a whole Plan group would deploy an ASR and an embedder Actor that no stage calls, loading whisper twice on an 8 GB GPU. **Decision:** deploy only slots whose variant engine is `vllm` (`pipeline.serve_slots`) ([B-294](decisions.md#b-294--only-vllm-slots-are-deployed-as-serve-apps-actor-slots-stay-in-process)).
- **Tried first (on paper): wrap the whole run in 1.7's `serving.run(plan, …)`.** Rejected: it deploys every group's apps, including the unused actors, and its `work(group)` callback does not fit the pipeline, because the stage order puts `knowledge.index` (group `text_prep`) after `knowledge.extract` (group `llm`), the reverse of the Plan's group order. **Shipped:** `pipeline.segments(STAGES, served)` cuts the stage list where the Serve group a stage needs changes; with the wired order: `[asr, align]` (no app) → `[story, extract]` in the `llm` group (the model loads once per run) → `[consolidate … report]` (no app) ([B-295](decisions.md#b-295--staged-serving-is-cut-into-stage-segments-not-17s-servingrunplan-work)).
- **`serving.group(apps, serve)`**, a context manager: measure idle VRAM (NVML), deploy, run the body, on exit record `up_mib`, tear down, wait for VRAM within 256 MiB of idle (1.7's check) and record `after_mib`; on an error it still tears down and keeps the original error. `serving.run` was rewritten on top of it and 1.7's tests pass unchanged. Before a group loads, `cluster_deploy` calls `asr.backend.cache_clear()` and `gc.collect()`; **not exercised** (every run was script-only), and whether CTranslate2 frees its VRAM on GC inside the container is unmeasured.
- **Host-side `nip run`** (no `--in-cluster`): `without(plan, served, …)` moves the vLLM slots to `skipped_slots` with `no Serve for text_llm: not deployed outside the cluster; run docker compose … exec ray-head nip run … --in-cluster`. `--in-cluster` on the host raises `BadParameter` with the in-cluster command (`import ray` fails there).
- **W-int I-1, fixed in `pipeline.py` only** (the stage modules `extract.py`, `index.py`, `asr.py` were not touched) ([B-296](decisions.md#b-296--a-skipped-slots-stages-are-skipped-by-one-guard-in-run_store-and-the-checks-they-feed-are-gaps)): `STAGE_SLOTS` maps `timeline.asr`→asr, `knowledge.story`/`knowledge.extract`→text_llm, `knowledge.index`→text_embedder; a stage whose slot is in `plan.skipped_slots` is not called and appears as `{"stage", "wall_s": 0.0, "skipped": true}` with its reason in `skipped_stages`. `knowledge.story` is `RULES_ONLY` (it runs on rules and says so). `FEEDS = {"knowledge.extract": "plot."}`: the plot rules are skipped with extraction, so script_critic coverage is 0.0 and the run is `completed_with_gaps`. ASR and index skips are recorded but are not gaps (no check reads their output). `ReportPlan` gains additive optional `skipped_slots` and `warnings`; the run summary gains `plan {mode, groups, skipped_slots, warnings}` and `serving [...]`. The guard keys on `skipped_slots`, not "absent from `decisions`", so the tests' `stub_plan()` (`decisions={}`) keeps the stages' own behaviour. The `ReportPlan` change broke `test_documented_report_validates_and_round_trips` (the docs' example lacks the keys); the expectation now adds the two empty fields.
- **"Say exactly why: free vs needed"** ([B-297](decisions.md#b-297--a-vram-skip-says-free-vs-needed-from-the-planners-own-formula)): `current_plan` replaces the planner's bare `no hardware for <slot>` with `no_fit()`, which computes the smallest candidate's per-GPU need at its minimum context against the roomiest GPU's usable memory. Real output in the container: `no hardware for text_llm: qwen3.5-4b-awq needs 6.25 GiB on one GPU, GPU 0 has 6.20 usable = min(0.90 x 8.00 total, 6.70 free - 0.50 margin): short 0.05 GiB`. A unit test checks the planner places text_llm at 6.75 GiB free and skips it at 6.70.
- **`HF_HOME` default** (W-int proposal W-7, ruled by the controller) ([B-298](decisions.md#b-298--importing-nip-defaults-hf_home-to-the-repos-model-cache)): `nip/config.py` runs `os.environ.setdefault("HF_HOME", …)` at import (`$NIP_MODEL_CACHE/hf`, else `<repo>/models/hf`), and the previously empty `nip/__init__.py` imports `nip.config`, so any `import nip.*` sets it before a Hugging Face library loads. Test: a fresh interpreter runs `import nip, huggingface_hub.constants` in 3 cases (unset, `NIP_MODEL_CACHE=X`, explicit `HF_HOME` kept). The ~2.8 GB W-int left on C: was not touched.
- **compose.dev.yaml** ([B-299](decisions.md#b-299--composedevyaml-mounts-the-weights-and-the-source-into-ray-head)): the named `models` volume held none of the weights in `models/hf`, and the 26.5 GB image runs its baked source copy, so the dev overlay's ray-head now mounts `./models:/models`, `./src`, `./config`, `./eval`, `./tests` with `PYTHONPATH=/app/src:/app` (the set 1.7's `exp001.compose.yaml` used). `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` must be set when the container is created, not with `exec -e`, because the vLLM child inherits Ray's environment. Up with `--wait` in 13.5 s; ray-head idle RAM 1.12 GiB.
- **The probe inside the container is truthful for free memory** ([C-47](experiments.md#c-47--free-vram-seen-inside-ray-head-nvml-cuda-vs-the-hosts-nvidia-smi)): three paired readings (host `nvidia-smi`, then pynvml + `torch.cuda.mem_get_info` in ray-head, then host again): container NVML free always fell between the two host readings; container NVML used was about 238 MiB higher (total − free includes the ~231 MiB the host lists as neither used nor free); CUDA in the container reported a fixed 7096 MiB (6.93 GiB), as in 1.7. The probe uses NVML free.
- **Source:** `notes/2.Wserve.md` (Design, I-1, W-7, compose.dev.yaml, probe); `reports/2.Wserve-report.md`; `briefs/2.Wserve-brief.md`; commit `c02a542`.

### 2026-10-16 · W-serve The real run on synthetic S01E01: BLOCKED-ON-VRAM at margin 0.5, end to end at 0.25

- **Inputs:** show `wserve-synthetic` (synthetic seed-1 `bible.yaml` + `S01E01.fountain` from W-int's `data/wint/synthetic/`), ingested from the host in 3.8 s and 3.6 s. Script-only, so no ASR.
- **Run 1, shipped margin 0.5** (lock 17:36, `data/wserve/run1.json`): probe 6.70 GiB free, `skipped_slots: [text_llm]`, **short 0.05 GiB**; wall **39.4 s**, `completed_with_gaps`; "knowledge.story ran on rules only: …" and "knowledge.extract skipped: …"; 2 units done (consolidate, index); the 4 plot checks in `skipped`, script_critic coverage 0.0; index 28.9 s (Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B on CPU, first load); `serving: []`.
- **GPU integration test `tests/integration/test_staged_run.py`** (17:38:34 to 17:38:40): 1 skipped, 3.26 s, "…GPU 0 has 6.04 usable = min(0.90 x 8.00 total, 6.54 free - 0.50 margin): short 0.21 GiB".
- **VRAM over 25 minutes** (host `nvidia-smi` every 30 s, 17:37 to 18:02, `data/wserve/vram-poll.log`): the desktop held 1,065 to 1,669 MiB, leaving 6,288 to 6,892 MiB free; the best reading (6,892 MiB = 6.73 GiB) still gives usable 6.23 < 6.25, **short 0.02 GiB**. text_llm needs at least 6.75 GiB free as the probe rounds it (6,912 MiB), meaning the desktop at about 1,045 MiB used or less. A 30-minute watcher for 6,960 MiB free never fired. `nvidia-smi` listed 26 desktop processes on the GPU (Chrome, Teams, ChatGPT, Telegram, Stremio, an IDE, Edge WebView ×5, …); WDDM shows no per-process numbers; nothing was closed or changed. **Verdict for the shipped path: BLOCKED-ON-VRAM, short 0.02 to 0.21 GiB.**
- **Run 2, margin lowered to 0.25 as a diagnostic in one process** (`python3 -c "import nip.pipeline as p; p.MARGIN_GB = 0.25; …"`, same command; lock 18:02:35 → 18:16:47; `data/wserve/run2.json`, `run2-vllm.log`). Not shipped; the reviewer called it "a hack, not a knob" (I-2).
  - VRAM at start 1,264 used / 6,693 free MiB (host); probe 6.54 GiB free. Plan staged `timeline+text_prep` → `llm`; qwen3.5-4b-awq at **16k context** ("text_llm context reduced 32768 -> 16384"), gmu 0.79, **kv_cache_bytes 1,889,533,952 (1.76 GiB)** = usable 6.29 − weights 4.0 − overhead 0.53. Only `nip-qwen3.5-4b-awq` was deployed.
  - **Wall 852 s (14 m 12 s), `completed_with_gaps`.** llm group: load **254.9 s**, NVML used 1,495 MiB idle → **7,585 MiB** up → **951 MiB** after teardown (the desktop dropped meanwhile; the idle + 256 check passed), unload **6.0 s**. vLLM log: weights 41.96 s (3.91 GiB, 9P mount), engine init 126.0 s, server up 3 m 45 s after the log starts; CUDA "initial free memory 6.93 GiB"; KV **46,996 tokens, 2.87x at 16,384**.
  - Stage walls: **extract 559.8 s**, story 3.97 s, index 15.1 s, the rest under 0.15 s. **knowledge.extract 25 scenes: 23 done** (mean 14.85 s, max 24.66 s), **2 failed** (mean 109.4 s, max 122.7 s): `S01E01/sc/7` and `sc/25`, both `IncompleteOutputException` at the 2,048-token limit, recorded as gaps. So W-int's `disable_any_whitespace` flag does not remove 2.1's runaway.
  - **117 fact_event rows, 112 active facts** (W-int's replay stub: 541 / 500 over 6 episodes). Story: the real `LLMClient` was built (3.97 s, mostly the instructor/openai import, vs 0.04 s rules-only) but **0 LLM asks**: every S01E01 transition was settled by rules or the memo.
