### 2026-10-16 · W-serve review and fix round: deploy failures are gaps, `NIP_VRAM_MARGIN_GB`, one stage-major order

- **Review** (commit `c02a542`; reviewer re-ran `pytest tests/unit/test_pipeline.py tests/unit/test_config.py tests/unit/contracts/test_report.py tests/unit/runtime`: 92 passed; no Postgres/GPU re-run): spec ✅ with caveats, quality approved with **3 Important, 7 Minor**. I-1 a serving error in a group discards the whole run (deploy `ServingError` rolls back every episode; post-work teardown raises if the desktop grew 256 MiB during a 14-minute extraction); I-2 no margin knob, so the one-command real run worked only by code injection; I-3 multi-episode results depend on where the run executes (cluster segment-major, host per-episode).
- **Controller rulings:** `NIP_VRAM_MARGIN_GB` env knob, default 0.5, laptop may set 0.25 ([B-300](decisions.md#b-300--nip_vram_margin_gb-default-05-the-8-gb-laptop-may-set-025)); a single execution order everywhere, by stage group across all episodes ([B-301](decisions.md#b-301--one-execution-order-everywhere-each-stage-over-all-episodes-before-the-next-stage)). Rate limit #4 (~20:20 by the ledger) interrupted the fix round, which then resumed.
- **Fix `dff0f7f`:**
  - **I-1** ([B-302](decisions.md#b-302--a-serve-group-that-fails-to-deploy-is-a-coverage-gap-vram-drift-after-the-work-is-a-warning)): `run_store` enters each `deploy(group)` through an `ExitStack`; a `ServingError` on enter (already torn down by `serving.group`) moves the group's slots out of every episode's Plan via `without(…, "group <name> failed to deploy: <first line>")`, and `degrade(ep, plan)` re-applies the I-1 machinery (recorded skips, story on rules, `plot.*` into `skipped`/`lost`), so the run commits `completed_with_gaps` with `serving[]` keeping `{group, stages, error}`. Up-front skips now go through the same `degrade()`, and `skipped_stages` is per episode (before, all episodes shared one dict). After successful work, VRAM not back within tolerance is `rec["warning"]`, not an exception; `serving.DRIFT_TOLERANCE_MIB = 1024` for a run's group (the implementer's value: the desktop moved 604 MiB in 25 minutes, a left-over model is at least 1.5 GiB); `teardown()` gained `tolerance_mib`, default still 256; 1.7's `serving.run` inherits warn-not-raise. M-5: a failing run logs its serving records before rollback.
  - **I-2:** `nip.config.vram_margin_gb()` reads `NIP_VRAM_MARGIN_GB` (default 0.5); `current_plan` reads it once and passes `planner.plan(margin_gb=…)` and `no_fit(…, margin)`; `pipeline.MARGIN_GB` is gone; `.env.example` documents 0.25 for the 8 GB laptop. M-1: `need_gb` reuses `planner._kv_gb`; M-2: `no_fit` returns the bare reason when VRAM is not the cause.
  - **I-3:** `run_store` runs each stage over every episode, in episode order, before the next stage, on the host and in the cluster; `segments()` only decides where a Serve group is held up. `test_host_and_cluster_runs_give_identical_flags_on_two_episodes` (wired pipeline, cpu stub Plan, synthetic seed-1 bible + S01E01-02, replayed extraction, a fresh store per path): **6 flags on both paths, identical** (check, fingerprint, title).
  - **Found by the parity test:** `FakeS3` in `tests/integration/conftest.py` had no `get_object`, which story uses to read the stored bible, so any FakeS3 run with a bible failed (the gpu test would have too). Added.
- **Real runs after the fix** (lock 18:38 → 19:08):
  - **Tried first, failed in 11 s before any GPU use:** ray-head was brought up from a shell that had sourced `data/wint/env.sh` for the host ingest, so compose substituted the host's `NIP_DATABASE_URL=…127.0.0.1:55432` into ray-head (`connection refused`, exit 1). Recreated from a clean shell (`--force-recreate`) and checked `NIP_DATABASE_URL=…@postgres:5432`, `NIP_S3_ENDPOINT=http://objectstore:8333`, `HF_HOME=/models/hf`, `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`.
  - **Run 3** (`exec -e NIP_VRAM_MARGIN_GB=0.25 ray-head nip run --show wserve-r1 --episodes S01E01 --in-cluster`, fresh slug `wserve-r1`, `data/wserve/run3.json`): desktop 688 MiB used at 18:39:08, probe 7.10 GiB free; staged, 16k context, KV 2,490,368,000 B; **wall 774.2 s**; llm group load **272.0 s**, VRAM 930 → **7,792** → 597 MiB, unload 5.0 s; extract 459.7 s, story 3.26 s, index 22.6 s; **25 extract units, 23 done** (mean 12.25 s, max 18.77 s), **2 failed** (again `sc/7` and `sc/25`, 89.1 s each on average); **117 fact events, 112 active facts, 8 flags** (5 / 1 / 2), counts, titles and tiers identical to run 2, so this episode is deterministic at T = 0.
  - **gpu test at the shipped default margin 0.5** (desktop light at 18:52: 388 MiB used, 7,569 free): **1 passed in 629.06 s**; load 159.3 s, VRAM 608 → 7,764 → 561 MiB, unload 4.0 s; units 29 done, 2 failed. The first time the shipped path ran a real LLM with no knob and no patch.
- **Tests after the fix:** cpu **757 passed**, 2 skipped, 88 deselected, 147 s; store integration **80 passed**, 1 deselected, 66 s; gpu `test_staged_run.py` 1 passed; ruff, mypy (110 files), lint-imports clean.
- **Re-review: I-1, I-2, I-3 all ADDRESSED**, no new Critical/Important; the reviewer re-ran the cpu unit subset (122 passed) and `tests/integration/test_store.py` against the live Postgres (18 passed), and checked that single-transaction semantics hold under stage-major order (one connection, one commit or rollback, per-unit savepoints strictly sequential).
- **Source:** `notes/2.Wserve.md` (Fix round 1); `reports/2.Wserve-review.md`, `reports/2.Wserve-rereview.md`, `reports/2.Wserve-report.md` (Fix round 1); `progress.md` W-serve entries; commit `dff0f7f`.

### 2026-10-16 · W-serve The extraction runaway: a loop inside valid JSON, not whitespace

- **Hypothesis carried from 2.1:** about 5 % of scenes "emit whitespace until max_tokens"; W-int added xgrammar's `disable_any_whitespace` on that basis ([B-287](decisions.md#b-287--vllm-structured-outputs-run-with-xgrammar-and-disable_any_whitespace)). Runs 2 and 3 still lost 2 of 25 scenes to the 2,048-token limit.
- **No raw output had been saved** (`knowledge.events.raw.jsonl` holds only successful scenes; the work_unit error is instructor's message). **Reproduction:** a scratch `runaway.py` piped into ray-head (output `data/wserve/runaway.json`) deployed text_llm through `serving.group` (load 159.9 s, unload 4.0 s, VRAM back to 455 MiB), called the shipped `extract.extract_scene` on `sc/7` and `sc/25`, and dumped instructor's `last_completion` on failure.
- **`S01E01/sc/7`** (4 script lines, prompt 4,008 chars): `finish_reason=length`, `completion_tokens=2048`, 5,747 chars of JSON valid up to the cut; **42 events, only 7 distinct; 40 are `wearing` with `confidence: 0.0`**, all quoting the same line ("Rui and Sasha share a thermos"), cycling through subject × object pairs (`rui/ada`, `sasha/rui`, `rui/rui`, `sasha/sasha`, …) until max_tokens. **Cause: an enumeration/repetition loop inside a schema-valid array**; the grammar allows an unbounded `events` array and zero-confidence events, so removing whitespace cannot stop it.
- **`S01E01/sc/25`** (3 lines) **succeeded in isolation with 37 events** (itself a sign of the same enumeration); it failed in runs 2 and 3, where it came right after `sc/24`. At T = 0 vLLM output is not invariant to batch or prefix cache, so whether the loop reaches 2,048 tokens depends on what ran before (the agent's explanation, not separately tested).
- **Not fixed here** (all candidates are in 2.1B's files): `maxItems` on `events` in `extract.event_schema` (xgrammar would enforce it), forbidding `confidence: 0.0` events, or `frequency_penalty` / `repetition_penalty` in `LLMClient`. Handed to 2.1B with the evidence; cost at the time about 89 to 110 s of GPU per runaway scene, each a recorded gap ([C-49](experiments.md#c-49--does-xgrammars-disable_any_whitespace-stop-the-extraction-runaway), [B-303](decisions.md#b-303--the-extraction-runaway-and-single-stream-throughput-are-handed-on-not-fixed-in-w-serve)).
- **Source:** `notes/2.Wserve.md` (Runaway extraction: the cause); `reports/2.Wserve-report.md` (Fix round 1); `progress.md` ("Runaway cause: model loops inside valid JSON (40× `wearing` conf 0.0) → handed to 2.1B").

### 2026-10-16 · 2.2B An ambiguous-transitions variant of the synthetic show, with exact truth

- **Why:** 2.2 showed the synthetic show has no transition the next-day default gets wrong (rules only 0.9998, rules + bible 1.0000), so EXP-007 could not separate its arms. Controller ruling: add a variant that can ([B-191](decisions.md#b-191--an-ambiguous-transitions-synthetic-variant-so-exp-007-can-discriminate)).
- **What** (commit `11354bc`): `eval/synthetic/ambiguous.py`, reached through `build(seed, ambiguous=True)`; `build(seed)` is unchanged (a test compares `truth()` of `build(1)` and `build(1, ambiguous=False)`). `ambiguate(show, seed)` rewrites a generated show and recomputes the truth (`story_day`, `story_seq`, `marker`), deterministic per seed (`random.Random("synthetic-ambiguous/<seed>")`). Story time is moved out of everything the rules read (heading, previous scene's transitions, all-caps opening super) into ordinary prose ([B-193](decisions.md#b-193--how-the-variant-hides-story-time-prose-cues-a-regressing-heading-silent-cases-kept)). Seed 1: 38 `loose_next_day`, 4 `later_unstated`, 8 `flashback_unmarked`, 6 `simultaneous_unmarked` scenes (table in [EXP-007](experiments.md#exp-007--story-order-rules-only-vs-rules--llm)).
- **Silent cases:** a scene whose heading cannot go back (the previous time of day is already the first one) is marked `silent`; the rules see no ambiguity and never ask. Seed 1 none; seed 2: 2 simultaneous + 2 flashback; seed 3: 2 simultaneous.
- **Solvability check:** a regex "cue reader" stub (reads "<N> years ago" and "at the same moment|meanwhile" from the second scene) takes seed 1 to tau 1.0 and flashback and simultaneous recall 1.0, so the variant can be ordered from the text alone; on seed 3 (silent cases) it stays below 1.0.
- **Rules only on the ambiguous show:** tau 0.915 / 0.916 / 0.906 (seeds 1/2/3) in a probe before the two dead-memory scenes were added (not logged); 0.88 / 0.87 / 0.86 in the logged runs (the review reads MLflow as 0.877 / 0.871 / 0.863).
- **Tried → switched:** N up to 14 years first; the cue reader's `Verdict(days=365*N)` failed validation above 3650 (`Verdict.days` is capped at ten years), so N is 3..10. The first pick of the two "memories of the dead" used a dict keyed by scene, which merged two picks into one scene (7 flashbacks on seed 2 instead of 8); now sampled over distinct scenes.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/eval/test_exp007.py`, 6 (determinism and unchanged default build; scripts pass `parse_fountain` with no warnings; facts' quotes are substrings of their lines and mentions equal `find_mentions`; `eval.synthetic.rules.violations` is `[]` on the ambiguous show; the rules miss the cues; the prose-cue reader solves the variant; truth arm = 1.0).
- **Source:** `notes/2.2B.md`, `reports/2.2B-report.md`; commit `11354bc`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.7 Clearance matching, occurrences, the clearance.ocr stage and the CSV

- **What:** package `src/nip/agents/clearance/`, taking over from 2.6. Commit `4c131c1` (first cut, 19 files), then `2407222`, `e464917` on 2026-10-17 (claims, scanner rules, CSV ids, e2e modes).
  - `matching.py`: `normalize(check, text)` (phones to digits with the leading US 1 dropped; URLs without scheme, `www.` and trailing slash; e-mails case-folded; plates alphanumerics upper-cased; names case-folded with street and legal-form abbreviations expanded or dropped and a leading "the" dropped) and `match(registry, check, text, episode) -> Match(status, item)` with architecture.md's five statuses. Phones, plates, e-mails and URLs match **exactly**; names (address, brand) by token containment (>= 2 tokens) or `rapidfuzz.ratio >= 90`. An item whose `scope` does not cover the episode does not apply. Several matching items: the strictest status wins (denied > pending > cleared). Config `config/clearance/matching.yaml` ([B-243](decisions.md#b-243--identifiers-match-exactly-names-fuzzily-the-strictest-status-wins)).
  - `occurrences.py`: `Hit` (one typed candidate at one keyframe) → `Occurrence`s. Detections of one item merge when their keyframe cells are at most `occurrence_gap_s` = 2 s apart (inclusive) ([B-246](decisions.md#b-246--occurrences-merge-by-a-2-s-gap-between-keyframe-cells-scene-boundaries-are-not-used)). Prominence = 0.30 area + 0.15 centrality + 0.15 sharpness + 0.40 duration (each 0 to 1 by a reference value), `featured` >= 0.5. Severity: denied 5, pending 4 whatever the prominence; otherwise featured 4 / background 2. Config `pipeline.yaml` `clearance:`.
  - `checks.py`: six `Check` classes (`ClrPhone` ... `ClrBrandText`) sharing `ClearanceText`; one flag per occurrence; they read only the store (`detection` rows, `clearance_item`, bible entities).
  - `stage.py`: pipeline stage `clearance.ocr`: keyframe images from object storage → OCR reader → `TextScanner` → `detection` rows + crops, one work unit per keyframe.
  - `report.py`: the clearance report CSV in api-contracts column order, the `clearance.csv` stage (run artifact) and the Typer sub-app `nip clearance report` ([B-258](decisions.md#b-258--clearance-csv-per-run-and-episode-cli-now-api-route-later)).
  - `agents/base.py`: `Finding.tier` override, so pending/denied are `must_review` and cleared `suppressed` whatever the confidence ([B-244](decisions.md#b-244--pendingdenied-are-must_review-and-cleared-suppressed-whatever-the-confidence)).
  - 2.6's `ocr_text.py` was extended (person/role filter, credits-card rule, 3-letter brand rule, deny-listed addresses). The first notes said it was untouched; review minor M1 caught that and the line was corrected.
- **Problem 1: a claim against a shot cannot score against a 3 s overlay.** The brief said "claim = occurrence's shot span(s)". `eval.metrics` needs IoU >= 0.3 between a claim's frames and the label's, and 2.9A's overlays are 72 to 96 frames (3 to 4 s) in low-motion stretches, which in these films sit in long shots. Measured on the seed-1 injections: shot length containing a labelled item, Sintel 4.1, 14.8, 24.0, 10.8, 7.0, 8.3, 3.5 (positives) and 12.3 s; Tears of Steel 15.2, 3.5, 5.5, 5.0, 7.9, 9.7, 20.8, 3.8 s. A 12 s shot against a 3.3 s label is IoU 0.28. The report says shot claims cap recall at 20/32 on these labels (the notes say "~0"; see HANDOVER). **Switched to** a keyframe span standing for its cell (the stretch of its shot nearer to it than to any other keyframe, capped at `cell_cap_s` = 4 s) as the claim, shots as context ([B-247](decisions.md#b-247--a-keyframe-span-stands-for-its-cell-the-flags-claim-is-the-keyframes-superseded-by-b-248), later superseded by the occurrence-range claim [B-248](decisions.md#b-248--the-claim-is-the-occurrences-frame-range-as-a-clrocc-hash-span); [C-36](experiments.md#c-36--claim-span-for-an-occurrence-shot-span-vs-keyframe-cell-vs-occurrence-range)).
- **Problem 2: Presidio's own score made every unlogged phone number `suppressed`.** Phone and plate candidates get recognizer score 0.4 (a loose regex; libphonenumber validates the number), so confidence = OCR confidence x 0.4 = 0.38, below the 0.70 tier line: a real, unlogged phone number on screen would never be shown (the docs example is conf 0.96, `must_review`). Real scores seen on Sintel seed 1: plate 0.40, phone 0.40, address 0.62 to 0.73, brand (deny-list) 0.85, URL 0.80, e-mail 0.99 to 1.0. **Fix:** `confidence = OCR confidence x max(recognizer score, certainty_floor[check])`, floors phone 0.9 / e-mail 0.9 / URL 0.85; plates and GLiNER spans keep their score ([B-245](decisions.md#b-245--candidate-confidence--ocr-confidence-x-maxrecognizer-score-certainty-floor)). Not exercised by the benchmark (every injected positive is in the clearance log, so its tier is forced).
- **Smaller findings while building:**
  1. `recheck` returned on the first occurrence sharing the keyframe: a pending and a cleared number on one keyframe made the cleared flag fail its re-check and be dropped (the integration test caught it). Now `any(...)` over keyframe + quote + item.
  2. Work-unit keys carry the occurrence's first frame (`.../f<frame>`), not the shot: the same item twice in one long shot would collide.
  3. The Laplacian sharpness has no column in `detection`; it is stored as a companion row ([B-259](decisions.md#b-259--crop-sharpness-is-stored-as-a-companion-detection-row)).
  4. Known at this point: two unknown items of one check on one keyframe share an R13 fingerprint. The implementer called it rare; the review measured 38 % ([C-38](experiments.md#c-38--fingerprint-collisions-among-clr-flags-41-of-109-per-seed--0-of-253)).
- **Tests (round 0):** CPU profile + Postgres integration 900 passed, 7 skipped, 3 deselected. New: `test_matching.py`, `test_occurrences.py` (exactly 2 s merges, +1 frame splits), `test_clearance_report.py` (routing per status, pending/denied never hidden at confidences 0.0 to 1.0, CSV round trip), `test_matching_person_filter.py`, `tests/integration/test_clearance_report_pg.py` (ingest → stage → checks → flags → crops → CSV artifact → CLI). ruff clean; mypy clean on 2.7's files (1 error in 2.1B's `exp020_rules.py`); lint-imports 7 kept.
- **Source:** `notes/2.7.md` (journal, smaller findings), `reports/2.7-report.md`; commits `4c131c1`, `2407222`, `e464917`.

### 2026-10-16 · 2.1B Design: a cap on events per scene line and argument kinds per predicate

- **Starting point:** W-serve's finding (scene 7: 42 events, 7 distinct, 40 `wearing` at confidence 0.0 until the 2,048-token cap; scene 25 nondeterministic) and EXP-006's 93 % of hallucinated events kept.
- **Options from the brief, and what was done** ([B-165](decisions.md#b-165--runaway-guard-at-most-4-events-per-scene-line-enforced-by-the-grammar)): `maxItems` on `events` was **shipped** as `EVENTS_PER_LINE = 4` × the scene's line count (the largest legitimate reply in 2.1's 1,217 saved events was 3.5 events per line, 7 events on a 2-line scene; the looping scene reached 10.5 per line); `confidence` minimum > 0 in the schema was **not done** (a constraint pydantic checks but the grammar might not would turn a whole reply into a repair, then a failed unit), so zero-confidence events are dropped after the call instead; a repetition or frequency penalty was **not tried** (it penalises the JSON keys every event repeats, and the cap already bounds the loop).
- **Argument type checks** (EXP-006 proposal 8, [B-167](decisions.md#b-167--post-model-drops-zero-confidence-duplicates-argument-kinds-per-predicate)): `entities.ARG_KINDS` lists per predicate the allowed subject and object kinds, taken from the prompt's own predicate definitions (data-model.md lists the predicates but not their argument kinds): present_at character → location; holds/owns/wearing character → prop|product|brand; state character|prop|product|location; learns/references character → secret; relationship character → character (not itself); setup prop|product|brand; payoff thing → character; decision character; mentions character → org|brand|product|person|term. A required object must be there. `Resolver` gained `kinds`, `lookup`, `typed` and `ensure`, so `save_events` looks both arguments up, checks, and only then creates candidates: a dropped event no longer mints candidate entities. Each raw event carries `dropped` = None | quote | zero confidence | duplicate | argument type.
- **First re-score on the 2.1 raw events (no model calls):** `exp020_resolution extract --variant enum|free-text --replay` (pre-2.1B annotated files kept as `events.2.1.jsonl`). Enum arm, 564 raw events: argument type 164 (holds 103, learns 23, references 14, mentions 11, present_at 5, state 5, wearing 3), zero confidence 41, duplicate 3; 356 kept. Free-text arm, 653: argument type 27, duplicate 1; 625 kept. Results in [EXP-006](experiments.md#exp-006--grounding-validator-threshold) and [EXP-020](experiments.md#exp-020--entity-resolution-enum-constrained-ids-vs-fuzzy-matching-afterwards).
- **Source:** `notes/2.1B.md` (2.1B design, EXP-006 follow-up); `briefs/2.1B-brief.md`.

### 2026-10-17 · 2.2B EXP-007 runs: prompt v1 makes things worse, prompt v2 +30 points on templated cues

- **Harness** (`eval/exp/exp007_story.py`, commit `11354bc`; the notes date this work 2026-10-16/17): `prepare` (both variants x seeds 1-3, the four 2.9A plot-L1 injectors stacked as `nip eval inject` does, labels), `derive --arm rules|bible|llm|llm-v2`, `up` / `down` (Serve in ray-head), `score` (Postgres + MLflow). The fact set is fixed: oracle facts from `truth.json` via 2.3's `layer1_oracle.consolidate`, identical in every arm, so 2.1B's concurrent extraction changes cannot move the experiment; only `scene.story_seq`, `markers`, `story_inferred` differ between arms ([B-192](decisions.md#b-192--exp-007-holds-the-facts-fixed-oracle-facts-only-the-story-columns-vary-between-arms)). Arms: `rules`, `rules+llm` (prompt v1), `rules+llm-v2`, `rules+bible` (reference), `truth` (sanity: 12/12 flags and 1.00 everywhere on both variants).
- **Real model:** qwen3.5-4b-awq (`cyankiwi/Qwen3.5-4B-AWQ-4bit`) in the `ray-head` container, `NIP_VRAM_MARGIN_GB=0.25`, Plan 16384 context, gmu 0.79, `--enforce-eager`, xgrammar; host-side `LLMClient` at `NIP_SERVE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8001`; `data/gpu.lock` held while the model was up.
- **Session 1 (prompt v1, not timestamped):** `up` 8 m 35 s wall (load 469.9 s; idle 1102 MiB, 7371 MiB with the model up); **158 LLM calls** over the three ambiguous shows in 6 m 29 s (median 2.45 s per call, 385 s summed); the base shows had nothing to ask.
- **Prompt v1 read "the time of day goes backwards" as `earlier`.** Of the 158 verdicts: 22 of 22 unmarked flashbacks right, but **84 present scenes called `earlier`** (32 + 18 `loose_next_day` scenes, 34 follow-ups) and 1 of 16 asked simultaneous scenes right (13 `earlier`, 2 `next_day`). Reasons were confabulated ("the day before the first scene, as indicated by the character asking if Ines slept") with `days` 1. Verdict accuracy **0.29** (per seed 0.47 / 0.20 / 0.19). A misread cascades: after an `earlier` verdict the day counter does not move (by design), so the next scene of the same morning again looks earlier and is asked again: asks per show **36 / 59 / 63** against the rules' 26 / 23 / 26.
- **`down` never tore the model down** (first version, same pattern as `exp020_resolution.down`): it re-planned to rebuild the app list, the planner no longer fit the model with it loaded, the list was empty, nothing was deleted, and `teardown` waited 120 s and raised "VRAM used 7822 MiB after teardown, idle was 1102" (twice). Session 1 ended by stopping the container (VRAM 578 MiB after). Fix (`23f13d4`): delete every app from `serve.status().applications` and wait for idle; measured 18.9 s wall, VRAM 7651 → 343 MiB ([C-28](experiments.md#c-28--experiment-teardown-delete-by-re-planning-vs-delete-by-serve-status), [B-197](decisions.md#b-197--the-experiments-down-deletes-serve-apps-by-status-under-the-gpu-lock)).
- **Prompt v2** (`config/prompts/knowledge.story.v2.md`, one version, no iteration after it ran): a regressing time of day normally means the next day, not `earlier`; `earlier`, `simultaneous`, `later_days` only when the second scene's own text says so; check in that order; default `next_day` ([B-194](decisions.md#b-194--prompt-v2-a-regressing-time-of-day-is-normally-the-next-day)).
- **Session 2 (prompt v2):** 2026-10-17 01:03:41 → 01:29:59, after waiting about two hours behind 2.1B's lock; load 384.2 s (idle 812 MiB, 7558 MiB up); **78 calls**, median **7.15 s** per call, 548 s summed, although v2's replies were shorter (72 vs 110 characters of `reason`) and its prompt 15% longer (1955 vs 1696 characters). Per-call time was about 3x session 1's with the same model and arguments; cause not investigated (the ray-head container had been started by 2.1B two hours earlier; GPU clocks and desktop load not recorded). Read the cost as **2.4 to 7 s per ambiguous transition**.
- **v2 verdicts:** 25/28, 19/24, 20/26 right (0.893 / 0.792 / 0.769, recomputed by the reviewer). The 14 wrong: 8 `same_day` truths answered `next_day` (no order effect) and 6 `simultaneous` truths answered `next_day` (5) or `same_day` (1) (lost for `two_places`).
- **Results** (3 seeds; [EXP-007](experiments.md#exp-007--story-order-rules-only-vs-rules--llm)): pooled precision of `plot.dead_acts` + `plot.knowledge_order` on the ambiguous variant **0.60 (rules) → 0.78 (v1) → 0.90 (v2)**, bible 1.00; tau 0.870 → 0.892 → 0.983; planted `dead_acts` recall 1.00 → **0.56** with v1, 1.00 with v2; `two_places` found 0 / 1 / 2 of 9. Base variant: 0 ambiguous transitions, 0 calls, every arm 1.00 (tau 0.9998).
- **Other costs:** `story.derive` alone 2-17 ms per show on the rules arms; `score` about 17 s per (variant, seed, arm). Report: 60 arm runs scored, 0 failures.
- **Tests:** `NIP_PROFILE=cpu pytest tests/unit/eval` 122 passed (before the last small edit), then `test_exp007.py` + `test_synthetic.py` 16 passed; ruff and mypy clean on its files.
- **Hand-off concerns** (DONE_WITH_CONCERNS): `story.PROMPT` still named v1 (`story.py` is 2.2's file); the ambiguity is templated by construction; `exp020_resolution.down` (2.1B's file) has the same teardown flaw; per-call time varied 3x.
- **Source:** `notes/2.2B.md`, `reports/2.2B-report.md`; commits `11354bc`, `23f13d4`; MLflow `sqlite:///data/mlflow/mlflow.db`, experiment `exp007-story`, tag `exp=EXP-007`; tables in `data/exp007/compare_all.md` (git-ignored).

### 2026-10-17 · 2.2B Review: "held out" was overstated; the controller switches `story.PROMPT` to v2

- **Controller ruling** ([B-195](decisions.md#b-195--storyprompt-is-v2)): switch `story.PROMPT` to v2; 2.2B may make that one-line edit in 2.2's file. The ledger's evidence line reads "+30 pts precision on held-out seeds, v1 creates false flashbacks"; cost if wrong: v2 overfit to templated ambiguity.
- **Review** (`2.2B-review.md`): spec ✅, quality good, approve with minors; 0 Critical, 1 Important, 6 Minor. The reviewer recomputed every per-seed number from the 30 active MLflow runs (42 deleted runs are superseded reruns; exactly 2 variants x 5 arms x 3 seeds active) and ran `test_exp007.py` + `test_synthetic.py` (16 passed) ([review-findings](review-findings.md#22b-exp-007-ambiguous-transitions-variant-and-harness)).
  - **I1, "held out" overstates independence.** v2's file mtime is about 4 minutes after v1's verdicts existed for all three seeds, and the failure analysis the prompt answers ("84 present scenes called earlier", "1 of 16 simultaneous right") is aggregated over the three seeds, so "written from seed-1 verdicts" cannot be verified from the artifacts. Independently, v2's example cues ("eight years ago", "at the same moment", "meanwhile") are the generator's own templates, identical across seeds, so seeds 2-3 test sampling variability only, not wording. And the regex cue reader already reaches tau 1.0 on seed 1, so this variant cannot show the LLM beating a regex. Seeds 2+3 alone still clear the bar: rules 12/20 = 0.60, v2 12/14 = 0.857 (+25.7 points); seed 2 alone 0.60 → 0.75, seed 3 0.60 → 1.00; tau 0.867 → 0.976.
- **Fixed in `6dc6ba8`:** I1: the notes' Takeaway and Decision drop "held out" and state that v2 was written after v1's runs on generator-templated cues, so +26 to +30 points is a ceiling for explicit cues on this generator and real-script generalisation is untested. Ruling applied: `src/nip/knowledge/story.py:57` `PROMPT = "knowledge.story.v2.md"` (and the `Verdict` docstring); no test pinned v1. **M5:** `score` logs `story_prompt=knowledge.story.<v>` as an MLflow param for llm arms (past runs lack it); `derive --arm llm` pins v1 explicitly. **M3:** `down`'s docstring says it deletes all Serve apps and must run only under `data/gpu.lock`.
- **Counts:** `NIP_PROFILE=cpu pytest tests/unit/knowledge/test_story.py tests/unit/eval/test_exp007.py` 45 passed; ruff and mypy clean.
- **Re-review** (`2.2B-rereview.md`): I1, M5 and M3 ADDRESSED; v2 has the identical `$` fields as v1 (`following`, `next_scene`, `prev_day`, `prev_scene`, `prev_tod`, `transition`) and its path resolves; the prompt-content test at `test_story.py:323-325` passes on v2. No new breakage; minor: no test pins `story.PROMPT` itself.
- **Source:** `reports/2.2B-review.md`, `reports/2.2B-report.md` (Fix round 1), `reports/2.2B-rereview.md`, `notes/2.2B.md` (Fix round 1); `progress.md` lines 210-215.

### 2026-10-17 · 2.7 Running the experiments with the GPU held by other agents

- **GPU contention:** 2.1B and 2.2B jobs held `data/gpu.lock` from 22:42 (2026-10-16) to 06:12 with only seconds between holders; waiters polling every 60 s never got it. Workarounds: the EXP-008 reads ran on the CPU (RapidOCR 0.8 kf/s alone, 2 to 6 s per frame under load: 4,374 frames in about 3.5 h wall); polling at 15 s (`GPU_LOCK_POLL_S`) won the lock at 06:12; one driver (`exp017_session.py`, commit `e778928`) did the EXP-017 vision-model arm and the OCR GPU timing in a single hold (06:14 to 07:10, 56 min).
- **Config edited under a running job:** a scan re-read `config/clearance/recognizers.yaml` when it built the next film's `TextScanner`, after the PERSON label had been added: `KeyError: 'PERSON'` at Sintel seed 2, frame 500. Restarted seeds 2 to 3 with the new code; cached scans resume. Lesson recorded by the agent: do not edit config a running job reads.
- **Stale lock:** the agent killed its own lock waiter after it had acquired the lock, leaving `data/gpu.lock` owned by 2.7 with no process. Found from `owner.txt`, GPU checked idle (707 MiB), removed with `rmdir`; the next driver took it 14 s later.
- **Formatting sweep:** `ruff format eval/exp` reformatted the whole directory once, including 2.1/2.1B's uncommitted `exp006_grounding.py` and `exp020_resolution.py` (formatting only); disclosed (review M8).
- **Stub-profile runs unaffected:** the stub-profile `nip run` builds a `TextScanner` (5 s, spaCy) only when an episode has stored keyframe images and an `ocr` decision, so the CPU-profile store tests are unaffected.
- **Source:** `notes/2.7.md` (operations log), `reports/2.7-report.md` concern 5.

### 2026-10-17 · 2.7 EXP-008 keyframe density: the bar picks 1 per shot, the recall target does not

- **Set and method:** the two films' `clr.text.v1` injections (`nip eval inject --set mvp --seed N --only clr`), seeds 1, 2, 3; 32 labelled pending/denied items (10 / 11 / 11), 48 rendered positives including cleared ones. Three nested frame sets read once each; everything after the read is production code (`stage.scan_image`, the six checks, `verify`), scored by `nip eval run`, tables from `nip eval compare --exp EXP-008 --md`. Full entry: [EXP-008](experiments.md#exp-008--keyframe-density-for-on-screen-text).
- **Result:** shown recall 1 per shot **0.50 ± 0.12**, current policy (1 per shot + 1 per 2 s in shots > 4 s) **1.00 ± 0.00**, 1 per 1 s **1.00 ± 0.00**; keyframes 302 / 723 / 1,458 (11.17 / 26.74 / 53.93 per minute).
- **The bar, read twice:** the agent first read "a denser policy is kept only if recall rises >= 5 points for <= 50 % more keyframes" against the current policy, so the dense arm (+0.00 for +101.7 %) was rejected and 4 s / 2 s "kept per the bar". The reviewer showed the text supports comparing against the sparsest arm: current vs 1 per shot is +0.50 recall for +139 % keyframes, which fails the cost clause, so the bar selects **1 per shot**. 1 per shot's recall (0.50) fails the 0.85 MVP recall target of the six `clr.*` checks. **Controller ruling:** keep 4 s / 2 s as a recorded override of the bar ([B-251](decisions.md#b-251--exp-008-keep-the-4-s--2-s-keyframe-policy-as-a-recorded-override-of-the-bar)); the "per the bar" wording was withdrawn and `config/pipeline.yaml`'s comment rewritten in `f324627`.
- **Caveats the agent recorded:** the set favours density (3 to 4 s overlays in long shots; a real storefront sign usually stays for the whole shot); the reads were CPU RapidOCR under heavy machine load, so the `read_s_per_content_hour` rows (837.7 / 3,558.8 / 7,185.8 s) are CPU wall time under load, not a GPU cost; the scanner rules were written while looking at seed-1 flags and the credits are identical in all seeds.
- **Source:** `notes/2.7.md` (EXP-008 Results, DP-2.7-9, Fix round 1 I1), `reports/2.7-review.md` (answer 1), `data/exp008/compare-exp008-r2.md`; commits `e464917`, `f324627`.

- **Arms:** `pp-ocrv6-medium` (RapidOCR PP-OCRv6 medium, 2.6's fallback; detection on GPU, recognition on CPU) vs `qwen3.5-4b-awq` (vLLM in ray-head through `serving.group`, structured output `{"lines": [{"text", "box" 0-1000 grid}]}`, T=0, thinking off, `max_tokens` 600, 8 requests at once, constant line confidence 0.9). - **Result:** recall 1.00 for both (32 labels and all 48 rendered positives); on the overlay frames the vision model read the exact injected text on 106 of 107 keyframes (1 failed read), PP-OCR on 107 of 107. Bar: keep PP-OCR unless the vision model's recall is >= 10 points higher: +0.00 → **keep PP-OCR** ([B-252](decisions.md#b-252--exp-017-keep-the-dedicated-ocr-engine-pp-ocrv6-medium-via-rapidocr)). Stage seconds per content hour 1,906.62 (vision model) vs 1,470.49 (OCR), 1.30x.

After fix round 2, with the 12 native URL labels counted, PP-OCR's precision is higher (0.44 vs 0.35): the vision model found 1 of the 12 native URLs, PP-OCR 12. Full entry: [EXP-017](experiments.md#exp-017--ocr-engine-vs-reading-text-with-the-vision-model).

"Pending/denied detected but not shown": the 32 pending/denied flags (17 + 15, one per label) all `must_review`, the 16 cleared all `suppressed`, 0 hidden.

Scanner rules took shown FPs on seed 1 from 95 → 29 (Sintel) and 89 → 24 (Tears of Steel) ([C-35](experiments.md#c-35--scanner-rules-on-unlogged-credits-text-shown-false-positives-95--29-and-89--24), [B-253](decisions.md#b-253--credits-and-title-cards-give-no-gliner-brand-or-address-guesses)).

### 2026-10-17 · 2.1B GPU sessions: CPU contention, 30-minute timeouts, a second failure class and a leaked lock

- **Sessions** (all under `data/gpu.lock`, released between sessions; times as in the notes):

| Session | Lock held | What | Deployment |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 23:02 → 01:03 | runaway `before` (2.1's schema, stopped after S01E01–E03) + `cap` (cap only, 150 scenes) | default KV, 8k; fresh container: load **860.5 s** |
| 2 | 01:30 → 02:38 | `shipped` schema: enum seed-1 clean (recorded into `tests/fixtures/llm`), free-text seed-1 clean, both arms' 12 injected seed-1 scenes | default KV, 8k; load 373.6 s |
| EXP-018 round 1 | 02:39 → 02:58 | 4 start trials | per arm |
| 3a / 3b | 02:58 → 03:12 / 03:12 → 03:51 | fp8-8k and fp8-16k fact passes (enum seed-1 clean) | FP8 KV |
| (leak) | 03:54 → 06:11 | lock held by a crashed EXP-018 driver, no model up | — |
| 4a | 07:12 → 08:24 | seeds 2–3, both arms, clean + injected (648 calls, 0 failed) | default KV, 8k; load about 5 min |
| EXP-018 rounds 2–10 | 09:12 → 10:51, released after every round (+150 s gap) | 36 start trials | per arm |

- Extraction passes ran 4 calls in flight (`exp020_rules extract --concurrency 4`); the shipped stage sends one at a time. The same concurrency was used for every pass compared.
- **Throughput collapsed under CPU contention.** In session 1 vLLM generated 8 to 15 tok/s in total with 4 requests running, and an unconstrained 128-token request sent into that batch took 38.3 s (3.3 tok/s), so every step was slow, not only the grammar-constrained ones. Host CPU was at 94 to 98 % (`Get-Counter`); 2.7's `eval.exp.exp008_keyframes scan --cpu` held about 44 % of all cores. Pass wall times therefore range from 627 s (fp8-8k, quiet host) to 2,348 s (default-8k, contended) for the same 150 calls and are **not comparable between arms**; no timing claim is made from them.
- **Runaway scenes became 30-minute timeouts.** At about 3 tok/s per stream a 2,048-token loop needs about 680 s, more than `LLMClient`'s 600 s timeout, and the OpenAI SDK's default `max_retries=2` re-sent the deterministic request twice: 3 scenes took 1,801 to 1,803 s each (3 × 600 s). Fixed with `OpenAI(..., max_retries=0)`; instructor's one repair stays ([B-168](decisions.md#b-168--the-openai-sdk-under-instructor-runs-with-max_retries0)). Only the `before` pass ran with the old setting.
- **A second failure class: "line 2 is not a line of this scene ([1])".** The `cap` pass still failed 2/150 scenes (S01E02/sc/16, S01E03/sc/24), and the repair call repeated the error. Shipped fix: `line` is a `Literal` of the scene's line numbers, so the grammar cannot emit another number; the shipped pass then had 0/150. The review later showed both scenes were a rendering bug, not the model ([B-166](decisions.md#b-166--line-is-an-enum-of-the-scenes-line-numbers)).
- **The grammar cap works but degrades the last item:** a probe with `maxItems: 3` asking for ten fruits returned `["Apple", "Banana", "Orange\", "]` with `finish_reason=stop`. In extraction the capped scenes are the looping ones, and their surplus is confidence-0 or mistyped and dropped.
- **Recordings re-made:** 2.1's 142 recordings (keyed by the old schema) and the 148 recorded by the cap-only pass were deleted; the 150 of the shipped enum pass replace them. The cpu-profile integration test now replays 68 kept S01E01 events (was 75: same model, new drops).
- **`down` and a leaked lock.** `exp020_resolution down` re-planned with the model loaded and deleted nothing (2.2B's finding in `23f13d4`); it now deletes every Serve app by status and waits for VRAM, and the EXP-018 driver calls `down` after every round. That `finally` crashed: `subprocess.run(text=True)` decoded vLLM's UTF-8 output as cp1252 (`UnicodeDecodeError` → `stdout=None` → `TypeError`), so `release()` never ran and **the lock stayed held from 03:54 to 06:11 with no model loaded** (the API rate-limit pause hid it; ledger rate limit #5 at about 06:10). Other agents lost that window. Fixed in `dc4e6fc`: UTF-8 decoding with `errors="replace"` and `release()` in its own `finally`.
- **The desktop took 2.3 GB.** At 03:51 the device sat at 2,838 to 3,069 MiB used with no Serve app and no vLLM process in ray-head (only Windows desktop processes in `nvidia-smi`); `down` waited 120 s and raised, the next `up` skipped text_llm, and EXP-018 round 2 recorded nothing (the planner refused every arm). Fixed in `bb68eac`: the driver releases the lock and retries every 5 min while the device holds more than 1,500 MiB, and waits 150 s after each release so the 60 s pollers of other agents get a turn.
- **Tests and lint** (07:15): `NIP_PROFILE=cpu uv run pytest -m "not gpu and not integration"` **818 passed, 2 skipped** (ray), 90 deselected (an earlier run failed only 2.7's in-progress `test_cli` dry-run test, later fixed by 2.7); integration (`test_knowledge`, `test_store`, `test_layer1_oracle`) **22 passed**, now asserting the drop reasons (a mistyped `holds maya → ada`, a confidence-0 `wearing`, a repeated event, a fabricated quote); new unit tests `test_argument_kinds_must_fit_the_predicate` and `test_events_are_capped_per_scene_line` (cap 8 on a 2-line scene, `line` enum [1, 2]); ruff clean, `mypy src` clean (82 files).
- **Commits:** `b8d320b` (fix, checks, re-recorded fixtures, harnesses), `bbf653a` (harness `down` by Serve status, runaway table, secret-in-scene variant), `dc4e6fc`, `bb68eac`.
- **Source:** `notes/2.1B.md` (GPU sessions, Broke and fixed, Tests), `reports/2.1B-report.md`.
