### 2026-10-17 · 2.1B Review: two paragraphs under one line number, the cause behind zero prop recall

- **Review** (`2.1B-review.md`): spec pass with ⚠️ on two decision records, quality acceptable with three Important follow-ups; **0 Critical, 3 Important, 9 Minor**. The reviewer traced every headline number to an active MLflow run (read-only sqlite), re-derived the EXP-006 counts from the replayed `events.jsonl` (hallucinated kept 0.2733 = 41/150, true dropped 0.0067 = 1/150) and re-ran `pytest tests/unit/knowledge` (78 passed).
- **Does the runaway guard drop real facts?** Measured by the reviewer on the shipped-pass events against truth: zero-confidence and duplicate drops removed **0** events matching a truth (line, predicate, subject); argument-type drops removed 11 / 16 / 6 such events (enum, seeds 1–3) and every one has a wrong object (`holds leo → ada` for truth `holds leo → prop_key`, `present_at orin → tomas`), so no true fact was lost. The recall effect of the schema change itself (cap, line enum) was not measured (M8).
- **I1, the rendering bug.** The Fountain reader joins consecutive action paragraphs into one `script_line` with `\n\n`; `render` printed the text verbatim, so the second paragraph sat unnumbered under `[1]` and the model cited it as `[2]`. On seed-1 clean all 18 truth `holds`, 3 `setup`, 3 `payoff` and 3 of 5 `state` facts sit in such a second paragraph; all 20 free-text prop `holds` ("Dov checks the signal flare again.", "Ines picks up the blue folder", …) cite line `.2` with the quote of line `.1`'s second paragraph and were dropped as `quote` (scores 26–41). Both "line outside the scene" failures of the `cap` pass were the same bug on one-line scenes, so the line enum treated a symptom. The two_places lines name no place, so a quoted `present_at` cannot come from them: a design gap. Consequence: prop_provenance recall 0 in both arms was at least largely a 2.1 rendering bug, and the EXP-020 rule half compared two arms handicapped by the same bug.
- **I2:** a candidate's guessed kind (`state` subject → character, `references` object → secret, `mentions` object → org) became a hard type constraint for every later event, so a prop first met as a `state` subject would drop every later `holds X → prop` (free-text seeds 1–3 clean: `state 'tide tables'` ×4 and `references … → tide tables` ×12 dropped as argument type).
- **I3:** EXP-020 "done, keep the enum" had no discriminating evidence (rule precision 1 true flag in 51 vs 0 in 81, both at the floor; enum accuracy 1.00 partly by construction; 0 alias-only arguments). EXP-018's "16k misses the bar (−0.023)" applied the bar to a difference at the width of the single-pass noise (M4).
- **Then the build stopped:** the ledger records a user stop on 2026-10-17 with 2.1B in its fix round; ray-head (holding vLLM, about 7.9 GB VRAM) was stopped and the stale 2.1B lock released by the controller. The fix round resumed on 2026-10-18.
- **Source:** `reports/2.1B-review.md`; ledger `progress.md` (2.1B review entry, USER STOP 2026-10-17, RESUME 2026-10-18).

### 2026-10-18 · 2.close Image: CUDA 12 cuBLAS for faster-whisper, and a uv cache mount that was never used

- **Why:** 2.7's in-cluster GPU run on Sintel ended `completed_with_gaps` because `timeline.asr` could not load `libcublas.so.12` in the nip image, and 2.7 read an ORT log line as "no CUDA execution provider" for OCR (0.41 kf/s). The closeout brief asked for the CUDA 12 cuBLAS + cuDNN 9 wheels the docs name. All GPU work ran under `data/gpu.lock` (taken 14:36). Machine: RTX 4060 Laptop 8 GB, driver 610.62. Numbers come from throwaway bench scripts run with `docker exec` inside ray-head (not committed), `nip run --in-cluster`, the EXP-001 trial command and pytest.
- **Reproduced:** in the existing `nip:dev` image (Docker image ID `afd27c67cc71`) inside ray-head, `make_asr("faster-whisper-large-v3-turbo-int8").transcribe(sintel.wav)` (16 kHz mono, 888.0 s) raised `RuntimeError: Library libcublas.so.12 is not found or cannot be loaded`. The image is CUDA 13 (`nvidia-cublas 13.1.1.3`, only `libcublas.so.13`); the CTranslate2 4.8.3 Linux wheel dlopens `libcublas.so.12` (`strings` on `libctranslate2-bdb19cbc.so.4.8.3` shows it, and no `libcudnn`).
- **Tried first (scratch, no rebuild):** `pip install --target /tmp/cu12 nvidia-cublas-cu12` (12.9.2.10, wheel 581,240,110 B, 817 MB unpacked: `libcublasLt.so.12` 749 MB, `libcublas.so.12` 105 MB) with `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`: ASR ran, turbo int8 load 17.8 s, transcribe 25.8 s for 888 s = **RTF 0.029**, 60 segments / 279 words, +1,330 MiB VRAM. So **no cuDNN wheel is needed on Linux**: the Windows wheel bundles `cudnn64_9.dll`, the Linux one does not use cuDNN; `nvidia-cudnn-cu12` would also have installed into `nvidia/cudnn/lib`, which the image's `nvidia-cudnn-cu13` (9.20) owns, overwriting it.
- **Shipped** ([B-304](decisions.md#b-304--cuda-12-cublas-only-on-ldconfig-and-the-uv-cache-dir-fixed)): `"nvidia-cublas-cu12; sys_platform == 'linux'"` in the `serve` extra (commit `d5ef357`; `uv lock` adds `nvidia-cublas-cu12 12.9.2.10` and its dependency `nvidia-cuda-nvrtc-cu12 12.9.86`; the Windows venv is untouched) plus, in the Dockerfile, an `/etc/ld.so.conf.d/nip-cublas12.conf` entry and `ldconfig` (survives whatever environment Ray or compose passes to workers); `ldconfig -p` lists `libcublas.so.12` in the new image.
- **A build bug found on the way** ([C-50](experiments.md#c-50--nip-image-size-the-wheel-then-the-uv-cache-mount-that-was-never-used)): the first rebuild (wheel + ldconfig only; `docker compose build ray-head` 6 min 43 s, cold wheel download) gave an image of **28,651,740,906 B, +2.17 GB** over the old 26,484,426,619 B, although `du` of `dist-packages` grew only 1,033 MB (only `nvidia/`); the `uv pip install` layer went 4.84 → 7.01 GB. `find -newermt` showed every package twice: in `dist-packages` and in `/opt/uv/cache` inside the layer. The vLLM base image sets `UV_CACHE_DIR=/opt/uv/cache`, so the Dockerfile's `RUN --mount=type=cache,target=/root/.cache/uv` mounted a cache uv never used: every build stored all wheels in the layer and no build was ever cache-warm. **Fix:** `ENV UV_CACHE_DIR=/root/.cache/uv UV_LINK_MODE=copy` (copy mode because the mount is another filesystem). Second build: layer **3.47 GB**, image **25,113,912,896 B, −1.37 GB vs the old image** (CUDA 12 libs +1.03 GB, cache duplication −2.4 GB). `pip freeze` old vs new: exactly two packages added. Commit `7532221`. The old image is kept as `nip:pre-close` (26.5 GB); the intermediate 28.65 GB image was deleted.
- **ASR on the final image** ([C-51](experiments.md#c-51--faster-whisper-in-the-container-missing-libcublasso12-scratch-fix-shipped-image)), ray-head recreated with `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.dev.yaml up -d --wait ray-head`: turbo int8 RTF **0.0201** (cold) and **0.0188**, 60 segments / 279 words, +1,294 / 1,295 MiB; large-v3 int8 RTF 0.0273, 53 segments / 372 words, +2,382 MiB.
- **Source:** `notes/2.close.md` (Image), `reports/2.close-report.md`; commits `d5ef357`, `7532221`.

### 2026-10-18 · 2.close OCR in the container: the CUDA provider was already loading

- **Finding:** in the old image `onnxruntime-gpu 1.31.0` is the only ORT (the CPU `onnxruntime` override from `0440e1d` was in the build), `ort.get_available_providers()` lists `CUDAExecutionProvider`, `ldd libonnxruntime_providers_cuda.so` resolves `libcublas.so.13`, `libcudart.so.13` and the rest through `/usr/local/cuda/lib64` with nothing missing, and the RapidOCR detection session's `get_providers()` is `['CUDAExecutionProvider', 'CPUExecutionProvider']`. The line 2.7 read as the failure, `No registered plugin EP device found for 'CUDAExecutionProvider' with device_id=0`, is printed by ORT 1.31 on every start before it falls through to the classic provider registration, and the session still gets CUDA (`ort.get_ep_devices()` is the plugin-EP API and lists only CPU).
- **Evidence** ([C-52](experiments.md#c-52--rapidocr-in-the-container-cuda-provider-vs-cpu-and-the-041-kfs-that-was-not-reproduced)), all 398 Sintel keyframes through `RapidOcrBackend` (det on CUDA, rec on CPU, the shipped split), warm-up excluded: old image with CUDA 357.0 s = **1.11 kf/s** (+1,147 MiB); old image CPU only 717.4 s = 0.55 kf/s; new image with CUDA 325.6 s = **1.22 kf/s** (+1,141 MiB). Nothing in the image changed for OCR; the CPU fallback stays ([B-308](decisions.md#b-308--nothing-changed-in-the-image-for-ocr-the-cpu-fallback-stays)).
- **2.7's 0.41 kf/s** (967 s) was the whole `clearance.ocr` stage (GLiNER, S3 reads, crops, Postgres writes) under load. In this task the same stage in a full `nip run --in-cluster` took 448.5 s = **0.89 kf/s**, and the stage's own scan loop (OCR + `gliner-x-base`, no DB) 370 s = **1.08 kf/s** (68 candidates). 0.41 kf/s could not be reproduced in any configuration; the agent's likeliest explanation (not verified) is that run's GPU/CPU load (2.1B and 2.2B jobs were active that morning) plus stage overhead.
- **Source:** `notes/2.close.md` (OCR), `reports/2.close-report.md`.

### 2026-10-18 · 2.close Full in-cluster Sintel run and vLLM smoke on the new image

- **Full shipped path** (`nip run --show clr-sintel-s1-b --episodes S01E01 --agents clearance_scanner --in-cluster` inside ray-head, `NIP_OCR_MODELS=/models/rapidocr NIP_VRAM_MARGIN_GB=0.25`, no `NIP_PIN_NER`; the same show as 2.7's run): **14 min 12 s** wall (2.7: 22 min), exit 0, status **`completed`** (2.7: `completed_with_gaps`, from the missing `libcublas.so.12`), 436 units done, 0 failed; `timeline.asr` 36.5 s (62 transcript segments); `clearance.ocr` 448.5 s with the planner's `gliner-x-large`; llm group load 300.0 s (first start after the container was recreated, cold compile caches), up 7,447 MiB, unload 5.0 s, back to 484 MiB. 34 flags (5 must_review, 21 review_recommended, 8 suppressed).
- **vLLM smoke** ([C-55](experiments.md#c-55--vllm-smoke-on-the-new-image-vs-earlier-runs)): `python3 -m eval.exp.exp001_vllm_headroom trial --setting plan --pilot --note close-smoke` (the shipped probe → planner → serving path; a throwaway file MLflow store inside the container so nothing landed in the experiment tables), qwen3.5-4b-awq, 2 trials: both started, generated, tore down and returned to idle; load 112.4 / 92.4 s, **134.6 / 165.5 tok/s**, KV 63,380 / 65,967 tokens, unload 4.0 s. Before the change (Phase 1 image smoke): load 85.4 s, 188.6 tok/s, KV 69,416; EXP-001 `plan` 175 to 176 tok/s. The agent read the drop as machine noise (1.7 recorded ±17 to 41 tok/s; the desktop used 500 to 600 MiB at start vs 332 earlier) because the two added wheels cannot touch vLLM's `.13` libraries; one trial is below that band and it was not investigated further (review M3, deferred).
- **Operations:** the Postgres integration tests ran against a throwaway `pgvector` container on 55433 (the stack's postgres was not published on 55432); full suite **938 passed, 7 skipped**; `ruff check` / `ruff format --check`, `mypy src` (82 files), `lint-imports` (7 kept) clean. A repo-wide `ruff format` of `tests/` also rewrote the unrelated `tests/unit/agents/test_shot_change.py`, which was reverted. ray-head stopped at the end, VRAM back to 139 MiB (desktop), lock released.
- **Source:** `notes/2.close.md` (Full shipped path, vLLM smoke, Operations log), `reports/2.close-report.md`.

### 2026-10-18 · 2.close Catalog: `gliner-x-large` measured, and `NIP_PIN_NER` no longer needed

- **Why:** the planner's quality objective picked `gliner-x-large` on the laptop because the catalog said `vram_gb: 3.7` (an hf-mem estimate: weights 3.2 GiB + 0.5), and it had never been downloaded or run; runs pinned `NIP_PIN_NER=gliner-x-base` as a workaround, and 2.7's controller ruling had already said the NER variant must come from the planner/catalog, not an unconditional pin.
- **Measured** ([C-53](experiments.md#c-53--gliner-x-base-vs-gliner-x-large-size-latency-and-candidates-on-sintels-stored-ocr)): `knowledgator/gliner-x-large` downloaded (`model.safetensors` 3,458,475,864 B) into `models/hf`; same harness for both variants on the Windows host (torch imported first, `GlinerBackend(cuda=True)`, 12 short OCR-like lines in en/es/fr/de x 5 labels, 5 passes = 60 calls after a warm-up, NVML whole-GPU peak over idle): x-base 35.5 s load, 35.7 / 35.1 ms per call (mean / median), peak **1,347 MiB**; x-large 46.3 s load, 64.8 / 63.6 ms, peak **2,578 MiB** (run 2: 33.7 s, 58.1 / 56.8 ms, 2,577 MiB). The estimate was too high (3.7 vs 2.52 GiB): large fits the 8 GB laptop; the planner's choice was never a fit problem, only an unmeasured one.
- **Set in `config/catalog.yaml`** (commit `d2a8fa3`, comments cite date, harness and numbers): x-large 3.7 → **2.6 GiB**, x-base 2.3 → **1.4 GiB**, `pp-ocrv6-medium` `family: rapidocr` (the ONNX/RapidOCR build) and 1.0 → **1.6 GiB** (2.6 measured 1.53 GiB on the host, this task 1.11 GiB in the image, both with torch's CUDA context; the larger was kept). Planner goldens moved: laptop ocr fraction 0.13 → 0.2, ner 0.47 → 0.33; 48 GB / 8x80 GB similar; 48 GB throughput `text_llm` KV 8182 → 8694 MiB (+512 MiB = the actors now reserve 0.5 GiB less). `nip plan` in the container (margin 0.25) still picks `gliner-x-large`, now by measurement, and the group plan fits ([B-305](decisions.md#b-305--gliner-x-large-stays-the-planners-pick-on-the-8-gb-laptop-measured-no-pin)).
- **Is large OK as the default?** On the stored OCR lines of `clr-sintel-s1-b` (199 keyframes with text, 1,634 lines), the stage's `TextScanner` with base gave 78 candidates {url 27, brand_text 39, address 7, plate 2, phone 2, email 1} and with large 63 {27, 28, 3, 2, 2, 1}; every large candidate is also a base candidate (63 common, 0 only-large); the 15 only-base candidates are credit-roll names and titles ("ART DIRECTION", "DAVID REVOY", "Noord-Holland", ...), read as false positives by the agent. The shipped in-cluster run agrees: 34 flags vs 2.7's 43, the 9 lost being those names; phone 1 / url 11 / email 1 / plate 2 identical. Cost: about +1.2 GiB VRAM and about +25 ms per call. The review asked for a scored check (entry below).
- **Source:** `notes/2.close.md` (Catalog), `reports/2.close-report.md`; commit `d2a8fa3`.

### 2026-10-18 · 2.close `clr/occ-<hash>` occurrence refs resolve from the store

- **Problem:** 2.7's clearance flags claim a span `clr/occ-<hash>` (frames = the occurrence's range) that the OCR stage added only to the run's in-memory timeline; `load_timeline` did not know it, so 2.9B re-verifying a stored clearance flag would have had to re-run OCR.
- **Options:** (a) a new `EvidenceKind` (touches the frozen `contracts/flag.py`, the DB enum and every consumer of kinds); (b) keep kind `clearance_item` (as the flags already store it) and make the ref a first-class format in `refs.py`; (c) store occurrence rows. **Took (b)** ([B-306](decisions.md#b-306--occurrence-ref-clrocc-hash-with-kind-clearance_item-occurrences-are-derived-not-stored)): `refs.occurrence(key)` builds `clr/occ-{key}`, `refs.is_occurrence(ref)` recognises it (`parse` already returned kind `clearance_item`, id `occ-<hash>`, which cannot collide with a numeric `clr/{id}`); `Occurrence.ref` goes through `refs.occurrence`. No new table: an occurrence is a pure function of the stored `detection` rows, the registry and the shots. When an episode has `ocr` detection rows, `load_timeline` calls `checks.with_stored_ocr`, which puts the OCR text on the keyframe spans (so a stored flag's keyframe `quote` resolves) and adds one `clr/occ-` span per occurrence. The helpers live in `checks.py`, not `stage.py`, because importing `stage` pulls Presidio/spaCy (**9.3 s** on first import, measured) against 0.8 s for `checks`; `stage.with_ocr` shares them and drops occurrence spans already in the timeline it is given.
- **Tests:** unit `test_occurrence_refs` and "`with_ocr` replaces earlier spans"; Postgres integration `test_a_stored_flag_reverifies_from_the_store_without_reading_the_keyframes` (run, forbid the OCR reader, `load_timeline`: claims equal the stored claim refs and frames, the six checks reproduce the 4 flags and `verify` keeps all 4; an episode without detections has no occurrence spans).
- **Real data:** on the in-cluster Sintel run, `load_timeline` on the stored show gave 668 spans incl. 34 occurrence spans (0.07 s after imports), and `verify` of the run's 34 stored flags with the six checks' `recheck`: **34 kept, 0 dropped**, with no OCR read.
- **Source:** `notes/2.close.md` (refs), `reports/2.close-report.md`; commit `5f65199`.

### 2026-10-18 · 2.close Review and fix round: a registry-independent occurrence hash, and the NER A/B on replayed OCR

- **Review** (`2.close-review.md`): spec PASS, quality GOOD, **0 Critical, 2 Important, 7 Minor** ([review-findings.md](review-findings.md#2close-phase-2-closeout)). The reviewer judged the cuBLAS-12-beside-13 setup correct and minimal, the uv cache root cause correct, the catalog numbers traceable, and the edits outside the brief's file list justified.
- **I1, occurrence refs moved with the registry.** `Occurrence.ref` hashed `who`, which is `clr/{item.id}` once an occurrence matches a registry item; logging a previously unknown item (the normal clearance workflow) changed the rebuilt ref, the stored claim stopped resolving and `verify` dropped the flag. **Controller ruling:** hash registry-independent fields only ([B-307](decisions.md#b-307--the-occurrence-hash-uses-registry-independent-fields-only)). **Fix** (commit `ddf6206`): the hash is episode, check, the normalized text of the first read (`matching.normalize`) and the first keyframe `(shot, n)`; the matched item stays separate `context` evidence. Known limit (in the docstring): logging an item that merges two differently spelled reads into one occurrence can move the first keyframe and so the ref. Tests: unit `test_the_ref_does_not_change_when_the_registry_logs_the_item` (pending, denied, cleared); Postgres `test_a_stored_claim_still_resolves_after_the_item_is_logged`. The rule re-check (`recheck`, "same logged item") still fails for the one flag whose item was logged, so `verify` drops it: its finding ("is not in the clearance log") is stale. The agent did not relax `ClearanceText.recheck`; 2.9B-1 later decided how a status change appears in the run diff ([B-124](decisions.md#b-124--a-clearance-status-change-shows-in-the-run-diff-as-resolved--new)). Agents + PG subset: 343 passed.
- **I2, the shipped default was unscored.** The published 2.7 EXP-008 numbers were produced with `gliner-x-base` (`exp008_e2e run-cpu` pins base in code), and the only evidence for large was one film by manual reading. **Controller ruling:** keep `gliner-x-large` as the default only if EXP-008's score (3 seeds, both films) shows recall at least equal to base; otherwise demote it ([B-305](decisions.md#b-305--gliner-x-large-stays-the-planners-pick-on-the-8-gb-laptop-measured-no-pin)). **Done** (commits `c0988ee`, `cf6724e`): the NER A/B on replayed OCR, entry below.
- **M1** (licence of the new wheels) recorded: `nvidia-cublas-cu12` and `nvidia-cuda-nvrtc-cu12` are NVIDIA CUDA proprietary redistributables, the same class as the CUDA libraries the base image already ships; the docs' License Policy governs catalog model variants, not pip wheels; accepted ([B-304](decisions.md#b-304--cuda-12-cublas-only-on-ldconfig-and-the-uv-cache-dir-fixed)). **M5:** stale `NIP_PIN_NER` references removed from the `pipeline.py` `current_plan` docstring and `exp008_e2e.py run-cluster`. M2, M3, M4, M6, M7 not acted on.
- **Re-review** (`2.close-rereview.md`): I1, I2, M1, M5 ADDRESSED, 0 Critical, 0 Important. The reviewer ran `pytest tests/unit/agents tests/integration/test_clearance_report_pg.py` (339 passed, 4 skipped: the PG tests need `NIP_TEST_DATABASE_URL`, so the new PG test was not run by the reviewer) and read the MLflow DB directly (below).
- **Source:** `notes/2.close.md` (Fix round 1), `reports/2.close-report.md`, `2.close-review.md`, `2.close-rereview.md`, `progress.md` (2.close entries); commits `ddf6206`, `c0988ee`, `cf6724e`.

### 2026-10-18 · 2.close NER A/B on replayed OCR: `gliner-x-large` vs `gliner-x-base` on the EXP-008 end-to-end benchmark

- **Method** ([C-54](experiments.md#c-54--ner-ab-on-replayed-ocr-gliner-x-large-vs-gliner-x-base-on-the-exp-008-end-to-end-benchmark), extends [EXP-008](experiments.md#exp-008--keyframe-density-for-on-screen-text)): to isolate the NER choice from OCR noise, `eval/exp/exp008_e2e.py` gained `ocr-lines` (snapshots each show's stored OCR text rows to `data/exp008/e2e/ocr-lines/<show>.json`; 1,390 to 1,634 lines per show per the re-review) and `run-ner --ner <variant> [--cpu]` (runs the real `pipeline.run_store` with OCR replayed from the snapshot by a stub reader keyed by keyframe image hash, and only `Slot.NER` set to the variant). Scoring is the unchanged `score` (`nip eval run`, MLflow tag `exp=2.7-e2e`, keyframe policy `1-per-shot+2s`, tag b, 3 seeds, both films). GLiNER ran on the CPU because 2.1B's vLLM jobs held the GPU: base 55 to 82 s per show, large 360 to 973 s (contended CPU).
- **Control:** the base replay reproduces the published base result (Sintel s1 43 flags, 42 for the other shows; shown P/R 0.21 / 1.00), so the replay stands in for the published run.
- **Result** (`nip eval compare --exp 2.7-e2e`, 3 seeds, shown flags; full table `data/exp008/compare-e2e-ner.md`): overall P / R / F1 on injected labels base 0.21 / 1.00 / 0.34, large 0.21 / 1.00 / 0.35; with natives counted correct 0.44 → **0.46**; shown FP per content-hour **90.99 → 86.55**; recall 1.00 in every check (address, brand_text, email, phone, plate, url) and every seed; flags kept per show base 42 to 43, large 33 to 36. **18 finished MLflow runs** (re-review count). Decision: keep `gliner-x-large`, no demotion ([B-305](decisions.md#b-305--gliner-x-large-stays-the-planners-pick-on-the-8-gb-laptop-measured-no-pin)).
- **Caveat (the agent's own):** the benchmark's positives are phone/url/email/plate/address/brand injections that the regex recognizers find; NER-driven checks (names, organizations) are barely exercised, so this bounds "no recall loss" and does not show large is better. **Statement for the EXP-008 write-up:** the published 2.7 numbers (e2e-cpu, EXP-008 tables, EXP-017) were produced with `gliner-x-base`; the shipped laptop default is `gliner-x-large`, scored as above.
- **GPU collision (from the 2.1B notes, not the 2.close notes):** while 2.1B held `data/gpu.lock` on 2026-10-18 (16:15 to 17:00), 166 of its 382 free-text extraction calls failed with `EngineCore … CUDA error: unknown error`; the `exp008_e2e run-ner --cpu --ner gliner-x-large` processes (started 16:29 and 16:39) were on the device, which stayed at 7,751 MiB after 2.1B's teardown and 7,738 MiB with ray-head stopped. 2.1B re-ran the 166 calls after the device returned to idle (0 failed, ending 18:02). The 2.close notes say GLiNER ran on the CPU and needed no lock; the 2.1B notes say these `--cpu` processes held the VRAM (2.1B attributes them to "2.7's" command; the ledger to the closeout agent). Why `--cpu` processes held GPU memory was not investigated. The controller recorded a lock-discipline reminder for GPU work outside the lock.
- **Source:** `notes/2.close.md` (I2), `reports/2.close-rereview.md` (MLflow check), `notes/2.1B.md` (free-text attempts), `reports/2.1B-report.md` (GPU collision), `progress.md` (2.close fix round, process incident); commits `c0988ee`, `cf6724e`.

### 2026-10-18 · 2.1B Fix round 1: one printed line per script_line, speakers present where they speak, and plot rules re-measured on real facts

- **Controller instruction with the fix round:** fix I1 to I3 and re-measure the plot rules on real facts before and after ([B-184](decisions.md#b-184--fix-round-1-must-re-measure-the-plot-rules-on-real-facts-later-rounds-re-score-without-the-gpu)). Commits `22074b9` and `8a5d5bd` (2026-10-17) and `11d03c4` (2026-10-18).
- **I1, render:** one printed line per `script_line`, whitespace collapsed; every citable unit keeps its own ref and grounding (which collapses whitespace too) accepts a quote from either paragraph. Line refs unchanged; 2.0's ingest not touched. Test `test_a_multi_paragraph_line_prints_as_one_numbered_line` ([B-169](decisions.md#b-169--the-prompt-prints-one-numbered-line-per-script_line)).
- **I1, derived speaker presence (D-2.1B-1):** `save_speakers` adds a `present_at` event for each resolved dialogue speaker at the location the heading names (`scene_location` matches the `INT./EXT.` segment to a `location` entity), on the speaker's first line, with the whole line as quote and confidence 0.9 (`SPEAKER_PRESENCE_CONFIDENCE`), unless the model already gave that event ([B-170](decisions.md#b-170--resolved-dialogue-speakers-are-present-at-the-headings-location-d-21b-1)).
- **I2:** `Resolver.provisional` holds every candidate id and `typed` checks only confirmed kinds; test `test_a_candidates_guessed_kind_does_not_drop_later_events` ([B-171](decisions.md#b-171--a-candidate-entitys-guessed-kind-is-provisional)). **M1:** `seen` gets kept events only, so a confidence-0 copy no longer shadows the good event. **M2 / M3:** `exp018_kv.gpu_session(purpose, arm)` does all GPU housekeeping (acquire; wait while the desktop holds the VRAM, lock released meanwhile; deploy; body; delete every Serve app and release in nested `finally`s; log a release with the device above 1,500 MiB), used by the EXP-018 driver and `exp020_rules extract --deploy ARM`, so no extraction pass can run without the lock ([B-174](decisions.md#b-174--all-gpu-work-in-the-exp-harnesses-goes-through-gpu_session-which-releases-only-a-lock-it-owns)). **M5:** EXP-006 drop counts above the threshold only (argument type 82, zero confidence 17, duplicate 0). **M6:** harness uses `EVENTS_PER_LINE`. **M7:** subject-less events are labelled `no subject`.
- **Found while re-scoring, holds values:** after the I1 fix the free-text arm extracts the planted prop uses ("Dov checks the signal flare again." → `holds dov → signal flare`) but with the value `check`, not the prompt's `use`. Of 417 kept free-text holds on the injected shows: `check` 186, `share` 171, `use` 24, `obtain` 20, `lose` 13, `take` 3; `plot.prop_provenance` reads `value.event = 'use'`, so it saw none of them. `11d03c4` mapped any other verb to `use` (verb kept as `detail`) and take/pick up/grab/get/find to `obtain` (D-2.1B-2, first version; superseded in fix round 2, [B-172](decisions.md#b-172--holdsowns-values-are-mapped-only-from-the-prompts-vocabulary-other-verbs-are-dropped-and-counted-d-21b-2)).
- **GPU runs, all via `gpu_session`:** enum seed-1 clean (recorded) 150 calls, 0 failed; enum seeds 1–3 336 calls, 0 failed; free text seeds 1–3 486 calls in three attempts: (1) 2026-10-17 12:18, 382/486 failed with `Connection error` (the build was paused and ray-head stopped mid-pass); (2) 2026-10-18 16:15–17:00, 166/382 failed with `EngineCore … CUDA error: unknown error` at 16:57 while other `exp008_e2e run-ner --cpu --ner gliner-x-large` processes (started 16:29 and 16:39, during this lock) were on the device; after teardown the device stayed at 7,751 MiB with no Serve app and no vLLM process, and at 7,738 MiB with ray-head stopped; `gpu_session` logged "lock released with 7751 MiB used"; the agent did not touch those processes; (3) 2026-10-18, ending 18:02, the remaining 166 calls, 0 failed. Failed rows were removed from the cache before each retry.
- **Images:** the enum passes ran on `nip:dev` afd27c67 and the free-text replies on afd27c67 (104, attempt 1) and dd67ae7b (382, attempts 2–3); both report vLLM 0.30.0 and xgrammar 0.2.8. The reviewer diffed the build inputs: only the `nvidia-cublas-cu12` and `nvidia-cuda-nvrtc-cu12` wheels differ (`.so.12` sonames that torch cu13 and vLLM do not load).
- **Postgres lost its loopback port** during the pause (it had been restarted from `compose.yaml` alone); postgres, objectstore and ray-head were recreated with `compose.dev.yaml` once the lock was free.
- **Before → after on real facts** (3 seeds, 36 planted; full table in [EXP-020](experiments.md#exp-020--entity-resolution-enum-constrained-ids-vs-fuzzy-matching-afterwards)): enum P/R 0.02 / 0.03 → **0.20 / 0.28** (found 1 → 10; two_places 0 → 9/9, entirely from derived presence, [C-24](experiments.md#c-24--derived-speaker-presence-with-without-and-skipping-intercut-scenes)); free text 0 / 0 → 0.08 / 0.25, and 0.06 / 0.47 with the holds vocabulary (prop_provenance 0 → 8/9) at 92.3 false positives per injected show ([C-25](experiments.md#c-25--holdsowns-values-as-written-vs-any-verb--use-vs-prompt-verbs-only)); knowledge_order still 0/9 everywhere. The I1 render fix alone cut quote drops (means over 3 seeds, from the re-review) from 28.7 to 3.7 (free text) and 57.3 to 4.3 (enum) ([C-26](experiments.md#c-26--one-printed-line-per-script_line-quote-drops-before-and-after)).
- **Tests:** cpu **855 passed**, 2 skipped (ray); integration **22 passed**; ruff and mypy (82 files) clean.
- **Re-review** (`2.1B-rereview.md`): I1, I2, I3, M2, M3 ADDRESSED; all 24 `EXP-020-rules` run ids active and the per-variant means recompute exactly; `pytest tests/unit/knowledge` 81 passed. **Three new Important:** N1 `gpu_session` could tear down and release when it did not hold the lock (an exception during the wait loop, where the lock is released and re-acquired, reached the `finally`, which deletes every Serve app on the shared ray-head and `rmtree`s the lock); N2 the catch-all "any other verb → use" (probe: `remove` → use, which before closed a holding and now opens one; `receives`, `buys`, `steals`, `pockets` → use; `gives`, `loses`, `drops`, `sells` → use; free-text F1 0.122 → 0.103); N3 derived presence would place V.O. and phone speakers at the heading's location, because `speaker_name` strips the cue extension at ingest. Minors m1 to m5 (MLflow commit tags, image disclosure, EXP-018 wording, `HOLDS_EVENTS` restated, ownerless-lock window in `acquire`).
- **Source:** `notes/2.1B.md` (Fix round 1), `reports/2.1B-report.md` (Fix round 1), `reports/2.1B-rereview.md`; ledger `progress.md`.

### 2026-10-18 · 2.1B Fix rounds 2 and 3: the lock belongs to its owner, only the prompt's verbs, and cue extensions survive ingest

- **Fix round 2** (`962512f`, no GPU: every number re-scored on the fix-round-1 replies, a copy in `data/exp020/rules/default-8k-r2/`, as the controller asked): **N1** `owner.txt` ends with this process's token (`pid N`); `release()` removes the lock only when `holds_lock()` finds that token, and the `finally` tears down and releases only when this process holds the lock; tests in `tests/unit/eval/test_exp018_lock.py`. **N2 (controller ruling):** `HOLDS_VERBS` maps only the prompt's verbs (obtain / pick up → obtain; use / carry / check → use; give, lose, lost, drop, sell, remove → themselves, with inflections); any other verb is dropped as `unknown holds value` and counted, never guessed; on the injected shows over 3 seeds that dropped 174 free-text events (171 `share`, 3 `take`) and 136 enum events (131 `share`, 3 `take`, 2 `slip(s)`) ([B-172](decisions.md#b-172--holdsowns-values-are-mapped-only-from-the-prompts-vocabulary-other-verbs-are-dropped-and-counted-d-21b-2)). **N3:** no derived presence when the speaker, or the speaker's preceding parenthetical, matches `OFFSCREEN` (V.O., O.S., O.C., filtered, pre-lap, phone, radio, intercom, voice-over).
- **Tried and reverted: skipping scenes marked `intercut`.** In this project `intercut` also marks SAME TIME / MEANWHILE scenes (`story.py` `_INTERCUT`; 2.3's oracle maps the generator's `same_time` to it), and evaluation.md defines `plot.two_places` on exactly those scenes. With the skip, two_places fell from 9/9 to **0/9** in both arms (first r2 re-score; those MLflow runs were deleted and re-logged with the shipped code) ([C-24](experiments.md#c-24--derived-speaker-presence-with-without-and-skipping-intercut-scenes)).
- **Re-score with the principled mapping:** free text P/R/F1 0.08 / 0.47 / 0.14 (was 0.06 / 0.47 / 0.10 with any verb → use), prop_provenance recall stays 0.89 (the planted uses are "checks the … again", a use by the prompt's definition), false positives per injected show 92.3 → 63.3 (prop_provenance 58.3 → 29.3), fact precision 0.543 → 0.602 at recall 0.887; enum unchanged (its 131 `share` holds were already dropped as mistyped). Tests: cpu **857 passed**, 2 skipped; integration **23 passed**.
- **Re-review 2** (`2.1B-rereview2.md`): N1 and N2 ADDRESSED; **N3 PARTIAL**: the common form is the cue extension (`MAYA (V.O.)`), which `script.speaker_name` strips at ingest, so the `OFFSCREEN.search(line.speaker)` branch was dead code in practice and only a separate parenthetical was seen. The skip-intercut revert was judged correct (`docs/architecture.md`: INTERCUT / SAME TIME scenes share a `story_seq`; `docs/evaluation.md`: two_places plants a character in two simultaneous scenes). All 12 re-score run ids active, means recompute exactly.
- **Fix round 3** (`dd557fe`, controller ruling: a minimal ingest change, no schema migration, [B-173](decisions.md#b-173--script_linespeaker-stores-the-character-cue-as-written-d-21b-3)): the readers pass the whole cue to `build_script` (Fountain rebuilds `NAME (EXT)` from screenplay-tools' `Character.name` / `.extension`; FDX and PDF pass the cue text); `script.Element` gains `extension`, `speaker` stays the clean name; `speaker_name` now strips every trailing parenthetical (`MAYA (V.O.) (CONT'D)` → `MAYA`; before only the last); `script_line.speaker` = the cue as written; `save_speakers` resolves `speaker_name(line.speaker)` and tests the cue with `OFFSCREEN`; `(CONT'D)` is not off-screen. **m5:** `owner.txt` is written into a private `gpu.lock.<pid>` that is then renamed onto `gpu.lock`, so the lock never exists without its owner. No re-score (the synthetic shows carry no cue extensions). Tests: `tests/unit/ingest/test_cue_extension.py` (Fountain and FDX `(V.O.)`, PDF `(O.S.)` and `(CONT'D)`), integration adds Ada (V.O.) and Tomas (O.S.) without presence and Rui (CONT'D) with it; cpu **860 passed**, 2 skipped; integration (knowledge, store, layer1, layer1_oracle, index_pg) **70 passed**, 1 skipped.
- **Rule breach, self-reported:** while checking an unrelated order-dependent torchcodec `TypeError` in `tests/unit/timeline` (appears only with `ingest+knowledge+timeline` run together; the full suite passes), the agent ran `git stash`, which the workspace rules forbid. It held only the agent's four uncommitted files and was popped about a minute later; the stash list is empty.
- **Re-review 3** (`2.1B-rereview3.md`, scoped): N3 ADDRESSED with a consumer audit of every reader of `script_line.speaker` (the dead_acts SQL reads `speaker_entity_id` only; the 2.8 index text now embeds `MAYA (V.O.): text`). One new Minor, M-new1: `render` printed the raw cue (`[3] ADA (V.O.): ...`) into the prompt, so a speaker not in the registry could become a candidate named with the extension. That was fixed later by 2.9B-1 (the extraction prompt renders `speaker_name(...)`, commit `e2bab95`). 2.1B complete.
- **Source:** `notes/2.1B.md` (Fix rounds 2 and 3), `reports/2.1B-report.md`, `2.1B-rereview2.md`, `2.1B-rereview3.md`; ledger `progress.md`.

### 2026-10-18 · Process: the GPU lock around 2.1 and 2.1B

- **The mutex** (`briefs/phase2-plan.md` › GPU contention): `mkdir data/gpu.lock` (atomic) + `owner.txt`, hold only for model runs, tear down and confirm VRAM back to idle before releasing.
- **Four incidents:**
  1. **2.1, 2026-10-16:** held 10:19 to 15:30 EST, about 1 h 35 m of model work and the rest idle while the agent's session was paused by the API rate limit with vLLM up; 2.5 and 2.6 waited (2.1 review "Items for the controller").
  2. **2.1B, 2026-10-17:** the EXP-018 driver's teardown `finally` crashed on a cp1252 decode of vLLM's UTF-8 output, so the lock stayed held 03:54 to 06:11 with no model loaded; fixed in `dc4e6fc`.
  3. **User stop, 2026-10-17:** ray-head was holding vLLM (about 7.9 GB VRAM) with 2.1B mid fix round; the controller stopped it and released the stale lock.
  4. **2026-10-18, GPU work outside the lock:** during 2.1B's lock, `exp008_e2e run-ner --cpu --ner gliner-x-large` processes (the closeout's EXP-008 NER A/B, per the ledger) held about 7.7 GB on the device; the vLLM EngineCore died with `CUDA error: unknown error` and 166 extraction calls failed; they were re-run with 0 failures once the device was idle. Ledger: "Lock discipline reminder needed for GPU work outside the lock."
- **Also:** the Windows desktop alone held 2,838 to 3,069 MiB at 03:51 on 2026-10-17 with no model loaded, which made the planner skip text_llm; the driver now waits with the lock released.
- **What changed in code** ([B-174](decisions.md#b-174--all-gpu-work-in-the-exp-harnesses-goes-through-gpu_session-which-releases-only-a-lock-it-owns)): UTF-8 decoding and an unconditional release (`dc4e6fc`); a desktop-VRAM wait with the lock released (`bb68eac`); `gpu_session` for every extraction pass (`22074b9`); an ownership token so a process never releases or tears down another holder's lock (`962512f`); an atomic rename so the lock never exists without its owner (`dd557fe`).
- **Other process items:** 2.1 ran `ruff format` over a directory shared with 2.2 and 2.8 (formatting only, self-reported); 2.1B ran `git stash` once (disclosed, restored within a minute).
- **Source:** `notes/2.1.md`, `notes/2.1B.md`, `reports/2.1-review.md`, `reports/2.1B-report.md`; ledger `progress.md` lines 118–261.

### 2026-11-06 · 2.9B-1 Stage cache, incremental re-runs and `GET /runs/{id}/diff`

- **What:** a content-addressed stage cache, scene-by-scene script re-ingest, and two run endpoints. Commits `e2bab95` (stage cache), `4eff744` (incremental re-ingest), `cca0f1c` (API) on `phase-2`. Built on the uncommitted work of five earlier implementer runs that had been stopped; nothing of theirs was discarded.
- **Cache key and storage** (`src/nip/runtime/cache.py`): key = sha256 of canonical JSON `{stage, stage_version, model_variant, prompt_version, params, sorted input hashes}`. The output is gzip JSON at `nip-artifacts/cache/{key[0:2]}/{key}.json.gz` plus a `stage_cache` row ([B-122](decisions.md#b-122--cache-objects-are-gzip-json-not-zstd)). Each put is its own short transaction, so a crash later in the run keeps the units already paid for; a hit bumps `hits`; `nip run --force` makes every lookup miss.
- **Work-unit protocol** (`pipeline.py`): `with run.unit(...) as u:` then `u.lookup(key)` returns the cached output (the unit is recorded `cached`) or None, in which case the body sets `u.value` and it is stored when the unit succeeds. A failed cache write never fails a unit.
- **What is cached:** knowledge extraction per scene (key = the rendered prompt + schema: the scene's lines, the speaker registry, the bible entries of the characters present), OCR per keyframe (image sha256 + OCR/NER variants + scanner config + clearance rule files), and every check unit (`pipeline.check_key`: check version, `params()`, the unit key, span digests, and `Check.inputs`: the show-wide facts a SQL rule reads, the observed standards for `loc.term`, the occurrence for clearance text checks). Not keyed: consolidate, index, cue_align, `loc.observed` (cheap CPU), asr, and `knowledge.story`.
- **Re-ingest:** a new script draft for an episode is now accepted. `_write_script` keeps every scene whose number, heading and lines are unchanged (row id, events, chunk, story position, so their cache keys stay valid), rewrites an edited scene in place, and deletes scenes no longer present (`scene_chunk` before `scene`). Inserting or moving a scene replaces every scene; that ceiling is a `ponytail:` comment in `_write_script`.
- **API:** `GET /api/v1/runs/{run_id}` and `GET /api/v1/runs/{run_id}/diff?against=previous` (any authenticated role, [B-126](decisions.md#b-126--reading-a-run-is-open-to-every-authenticated-role)). The diff classes flags as new / resolved / unchanged by fingerprint ([B-124](decisions.md#b-124--a-clearance-status-change-shows-in-the-run-diff-as-resolved--new)) and lists `changed_inputs` ([B-125](decisions.md#b-125--changed_inputs-labels-a-source-by-version-label-else-src_id)).
- **Broke, then fixed: rule units replayed another episode's findings.** The first integration run reported 4 "cached" units on a cold run, all `S01E02/plot.*`. A SQL rule unit has no spans and reads show-wide facts, so S01E02's key equalled S01E01's and S01E02 replayed S01E01's findings, with S01E01's episode-scoped evidence refs. Fix: the unit key is an input of every check key ([B-123](decisions.md#b-123--every-check-units-cache-key-includes-the-unit-key)); extraction hits are re-stamped with the current scene's ref, because an identical scene elsewhere has the same key. Tests: `test_check_key_follows_what_the_check_reads` (3 distinct keys across episodes) and `test_rerun.py` asserting 0 cached units on run 1.
- **Measured** (host, `NIP_PROFILE=cpu` stub Plan, synthetic show seed 1, S01E01 + S01E02 + en/es subtitles; details in [C-020](experiments.md#c-020--stage-cache-cold-run-vs-a-one-line-scene-edit)): cold `run_51` 74 units, 0 cached (66 keyed); after editing one dialogue line in S01E01 scene 3, `run_52` **61 of 66 keyed units cached, hit rate 0.9242**, 13 units re-run. `GET /runs/run_52/diff` returned `changed_inputs: ["S01E01 script src_68 → src_74 (scenes 3 changed)"]`, 0 new, 0 resolved, 7 unchanged flags; no token → 401, `run_99999` → 404.
- **Review notes applied:** `SqlRule.params()` carries `inferred_factor` (`NIP_INFERRED_STORY_CONFIDENCE_FACTOR`), so changing it misses the cache; the extraction prompt renders `speaker_name(...)` (no "(V.O.)").
- **Tests:** CPU suite 875 passed, 2 skipped (was 860 / 2); integration 86 passed, 3 skipped, 4 failed, all 4 in `test_compose_stack.py`, which needs the full compose stack (`api`, `mlflow` not running). New: `tests/unit/runtime/test_cache.py` (10), `tests/unit/api/test_runs.py` (5), `tests/integration/test_rerun.py` (end to end). Two `test_store.py` tests encoded the old behaviour ("second script rejected", "failed run writes no objects") and were updated: cache objects deliberately outlive a failed run.
- **Known gaps:** `knowledge.story` is not cached, so with a real LLM a re-run repeats story ordering in full; `SqlRule.reads` restates each rule's SQL predicates by hand (a rule that reads a new predicate must add it, or its cache goes stale); the demo used stubs, so it measures cache mechanics, not GPU time saved.
- **Source:** `.superpowers/sdd/phased-plan/notes/2.9B.md`, `reports/2.9B-1-report.md`; demo script `data/demo-2.9B/demo.sh` (git-ignored).

### 2026-11-06 · Environment: Postgres's dev port 55432 fell inside a Windows excluded port range

- **Symptom:** `docker start` of the postgres container failed with "An attempt was made to access a socket in a way forbidden by its access permissions".
- **Cause:** `netsh interface ipv4 show excludedportrange protocol=tcp` listed **55378–55477** as reserved (Windows dynamic exclusions, typically Hyper-V/WSL), and compose publishes Postgres on `127.0.0.1:55432`.
- **Fix used:** the same container image and `pg-data` volume, recreated with `--pull never` from a scratch override outside the repo (`ports: !override ["127.0.0.1:54432:5432"]`). Host commands and tests use `NIP_DATABASE_URL` / `NIP_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://nip:change_me@127.0.0.1:54432/nip`. `compose.dev.yaml` is unchanged; the discrepancy is a row in [doc-corrections.md](doc-corrections.md#non-doc-files-that-the-docs-describe).

### 2026-11-06 · First push of `phase-2`: Claude co-author trailers removed from 96 commit messages

- **Request:** push the work to GitHub "as me and not you". Every commit in `origin/main..phase-2` (97) was already authored by `prathamchopra001`, but 96 messages ended with a `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailer, which GitHub shows as a second author.
- **Done** ([B-127](decisions.md#b-127--phase-2s-commit-messages-were-rewritten-once-before-the-first-push-to-drop-claude-co-author-trailers)): backup branch `backup/phase-2-before-trailer-strip`; `git filter-branch --msg-filter` over `origin/main..phase-2` removing only the trailer lines. Checked afterwards: the final tree is byte-identical, 0 trailers remain, author and dates unchanged. Then `git push --set-upstream origin phase-2` (the first attempt, `git push --set-upstream origin` with no branch name, failed: a new branch has no upstream to infer).
- **Consequence:** every Phase 2 SHA changed, e.g. 2.9B-1's `228d05c` → `e2bab95`, `e9173ba` → `4eff744`, `aaced85` → `cca0f1c`. SHAs quoted in the SDD ledger before 2.9B-1 point to the backup branch. The Phase 1 SHAs in this log refer to the local, unpushed `phase-1` branch; on `origin/phase-2` the same commits carry other SHAs (Phase 1's closeout `09c87f8` is `45a525f` there).

### 2026-11-06 · CI on PR #1: three failures that only appeared on the GitHub runner

- **Context:** PR #1 (`phase-2` → `main`) ran `.github/workflows/ci.yml` on GitHub for the first time since Phase 1 (the workflow triggers on pull requests and pushes to `main`; nothing had been pushed before). Each failure stopped the job, so they surfaced one at a time.
- **1. mypy:** `eval/exp/exp020_rules.py:177: error: Need type annotation for "out" [var-annotated]`. The 2.9B-1 report had listed it as "1 error, pre-existing" and left it; CI does not distinguish pre-existing errors. Cause: `schema: type` is a bare `type`, so mypy cannot solve `M` in `LLMClient.complete[M: BaseModel](..., schema: type[M]) -> M`. Fix: `out: Any`, because the schema differs per experiment arm and the code only reads `out.events`. Commit `4216fb5`.
- **2. Five CLI tests** (`test_cli.py::test_root_help`, `test_eval_nests_corpus_and_labels`, `test_run_takes_show_and_episodes`, `test_phase2_sub_apps_are_wired`, `test_pipeline.py::test_cli_picture_must_be_a_file`): Typer's `rich_utils` sets `FORCE_TERMINAL` when `GITHUB_ACTIONS` (or `FORCE_COLOR` / `PY_COLORS`) is set, so the captured help text is Rich-styled with ANSI codes and substrings such as `--show` are split. Reproduced locally with `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true` (4 of the 5 failed; the fifth passes locally either way). Fix: `tests/conftest.py` sets `_TYPER_FORCE_DISABLE_TERMINAL=1` before any test imports Typer ([B-128](decisions.md#b-128--tests-force-typers-plain-non-rich-terminal-output)).
- **3. `test_inject_cli.py::test_real_sintel_labels_describe_the_written_tracks`:** it skips unless `data/corpus/sintel/...` and `data/exp002/.../picture.json` exist, and with `NIP_CI=1` the conftest turns every skip into a failure (the Phase 1 "green, never ran" rule). The runner has no `data/`. Fix: marked `integration`, so the CPU step deselects it; the CI integration step lists explicit files and does not include it; it still runs locally with `-m integration` ([B-129](decisions.md#b-129--tests-that-need-local-data-files-are-marked-integration-ci-is-replayed-locally-before-a-push)). Commit `36e9305` holds fixes 2 and 3.
- **Local CI replay** (`GITHUB_ACTIONS=true NIP_CI=1 NIP_PROFILE=cpu`, Postgres on 54432): ruff clean; mypy no issues in 126 source files; lint-imports 7 contracts kept; `pytest -m "not gpu and not integration"` **874 passed, 2 skipped, 95 deselected** (the 2 skips are module-level `importorskip("ray")` at collection, which the NIP_CI hook does not fail; the runner reported the same 2); the CI integration file set **81 passed, 1 deselected**.
- **Lesson:** local gates were green while CI was red because they ran without the runner's environment. Replaying with `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true NIP_CI=1` reproduces both environment-dependent failures, and a "pre-existing" gate error is a failing gate.
